**States.** Active marks the cell that takes the next character: Bits UI writes `[data-active]` on it and `[data-inactive]` on the others. Nova styles the active cell with `data-[active=true]:`, which matches no cell, so the active cell shows only the caret. Give each `InputOTP.Slot` `class="data-active:z-10 data-active:border-ring data-active:ring-3 data-active:ring-ring/50"`, which draws the focus ring of `## Shapes`. Invalid: a cell with `[aria-invalid="true"]` draws a `destructive` edge, also in dark. A cell is hidden, so its `aria-invalid` draws only the edge, and the root carries the state: give the root `aria-invalid={invalid || undefined}`, which reaches the hidden input. Disabled: the hidden input is `:disabled`, and the root draws at 50% opacity.

**Tokens.** `input-otp-slot`, `input-otp-slot-invalid`, `input-otp-slot-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The root gives its other props, such as an `aria-label`, to the hidden input, which a screen reader reads in place of the cells. The cells show a copy of the value as text, and Bits UI and nova put no `aria-hidden` on them. So the accessibility tree holds each digit and the separator beside the value of the input, and a screen reader reads the code twice. Give each `InputOTP.Group` and each `InputOTP.Separator` `aria-hidden="true"`. Then the tree holds only the input, with its name and its value (measured in the accessibility tree of Chromium 148). A prop on the root reaches the input and not the box of the cells, so it cannot hide them. To tie a `label` to the input, give the root `inputId` and point the `for` of the label at it: an `id` on the root lands on the outer box, not on the input (Bits UI 2.19.5). Bits UI sets `autocomplete="one-time-code"` on it, so that a phone offers the code from a message. A code that the server refuses, such as a wrong or an expired code on a sign-in page, shows as the `field-error` of the `field` around the input. `aria-invalid="true"` on the root reaches the hidden input, which Chromium then reads as invalid, and on each cell it draws the edge, until the person changes the code (measured in Chromium 148, see `### Page outside the shell`).

**Example.**

```svelte
<InputOTP.Root
  maxlength={8}
  pattern="^[0-9A-TV-Za-tv-z]+$"
  inputmode="text"
  autocapitalize="characters"
  pasteTransformer={(text) => text.replace(/[\s-]/g, '')} // For a sign-in code, CodeInput of @stack-kit/stack/outside/CodeInput writes this line and the binding.
  aria-label="Code from another device"
  bind:value={() => code, (v) => (code = v.toUpperCase().replace(/O/g, '0').replace(/[IL]/g, '1'))}
>
  {#snippet children({ cells })}
    <InputOTP.Group aria-hidden="true">
      {#each cells.slice(0, 4) as cell, i (i)}
        <InputOTP.Slot {cell} class="data-active:z-10 data-active:border-ring data-active:ring-3 data-active:ring-ring/50" />
      {/each}
    </InputOTP.Group>
    <InputOTP.Separator aria-hidden="true" />
    <InputOTP.Group aria-hidden="true">
      {#each cells.slice(4) as cell, i (i)}
        <InputOTP.Slot {cell} class="data-active:z-10 data-active:border-ring data-active:ring-3 data-active:ring-ring/50" />
      {/each}
    </InputOTP.Group>
  {/snippet}
</InputOTP.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px each cell is 44 px high and keeps its 32 px width (`input-otp-slot-phone`), and its character renders 15 px. Cells of 44 by 44 px do not fit 8 characters. Their row was 368 px, 10 px wider than the 358 px column of a 390 px screen. At 375 px and below, the page then grew wider than the screen (measured). Bits UI lays the hidden input over the whole root (`inset: 0`), so the touch target is the root, not a cell. With cells of 32 px the row is 272 by 44 px, and it fits the 288 px column of a 320 px screen. That is also the width of a 1280 px screen at 400% zoom. At 390, 375, 360 and 320 px the page stayed as wide as the screen (measured in Chromium 148).

### textarea

**Purpose.** A text area takes text of more than one line, such as a note or a message. It sits in a `field` with a visible label, as an input does.

**Anatomy.** One `textarea` slot, a native `<textarea>`, with the ground, the edge and the radius of `input`. It grows with its content (`field-sizing-content`) from a height of 64 px.

**Variants.** None. Add `max-h-80`, so that the text area grows to 320 px and then scrolls. A text area with actions, such as a message box, goes into an `input-group`.

**Sizes.** At least 64 px high (`min-h-16`), with 10 px side padding and 8 px top and bottom padding. The text is the `input` step.

**States.** Focus-visible, disabled, invalid and read-only look as on `input`, with the same selectors: `:focus-visible`, `:disabled`, `[aria-invalid="true"]` and `[readonly]`.

**Tokens.** `textarea`, `textarea-invalid`.

**Accessibility.** As on `input`. When the text has a limit, the `field-description` shows the count of characters that remain.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Textarea id="note" class="max-h-80" placeholder="Add a note" bind:value={note} />
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the text renders 16 px. The text area is at least 64 px high, which is above the 44 px target, so it has no phone key.

### code-editor

**Purpose.** A code editor takes a text that a machine reads, such as an instruction, a configuration or a script. It numbers the lines, and Tab indents. It sits in a `field` with a visible label, as a text area does. A text that a person reads, such as a note or a message, goes into a `textarea`. The code editor is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/CodeEditor`, and gives the value and the name of the text. STACK builds the part on CodeMirror 6 (see `STACK.md`). Its root (`code-editor-wrapper`) holds the box, and it takes the class, the other attributes and `ref` of the product. The class places the part, and a `max-h-*` class also sets the height where the box stops.

**Anatomy.** The `code-editor` slot is the `.cm-editor` element of CodeMirror, and it draws the box: a 1 px `input` edge, the 8 px radius and the `background` ground, in both modes. The box takes `dir="ltr"` through `EditorView.editorAttributes`, because code reads from left to right in every language: inside a block in Arabic, an editor with no `dir` turns its text to right to left (measured). The ground is an exception to the form controls, which take no ground in light and `input/30` in dark (see `### input`). The gutter must be opaque to cover a line that scrolls sideways under it. It takes the ground of the box, so the box takes an opaque ground too. The box holds the gutter (`.cm-gutters`) on the left and the text (`.cm-content`), a `contenteditable` element with the textbox role. The text is `font-mono` in the `code` step. The gutter shows the line numbers in `muted-foreground`, right aligned, with a 1 px `border` edge on its right. A `field-description` under the box names the keys that leave the editor (see Accessibility). The theme of the editor (`EditorView.theme`) reads the tokens of `stack.css` as CSS custom properties, such as `var(--color-muted-foreground)`, so a change of the mode or the theme needs no editor code (measured in both modes of both themes). The theme also replaces each value of the CodeMirror base theme that shows: the dotted focus outline, the `monospace` font and the 1.4 line height of the scroller, the black caret and the grey gutter. The box takes its font from its classes, `font-mono text-xs`, and the scroller inherits it, so that the phone key can change it.

**Variants.** None. The text has no syntax colors, because the editor loads no language package.

**Sizes.** The box grows with its text, 16 px a line, and it is at least 66 px high. The text and the gutter are each at least 64 px high (`min-height: calc(var(--spacing) * 16)` on `.cm-content` and `.cm-gutter`), which holds three lines and the padding from 768 px. A min-height on the box leaves the gutter edge at the height of the text (measured). The product sets the height where the box stops with a class on the part, such as `max-h-80`, and the text then scrolls inside the box. At `max-h-80` the box stops at 320 px, with 19 lines (measured). The text has 8 px padding at the top and the bottom, and each line has 10 px at the sides. The gutter is 36 px wide up to line 999 and 40.9 px at line 1,000, and its 1 px edge comes after that width (measured). From 768 px a long line does not wrap, and it scrolls sideways.

**States.** Focus-visible: the text is a `contenteditable` element, so `:focus-visible` matches on a click too, as on an input. The box then draws the focus ring of `## Shapes`, a `ring` edge and the 3 px `ring/50` halo (`has-focus-visible:border-ring has-focus-visible:ring-3 has-focus-visible:ring-ring/50`), with the same values as an input (measured). Read-only: the component sets `[data-readonly]` on the box, and the box has no edge (`data-readonly:border-transparent`) and the `muted` ground, also under the gutter. On focus a read-only box draws the full focus ring, with its `ring` edge (`data-readonly:has-focus-visible:border-ring`, measured). A `field-description` above the box gives the reason, such as "The release runs now, so its steps do not change." The text keeps the focus, so a person can select and copy it, and CodeMirror refuses each edit (measured). Set read-only with `EditorState.readOnly`, never with `EditorView.editable` set to false, because that takes the text out of the focus order. Invalid: `[aria-invalid="true"]` on the text, which `has-aria-invalid:` matches on the box, draws the `destructive` edge and the `destructive/20` halo of a text area, and `destructive/50` and `destructive/40` in dark (measured equal to a text area). A `field-error` under the box names the line, quotes the start of the line and gives the fix, such as "Line 2 needs a colon after 'steps'." Below 768 px the gutter hides and long lines wrap, so the quote finds the line where the number does not. A code editor has no disabled state: a text that the person may not change is read-only, with its reason.

**Tokens.** `code-editor`, `code-editor-readonly`, `code-editor-invalid`, `code-editor-gutter`, `code-editor-phone`. The gutter sets no data-slot, so its key names the gutter element of CodeMirror. The edge of a read-only box is transparent, and the halos carry an opacity, so they have no key.

**Accessibility.** CodeMirror gives the text the textbox role and `aria-multiline="true"`, and `aria-readonly="true"` when it is read-only. A `label` reaches only a native control through `for`, so the `field-label` takes an `id`, and the text takes `aria-labelledby` with that id. Tab indents the line and Shift+Tab outdents it, so Tab alone does not leave the editor. Esc, then Tab within 2 s, moves the focus to the next control, and Esc, then Shift+Tab, moves it to the previous control (measured). This meets WCAG 2.2 success criterion 2.1.2, No Keyboard Trap (level A). The `field-description` under the box names the two keys: "Tab indents. Esc, then Tab, moves the focus out." The text points to it with `aria-describedby`. In a read-only editor, Tab moves the focus at once, because CodeMirror does not indent a read-only text (measured). The component takes two bindings out of `defaultKeymap`. With the default binding of CodeMirror, Escape collapses a selection, and then the Esc does not open the way out (measured). Mod-Enter adds a blank line, and Cmd+Enter is the shared key of the main action of a form (see `architecture/web-app.md`). The component also stops each Esc in an editor that a person can edit. Then a dialog, a sheet, a drawer or a popover around it stays open. This is an exception to the rule that Esc closes the open layer (see `architecture/web-app.md`). Tab then leaves the editor, and the next Esc closes the layer (measured in a dialog). A read-only editor does not stop Esc, because Tab leaves it at once, so Esc closes the layer around it (measured in a dialog).

**Example.**

```svelte
<Field.Field>
  <Field.Label id="steps-label">Steps</Field.Label>
  <CodeEditor bind:value={steps} class="max-h-80" aria-labelledby="steps-label" aria-describedby="steps-keys" />
  <Field.Description id="steps-keys"><Kbd.Root>Tab</Kbd.Root> indents. <Kbd.Root>Esc</Kbd.Root>, then <Kbd.Root>Tab</Kbd.Root>, moves the focus out.</Field.Description>
</Field.Field>

<!-- In CodeEditor.svelte: the keys, the Esc that stays in an editor that a person can edit, and a theme that reads the tokens of stack.css. -->
<script lang="ts">
  const keys = keymap.of([indentWithTab, ...historyKeymap, ...defaultKeymap.filter((b) => !['Escape', 'Mod-Enter'].includes(b.key ?? ''))]);
  const keepEscape = EditorView.domEventHandlers({ keydown: (e, view) => { if (e.key === 'Escape' && !view.state.readOnly) e.stopPropagation(); return false; } });
  const theme = EditorView.theme({
    '&.cm-focused': { outline: 'none' },
    '.cm-scroller': { fontFamily: 'inherit', lineHeight: 'inherit', overflow: 'auto' },
    '.cm-content, .cm-gutter': { minHeight: 'calc(var(--spacing) * 16)' },
    '.cm-content': { padding: 'calc(var(--spacing) * 2) 0', caretColor: 'var(--color-foreground)' },
    '.cm-gutters': { backgroundColor: 'var(--color-background)', color: 'var(--color-muted-foreground)', borderRightColor: 'var(--color-border)' },
    '&[data-readonly] .cm-gutters': { backgroundColor: 'var(--color-muted)' }
  });
</script>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the gutter hides and long lines wrap (`EditorView.lineWrapping`), so the text never scrolls sideways (measured at 390 px). The line numbers do not show, so an error finds its line by the quote of its start (see States). The component swaps the two in a `Compartment` when the width crosses 768 px, and the swap works both ways while the page is open (measured). The text renders 16 px on a 22 px line (`code-editor-phone`), so that a phone does not zoom on focus, as on an input. The box is at least 66 px high, which is above the 44 px target.

### code-block

**Purpose.** A code block shows a text that a person reads and copies and never changes: code, a command or a log. It has a copy button. A text that a person changes goes into a `code-editor`. A text that a person may not change for a time, such as the steps of a release that runs, is a read-only `code-editor` with its reason, because it stays a field of a form. One value in a sentence, such as a branch or a short hash, takes `font-mono` in the sentence and needs no block (see `## Typography`). A value on its own line that a person copies, such as a token that shows once, is a code block of one line. A log that grows while it shows goes into a `message-scroller`, which keeps the newest line in view. The change between two versions of a text goes into a `diff`. The code block is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/CodeBlock`, and gives the text and the name of the copy button. STACK builds the part from `scroll-area`, `button` and `tooltip`, and the part needs no library of its own. Its root (`code-block-wrapper`) holds the box and the status line, and it takes the class, the other attributes and `ref` of the product. The class places the part, and a `max-h-*` class also sets the height where the box stops.

**Anatomy.** The `code-block` slot is the box: the `muted` ground, the 8 px radius and no edge, which is the look of a read-only `code-editor`. The code block takes `dir="ltr"` on its outer element, which holds the box and the status line, because code reads from left to right in every language: inside a block in Arabic, a box with no `dir` puts the copy button on the left (measured). The box holds two columns. The left column is a `scroll-area` that holds the text (`code-block-text`), `font-mono` in the `code` step and in `foreground`. The right column holds the copy button at its top: an `outline` icon button of size `icon-xs` with the copy icon, and a `tooltip` that shows its name. The button is outside the scroll area, so it never covers a line, and it stays in place while the text scrolls. A command shows a prompt sign (`code-block-prompt`), `$` in `muted-foreground`, before each line. A line after a line that ends in `\` continues the same command, so it takes no sign. A log can show the time of each line (`code-block-time`) before its text, in `muted-foreground` and two spaces from the text. Code can show the line numbers in a gutter (`code-block-gutter`) on the left, as the code editor does: in `muted-foreground`, right aligned, with a 1 px `border` edge on its right, on the `muted` ground of the box. The gutter sticks to the left edge while a long line scrolls sideways (measured). The text has no syntax colors, as in the code editor. A name of the block that shows, such as a file path or "Import log", is a line of the screen above the box, such as the heading of a section of the pane (see `### Detail pane`) or a caption in `small` type at 600 in `muted-foreground`, and the block takes its name from that line (see Accessibility).

**Variants.** `kind` is `code` by default, `command` or `log`. `code` is a file, a configuration, a script or an instruction. From 768 px a long line of code does not wrap, and it scrolls sideways, so that the indent of each line keeps the structure of the code (`orientation="both"` on the scroll area). Below 768 px it wraps, as in the code editor (see Phone). `numbers` adds the gutter to code. `command` is one or more commands that a person runs in a terminal. A long line wraps, and the wrapped part starts under the command, after the place of the sign. `log` is a text that a system wrote or sent, such as the screen of a terminal, the prompt that a program sent to an agent or the record of an import, and a long line wraps. A command and a log wrap at every width and scroll down only (`orientation="vertical"`). `entries` gives each line of a log a time, in the form that `architecture/web-app.md` gives for a log. From 768 px a wrapped line of a log starts under the text, not under the time, so that the times make a column (measured).

**Sizes.** Each line is 16 px high. The text has 8 px of padding at the top and the bottom, and 10 px at the sides, as a line of the code editor. The copy button is 24 px, with 4 px around it, so a block of one line is 32 px high, the height of a control, and the icon sits level with the first line (measured). The box grows with its text and stops at 320 px (`max-h-80`), as a text area and a code editor do, and then the text scrolls inside the box (measured). A class on the part sets another height, such as `max-h-30` for 120 px (measured). The gutter is at least 36 px wide (`min-w-9`), with 4 px of padding on its left and 8 px on its right. The time column is as wide as the longest time and its two spaces.

**States.** The copy button has the states of a `button`. Copied: a click, Enter or Space writes the text to the clipboard, and the icon turns to a check for 2 s. The tooltip then says "Copied", and a polite live region says it to a screen reader (measured). After 2 s the icon and the tooltip go back, and the live region empties (measured). The confirmation stays in the button and never shows as a toast, because a copy has no Undo (see `### sonner`) and `## Do's and Don'ts` allows no toast for a trivial action. Not copied: when the browser refuses the write, as on a page that is not served over HTTPS, the block selects its text, so that the person can copy it by hand. The tooltip and the live region then say so ("The text is selected, so copy it by hand."), and the icon stays the copy icon (measured with a refused write). The same words show as a line of `text-xs` in `muted-foreground` under the box, because a phone shows no tooltip, and the selection alone is easy to miss there. The line and the live region go back to empty after 2 s, or at the next copy that works. Focus-visible on a viewport that scrolls draws the focus ring of `## Shapes`: a 1 px `ring` edge and the 3 px `ring/50` halo (measured). Nova draws the halo only, so the example adds the edge to the scroll area. A code block has no disabled, invalid or read-only state, because a person never changes its text.

**Tokens.** `code-block`, `code-block-gutter`, `code-block-prompt`, `code-block-time`. The copy button is an outline button that renders the slot of a tooltip trigger, so it takes the look of `button-outline` and the target of `tooltip-trigger-phone`. The halo carries an opacity, so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** The copy button copies the text and nothing else: no prompt sign, no line number and no line break at the end (measured). A block with `copyValue` writes that text in place of its own. An example is a setup key that shows in groups of four and copies with no space. A selection by hand of such a block gives the text as it shows, with its spaces. The selection after a copy that the browser refused gives the same text. A command that ends in a line break runs at once when a person pastes it into a terminal. A log copies each line with its time and the two spaces, as it shows (measured). The prompt sign and the gutter take `select-none` and `aria-hidden="true"`, so a selection by hand and a screen reader skip them too. A selection by hand of a command, of code and of a log gives the same text as the button (measured in Chromium). The button has a name in words in the language of the product, and its tooltip shows the same name on hover and on keyboard focus (measured). On a screen with more than one block, the name says what the button copies, such as "Copy the join command". The block takes its name from the line above it with `aria-labelledby`, or from `aria-label`. When the text overflows the box, the viewport of the scroll area takes `tabindex="0"`, the `region` role and that name, so that a keyboard reaches it, and each press of an arrow key scrolls it 40 px (measured). This meets WCAG 2.2 success criterion 2.1.1, Keyboard (level A). When the text fits, the viewport takes none of them, so the block adds no stop to the tab order (measured). A `ResizeObserver` on the viewport and on its content reads the overflow again when the width or the text changes. The nova scroll area passes no attribute to its viewport, so the component sets them on the element of `viewportRef`. Each time of a log is a `<time>` element with its `datetime`, and its tooltip (`title`) gives the full date and time, as `architecture/web-app.md` asks for a log.

**Example.**

```svelte
<h3 id="join-label" class="text-xs font-semibold text-muted-foreground">Join command</h3>
<CodeBlock kind="command" code={command} aria-labelledby="join-label" copyLabel="Copy the join command" />

<!-- In CodeBlock.svelte: the box, the text that scrolls, the copy column and the status line. -->
<div bind:this={ref} data-slot="code-block-wrapper" dir="ltr" class={cn('flex max-h-80 min-w-0 flex-col gap-1 print:max-h-none', className)} {...rest}>
<div data-slot="code-block" class="flex min-h-0 rounded-lg bg-muted font-mono text-xs text-foreground">
  <ScrollArea bind:viewportRef orientation={kind === 'code' ? 'both' : 'vertical'} class="flex min-w-0 flex-1 rounded-l-lg [&>[data-slot=scroll-area-viewport]:focus-visible]:-outline-offset-1 [&>[data-slot=scroll-area-viewport]:focus-visible]:outline-solid [&>[data-slot=scroll-area-viewport]:focus-visible]:outline-ring">
    <!-- The text of a command and of a log: this wrap. The text of code (kind="code") does not wrap from 768 px, and below 768 px it takes max-md:whitespace-pre-wrap max-md:wrap-anywhere. -->
    <pre data-slot="code-block-text" class="px-2.5 py-2 whitespace-pre-wrap wrap-break-word max-md:py-4"><code>{text}</code></pre>
  </ScrollArea>
  <div class="shrink-0 p-1 print:hidden">
    <Tooltip.Root disableCloseOnTriggerClick>
      <Tooltip.Trigger>
        {#snippet child({ props })}
          <Button {...props} variant="outline" size="icon-xs" aria-label={copyLabel} onclick={copy}>{#if copied}<CheckIcon />{:else}<CopyIcon />{/if}</Button>
        {/snippet}
      </Tooltip.Trigger>
      <Tooltip.Content>{copied ? copiedLabel : copyLabel}</Tooltip.Content>
    </Tooltip.Root>
  </div>
</div>
<span role="status" class={failed ? 'text-xs text-muted-foreground' : 'sr-only'}>{copied ? copiedLabel : failed ? failedLabel : ''}</span>
</div>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the code step follows `text-xs`, so the text renders 13 px on an 18 px line (measured), with 16 px of padding at the top and the bottom. The copy button renders the `tooltip-trigger` slot, so `tooltip-trigger-phone` makes it 44 by 44 px, and a block of one line is 52 px high (measured). The 16 px padding keeps the first line level with the icon: their middles are 25 px and 26 px below the top of the box (measured). Code wraps (`max-md:whitespace-pre-wrap max-md:wrap-anywhere`) and the gutter hides (`max-md:hidden`), as in the code editor, so that no block scrolls sideways on a phone (measured at 390 px). A word that is wider than the box, such as a token, breaks at any place there. A wrapped line of a log starts at the left edge, under the time, so that the text keeps the width of the screen. A phone shows no tooltip, so the check icon and the live region confirm the copy, and a refused copy shows its line under the box.

### diff

**Purpose.** A diff shows the change between two versions of a text or of code. Examples are the change of a branch, the edit of a document since its last approval, and a change of a configuration. It is read-only. A text that a person changes goes into a `code-editor`, and a text that a person reads and copies goes into a `code-block`. The product brings the lines: each line with its kind, unchanged, added or removed, its old and its new number, and the words that changed in a changed line. Git, the server or the product computes them where the two versions are, so the part loads no diff library and computes nothing. The diff is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/Diff`, and gives the lines and the names of the two versions. STACK builds the part from `button`, `tooltip` and `kbd`. Its root (`diff-wrapper`) holds the box, the line of a refused copy and the status line, and it takes the class, the other attributes and `ref` of the product. The class places the part.

**Anatomy.** The `diff` slot of code is the box of a `code-block`: the `muted` ground, the 8 px radius and no edge, with the text in `font-mono` in the `code` step. A sides row (`diff-sides`) at the top of the box names the two versions in `small` and `muted-foreground`, with a `border` edge below. It shows `−` and the old name, then `+` and the new name, such as a commit and a branch. A copy button follows each name, and the Previous change and Next change buttons stand at the right end. Each is a `ghost` icon button of size `icon-xs` in a `tooltip`. Each line (`diff-line`) holds the old and the new number in two gutters (`diff-gutter`), then the sign (`diff-sign`), then the text. The gutters are `muted-foreground`, right aligned, with a 1 px `border` edge after the second gutter, as in a code block. The sign is `+` on an added line, `−` on a removed line and nothing on an unchanged line, in `foreground`. An added line has only its new number, and a removed line only its old number. An added line takes the `diff-added` role at 10% (`bg-diff-added/10`) across its gutters and its text, and a removed line takes `bg-diff-removed/10`. In a changed line, each word that changed takes the role at 30% over the tint of its line. An added word also takes an underline (`bg-diff-added/30 underline`), and a removed word a line through it (`bg-diff-removed/30 line-through`). So the sign, the number column and the underline or the line carry each change, and the tint never carries it alone. A fold row (`diff-fold`) stands for unchanged lines that fold. It is a button across the box, with a `border` edge above and below. It holds the unfold icon over the gutters and words such as "Show 8 unchanged lines" in `small` and `muted-foreground`.

**Variants.** `kind` is `code` by default, or `text`. In `text`, such as a document or a message, each paragraph is a line in the `reading` step, on the ground of the page, with no box and no numbers. Its sign stands in a column of 12 px, and its tint is a block with the 6 px radius and 8 px by 4 px of padding. `view` is `unified` by default, or `split`. Unified shows one column, and each removed line comes before the added lines that replace it. Split shows the old version on the left and the new version on the right, each with its own gutter. An unchanged line shows on both sides, a removed line stands beside the added line that replaces it, and a side with no line shows an empty cell. The product names that removed line in `replaces` on the added line, as its old number. A line with no pair takes a row of its own, and each side keeps its order (measured). Without `replaces`, a change pairs its lines by position: the first removed line stands beside the first added line, the second beside the second, and so on. Use unified for a short change, for text, and in a pane, a card or a dialog. Use split for code where many lines change in place, so that a person reads each old line beside its new line. Split needs the width of a page, so below 1024 px (`lg`) every diff is unified (measured at 1000 px). A page with diffs holds one switch above the first diff, a segmented control of `### toggle-group` with Unified and Split, which hides below 1024 px. The pick applies to every diff of the page. Unchanged lines that are more than three lines away from a change fold into one row, when that row hides two lines or more. In text the distance is one paragraph. A product that has only the lines near each change, such as the hunks of git, gives a line of the kind `folded` with the count of the lines between. Its row shows the count, such as "37 unchanged lines", and it is no button, because the product does not have those lines. Its `context`, the text after the second `@@` of the git hunk header, shows after the count in `font-mono`, on one line that ends in an ellipsis (measured). A change of several files shows one diff for each file, under a heading with the path in `font-mono` and the counts of the file, such as "+7 −3". The counts are text in `muted-foreground`, with words for a screen reader, such as "7 lines added, 3 lines removed". They take no color, because the sign carries the change.

**Sizes.** Each line of code is 16 px high, as in a code block, with 8 px of padding above the first line and under the last line. The text ends 10 px from the right edge. A long line wraps at every width, and the wrapped part starts under the text, after the sign, so that the numbers and the signs stay a column. A word that is wider than the column breaks at any place (`wrap-anywhere`). A diff never scrolls sideways, because the two sides of the split view would scroll apart. A gutter is 36 px wide up to line 999 and 40.89 px from line 1,000, with 4 px of padding on its left and 8 px on its right. The sign column is 20 px wide (measured). The sides row is 33 px high, with buttons of 24 px, and a fold row is 24 px high (`spacing.control-xs`, measured). A paragraph of one line in text is 29 px high: the 21 px line of `reading` and 4 px of padding above and under it. The paragraphs are 4 px apart (measured).

**States.** The first line of each change takes `tabindex="-1"`. Next change, Previous change, N and Shift+N move the focus to it, and the line draws the focus ring of a row inside its edge (`ring-inset`). The component focuses the line with `focusVisible: true`, so the ring shows after a click on a button too, and not only after a key (measured in Chromium). A click, Enter or Space on a fold row shows its lines in place, and the focus moves to the first line that shows, because the button goes (measured). N then moves the focus to the first change after that line, and Shift+N to the change before it (measured). Copied: a copy button writes the whole old or new version to the clipboard, and its icon turns to a check for 2 s, as on a code block (measured). Only a diff that the product marks as complete has copy buttons (`complete`). The product marks it only when it gave every line of both versions, because nobody can copy a side whole when some of its lines are missing. The part cannot see this itself: the hunks of git that start at line 1 leave out the lines after the last hunk. When the browser refuses the write, the diff opens its folds and selects the text of that side, as a code block does. The person then copies it by hand. While that selection lasts, the lines of the other side take `select-none`, so the selection holds one side only (measured in both views). A copy by hand of code then gives the same text as the button. A copy by hand of text puts one line break between two paragraphs, where the button puts an empty line (measured). For 2 s, a line under the box says that the browser did not copy the text and that the text is selected. It takes `small` and `muted-foreground`, and a move to a change does not replace it (measured). A line has no hover state. A diff has no disabled, invalid or read-only state, because a person never changes its text.

**Tokens.** `diff`, `diff-gutter`, `diff-sides`, `diff-fold`, `diff-fold-phone`. The tints carry an opacity, so they have no key, and the contrast check reads their pairs (see Colors). The buttons take the keys of `button` and the phone key of `tooltip-trigger-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The box has the `group` role and takes its name from the line above it with `aria-labelledby`, as a code block does. Each changed line starts with a word that does not show, "Added: " or "Removed: ", so that a screen reader says what changed. Chromium gives a removed line the text "Removed: if (tries > 2) throw new GaveUp(part.index);" (measured in the accessibility tree). The sign and the gutters take `aria-hidden="true"`, and the sign, the gutters and the word take `select-none`. So a selection by hand gives the text of the lines and nothing else (measured). An empty line holds a `<br>`, so that a copy by hand keeps it (measured). A changed word is an `<ins>` element in an added line and a `<del>` element in a removed line, with the marks that Anatomy gives. Chromium gives them the `insertion` and the `deletion` role (measured in the accessibility tree). In text, a word that does not show, "inserted" or "deleted", comes before each changed word, so that a screen reader finds the change in a long paragraph. Chromium gives the added words of the spec the text "inserted goes again, up to three times, with a longer wait each time" (measured). The sides row reads "Old version: main, commit 4e1a9c2" and "New version: part-retry". While the focus is in a diff, N moves it to the next change and Shift+N to the previous change, and the move wraps at each end. The diff stops the event, so a key of the product does not fire too (measured). The buttons name their keys in `aria-keyshortcuts` and in their tooltip. A polite live region outside the box says "Change 2 of 5" after each move and "Copied" after a copy (measured). `architecture/web-app.md` lists the keys of the component. The product gives each word of the part in its own language.

**Example.**

```svelte
<h3 id="retry-label" class="flex gap-2 text-xs font-semibold text-muted-foreground">
  <span class="font-mono font-normal text-foreground">src/upload/retry.ts</span>
  <span class="font-normal tabular-nums">+7 −3<span class="sr-only">: 7 lines added, 3 lines removed</span></span>
</h3>
<Diff {lines} {view} complete oldName="main, commit 4e1a9c2" newName="part-retry" aria-labelledby="retry-label" />

<!-- In Diff.svelte: the box, an added line with a changed word, and a fold row. -->
<div data-slot="diff" role="group" aria-labelledby={labelledby} style:--diff-gutter={gutter} class="rounded-lg bg-muted font-mono text-xs text-foreground" {onkeydown}>
  <div data-slot="diff-line" data-kind="added" data-change={k} tabindex="-1" class="flex min-h-4 bg-diff-added/10 outline-none focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 focus-visible:ring-inset">
    <span data-slot="diff-gutter" aria-hidden="true" class="w-(--diff-gutter) shrink-0 pr-2 pl-1 text-right text-muted-foreground select-none max-md:hidden"></span>
    <span data-slot="diff-gutter" aria-hidden="true" class="w-(--diff-gutter) shrink-0 border-r border-border pr-2 pl-1 text-right text-muted-foreground select-none max-md:hidden">8</span>
    <span data-slot="diff-sign" aria-hidden="true" class="w-5 shrink-0 text-center select-none">+</span>
    <span class="min-w-0 flex-1 pr-2.5 whitespace-pre-wrap wrap-anywhere"><span class="sr-only select-none">Added: </span>  if (tries <ins class="bg-diff-added/30 underline underline-offset-2">&gt;= limits.tries</ins>) {</span>
  </div>
  <button type="button" data-slot="diff-fold" class="flex h-6 w-full items-center gap-2 border-y border-border font-sans text-xs text-muted-foreground outline-none hover:text-foreground focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 focus-visible:ring-inset" onclick={() => open(from)}>
    <span class="flex shrink-0 justify-end pr-2 max-md:hidden" style:width="calc(2 * var(--diff-gutter))" aria-hidden="true"><UnfoldVerticalIcon class="size-3.5" /></span>
    <span class="max-md:pl-2.5">Show 8 unchanged lines</span>
  </button>
</div>
<span role="status" class="sr-only">{said}</span>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a diff is unified. The gutters hide (`max-md:hidden`), as in a code block, and the signs stay, so each line keeps its change. The code step follows `text-xs`, so the text renders 13 px on an 18 px line (measured). A fold row is 44 px high (`diff-fold-phone`), and each button is 44 by 44 px through `tooltip-trigger-phone` (measured at 390 px). The sides row wraps when the names do not fit, and the change buttons then take a line of their own. A diff of text takes the 15 px `reading-phone` step on a 22 px line, so a paragraph of one line is 30 px high (measured).

### qr-code

**Purpose.** A QR code carries a value from the screen to a phone, or to a device with a camera. Examples are a link that opens a record on a phone, the pairing of a scanner, and a key that an app reads. The code always stands beside its value as text, in a `code-block` of one line with its copy button. So a person with no camera, a person with a screen reader and a person with the page on the phone all get the value. A value that a person reads aloud, such as a ticket number, takes no code. The QR code is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/QrCode`, and gives the value and its names. The part computes the modules with lean-qr, the library of its record in `STACK.md`, and draws them as SVG. The library computes on the server too, because it needs no DOM. Its root (`qr-code`) takes the class, the other attributes and `ref` of the product, and the class places the part.

**Anatomy.** The `qr-code` slot is a box that reads its own width (`@container`). It holds the tile and the column of the value. The tile (`qr-code-tile`) is a square of 224 by 224 px with the 8 px radius. It holds one `<svg>` of the same size, in which one `<path>` in `currentColor` draws the dark modules. The tile is dark on light in both modes, because a camera reads dark modules on a light ground. In light mode the ground is `background` and the modules are `foreground`. In dark mode the tile swaps the two roles (`dark:bg-foreground dark:text-background`), so it shows as a light square on the dark page. A quiet zone of at least 4 modules surrounds the code inside the tile, the figure of ISO/IEC 18004. STACK read the figure on the page of the maker of the format, and not in the standard. That page says: "QR Code requires a four-module wide margin at all sides of a symbol" (qrcode.com, read 2026-10-26). The tile holds the code alone: no logo, no color and no round modules. A logo covers modules and uses up the error correction. The column of the value holds an optional instruction in `body`, then the caption of the value in `small` at 600 in `muted-foreground`. The `code-block` of the value and, for an address, the link that opens it follow. Some codes hold an address that only an app reads. For such a code, the code block holds what a person types into that app in place of a scan. For the `otpauth://` address of an authenticator app, the block holds the setup key in groups of four. Its copy button writes the key with no space (see `### Authenticator app` in `architecture/web-app.md`). The component takes `text` for the text of the block, which is `value` by default. It takes `copyValue` for the text that its button writes, which is the text of the block by default. Two decoders, jsQR 1.4.0 and ZXing in zxing-wasm 3.1.6, read each code of the showcase with no inversion. They read it from screenshots at 1440 and 390 px, at 1x and 2x, in both modes of both themes. The result is 80 codes of 80 (measured 2026-10-27). The 80 include the code of the setup of an authenticator app.

**Variants.** `kind` is `text` by default, or `link`. `text` is a value that an app or a device reads, such as a pairing key or the `otpauth://` address of an authenticator app. `link` is an address that a phone opens. It takes only an `https:` address that the product makes, never a value that a person typed, because the button passes the value to `href` as it is. It adds an `outline` button, "Open the link", under the value. The button is an `<a>` with the address, because a person with the page on a phone opens it there. From a box of 512 px (`@lg`), the column of the value stands beside the tile, 24 px from it (`@lg:flex-row @lg:gap-6`). Below 512 px it stands under the tile, 16 px from it (`gap-4`). So in a pane or a dialog on a desktop the code comes first, and a phone scans it there. The value is not a log, but it takes `kind="log"` of the code block, the kind that wraps a long line at every width, so the whole value shows.

**Sizes.** The tile is 224 by 224 px, the quiet zone included. It keeps its size in px at every text size of `### Text size`, so each module stays a whole px (measured at 93% and 115%). Each module is a whole number of px and at least 4 px, so its edges fall on the pixels of a screen. At the reference size of a CSS px, 0.26 mm, a module of 4 px is 1.06 mm wide. That is four times the module of 0.254 mm in the printed example of the maker (computed). The camera app of a phone read a code of version 7 at 4 px a module from the screen of a laptop (measured 2026-10-26). The component takes the largest module that fits the tile, and it centers the code on a whole px. So a short value gets larger modules: version 2 and version 3 took 6 px, and version 7, of 45 modules, took 4 px (measured). Version 7 is the largest code that keeps modules of 4 px in the tile. At level M it holds 122 bytes, which is enough for a link with a token. A link of 113 bytes took version 7, and a link of 126 bytes was too long (measured with lean-qr 2.7.4). The level is M, which restores about 15% of the codewords. The maker of the format says "Typically, Level M (15%) is most frequently selected" (qrcode.com, read 2026-10-26). Level L restores about 7.5% (the type notes of lean-qr 2.7.4), and the maker keeps it for "clean environment with the large amount of data". A screen can show a reflection, and paper can fold, so STACK keeps M (not measured). The library raises the level to Q or H when the higher level fits in the same version, so the raise costs no module. The key of the showcase took level Q in version 2 (measured).

**States.** Loading: a still `skeleton` of 224 by 224 px takes the place of the tile. A skeleton of 32 px takes the place of the code block. The caption stays, and the box takes `aria-busy="true"`. At 1440 px the part was 224 px high while it loaded and after it loaded. At 390 px a part with no instruction and no link was 318 px high in both states (measured). Too long: a value that needs a version past 7 shows a note in the place of the code (`qr-code-note`). The note is a square of the same size on `muted`, with "This value is too long for a QR code. Copy it as text." in `small` and `muted-foreground`. The value, its copy button and its link stay. For such a value lean-qr throws the error "Too much data" (code 4), and the component shows the note. A product treats the note as a defect. It puts a short address or an identifier into the code, and its server keeps the long value. Copied and not copied: the states of the copy button of `### code-block`. Error: when the value does not arrive, the error shows inline in the place of the part, with a retry button. "Empty, loading and error" in `architecture/web-app.md` gives the rule.

**Tokens.** `qr-code-tile`, `qr-code-note`. `qr-code-tile` gives the pair of light mode. In dark mode the tile swaps the two roles. A ratio does not change when its two colors swap, so the contrast check covers both modes (see Colors). The contrast check measured 19.80:1 and 18.97:1 in the two modes of the default theme. It measured 12.59:1 and 14.61:1 in the graphite theme. A drawn code at 4.54:1 read in both decoders, and at 3.19:1 jsQR read nothing (measured). The code block and the link take their own keys, and the link takes `button-phone` below 768 px.

**Accessibility.** The `<svg>` takes `role="img"` and an `aria-label` that says what the code opens or holds. An example is "QR code of the link that pairs a phone". The name never holds the value. A long address or a key read aloud tells a person little, and the code block beside the code gives the value as text. Chromium reads the part in this order: the image, the instruction, the caption, the code, the copy button and the link (measured in its accessibility tree). While the part loads, the skeletons have no role, so a screen reader reads only the caption. The tile takes `forced-color-adjust: none` (`forced-color-adjust-none`), so that it keeps its two colors in forced colors. Without the class, forced colors in a dark scheme drew white modules on a black tile. Then neither decoder read the code (measured in Chromium). A value that is a secret, such as a pairing key, shows only on the page that makes it. It never goes into the name of the code, a tooltip, the address of a page or a log.

**Example.**

```svelte
<QrCode value={link} kind="link" label="QR code of the link that pairs a phone" valueLabel="Pairing link" copyLabel="Copy the pairing link">
  <p class="text-sm">Scan the code with the camera of the phone. On the phone itself, open the link.</p>
</QrCode>

<QrCode value={otpauth} label="QR code of the setup key" valueLabel="Setup key" text={setupKey.replace(/(.{4})(?=.)/g, '$1 ')} copyValue={setupKey} copyLabel="Copy the setup key">
  <p class="text-sm">Scan this code with the authenticator app on your phone, or type the setup key into it.</p>
</QrCode>

<!-- In QrCode.svelte: the modules at level M or higher up to version 7, then the box, the tile and the column of the value. -->
<script lang="ts">
  import { correction, generate } from 'lean-qr';
  let code = null;
  try {
    code = generate(value, { minCorrectionLevel: correction.M, maxVersion: 7 });
  } catch (e) {
    // Code 4 is "Too much data": the part shows qr-code-note.
    if ((e as { code?: number }).code !== 4) throw e;
  }
</script>

<div data-slot="qr-code" class="@container print:break-inside-avoid">
  <div class="flex flex-col gap-4 @lg:flex-row @lg:items-start @lg:gap-6">
    <div data-slot="qr-code-tile" class="w-fit shrink-0 rounded-lg bg-background text-foreground forced-color-adjust-none dark:bg-foreground dark:text-background">
      <svg role="img" aria-label="QR code of the link that pairs a phone" width="224" height="224" viewBox="0 0 224 224" class="block">
        <path d="M25 25h42v6h-42z…" fill="currentColor" shape-rendering="crispEdges" />
      </svg>
    </div>
    <div class="flex min-w-0 flex-1 flex-col gap-2 max-md:order-first print:order-none">
      <p class="text-sm">Scan the code with the camera of the phone. On the phone itself, open the link.</p>
      <span id="pair-label" class="text-xs font-semibold text-muted-foreground">Pairing link</span>
      <CodeBlock kind="log" code={link} aria-labelledby="pair-label" copyLabel="Copy the pairing link" />
      <Button href={link} variant="outline" class="self-start print:hidden">Open the link</Button>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a person usually holds the page on the phone that would scan it, and a phone cannot scan its own screen. So the column of the value comes first (`max-md:order-first`): the instruction, the value with its copy button, and the link that opens an address. In a box under 512 px the code follows 16 px under the column, for a second device. From a box of 512 px it stands 24 px to the right of the column. The tile keeps 224 px. The copy button is 44 by 44 px through `tooltip-trigger-phone`, and the link is 44 px high through `button-phone` (measured at 390 px). The skeleton of the code block is 52 px high (`max-md:h-13`), as a code block of one line is. A box under 512 px on a desktop keeps the code first, because a phone can scan that screen.

**Print.** On paper a phone scans the code, so the code comes first again (`print:order-none`). The column of A4 is 657 px wide, so the value stands beside the code. The copy button and the link take `print:hidden`, and the box takes `print:break-inside-avoid`. The page takes light mode for the print (see `### A screen page in print`). The ground of the tile drops, so the modules print in `foreground` on the paper. STACK made a PDF of A4 with the margins of `### Paper`, from windows of 1440 and 390 px in dark mode. It held each code in gray 10 on white. Both decoders read each code at 300 dpi (measured with `page.pdf()` and `pdftoppm`). Version 3 printed 46.1 mm wide with modules of 1.59 mm, and version 7 printed 47.6 mm wide with modules of 1.06 mm.

### label

**Purpose.** A label names a control. It sits above an input, a select or a text area, and beside a check box, a radio button or a switch. In a form, use `field-label`, which is this label inside a `field`.

**Anatomy.** One `label` slot, a native `<label>` from Bits UI, in `body` at 500 with a line height of 1 (`leading-none`). An icon or a badge after the text keeps an 8 px gap.

**Variants.** None.

**Sizes.** 13 px text on a 13 px line. Keep a label to one line, because a second line would touch the first.

**States.** Disabled: a label after a disabled control with the `peer` class (`.peer:disabled ~ label`), or inside a group with `[data-disabled="true"]`, draws at 50% opacity.

**Tokens.** `label`.

**Accessibility.** Every control has a visible label, and a placeholder never replaces it. Connect the label with `for` and the `id` of the control, so that a click on the label focuses or sets the control. A label with `for` gives a button its whole name, and the text in the button does not count. So a button that shows a value, such as the trigger of a `select`, of a date field (see `### calendar`) or of a `time-zone-field`, keeps `for`. It also takes `aria-labelledby` with the `id` of the label and its own `id`. Its name then holds the label and the value, such as "Fruit Pear" (measured in Chromium 148). A native `<select>` and an `input` keep `for` alone, because they give their value apart from their name.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Label for="name">Name</Label>
<Input id="name" bind:value={name} />
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the text renders 15 px (`body-phone`). A label is not a target by itself, and the row of its `field` gives the target (see `field`).

### field

**Purpose.** A field frames every form control: the label, the control, the description and the error, in one block. Build every form from fields.

**Anatomy.** A `field-set` with a `field-legend` holds a `field-group`, in which the fields are 20 px apart. A `field` holds a `field-label`, the control, a `field-description` and a `field-error`, 8 px apart. `field-content` holds a label and a description beside a check box or a switch, and `field-title` takes the place of the label when the field holds more than one control. A `field-separator` is a line with an optional word, such as "Or".

**Variants.** `orientation="vertical"`, the default, puts the label above the control. `horizontal` puts a check box, a radio button or a switch beside its label. `responsive` is vertical, and horizontal when its `field-group` is 448 px wide or more. A choice card is a `field` inside a `field-label`: it has a `border` edge and the 8 px radius, and with a checked control a `primary/5` ground and a `primary/30` edge (`primary/10` and `primary/20` in dark).

**Sizes.** The label is `body` at 500 with a line height of 1.375 (`leading-snug`). The description is `body` in `muted-foreground` with a line height of 1.5, and the error is `body` in `destructive`. A legend is `section` at 500, or `body` at 500 with `variant="label"`.

**States.** Invalid: `[data-invalid="true"]` on the `field` turns its text `destructive`, and the control carries `[aria-invalid="true"]`. Disabled: `[data-disabled="true"]` on the `field` draws its label at 50% opacity. A choice card shows checked through `:has([data-state="checked"])`, which the `has-data-checked:` variant matches, together with `[data-checked]`.

**Tokens.** `field-invalid`, `field-legend`, `field-label`, `field-description`, `field-error`, `field-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The description states the format, and the error names the rule and the fix. The control points to both with `aria-describedby`. `field-error` has `role="alert"`, so that a screen reader reads it when it shows. A warning that does not block the save is a `field-description` that starts with a warning icon, with the icon and the text in `warning` (`class="text-warning"`).

**Example.**

```svelte
<Field.Field data-invalid={!!error}>
  <Field.Label for="email">Email</Field.Label>
  <Input id="email" type="email" aria-invalid={!!error} aria-describedby="email-help email-error" bind:value={email} />
  <Field.Description id="email-help">The address for invoices.</Field.Description>
  {#if error}<Field.Error id="email-error">{error}</Field.Error>{/if}
</Field.Field>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px every field is at least 44 px high (`field-phone`), so a row with a check box, a radio button or a switch is a 44 px target. Give the label of such a row `max-md:self-stretch`, so that a tap anywhere in the row sets the control. The label and the description render 15 px.

### form

**Purpose.** `form` connects fields to the validation of sveltekit-superforms through formsnap. The preview runs it on SvelteKit 3 with the versions that `preview/components.lock.json` pins. A product builds every form with `field` and validates it in its own code. The `packages` list of `form` in the lock holds `@sveltejs/kit` and `vite`, so its install line pins both at exact versions, because `sveltekit-superforms` needs `@sveltejs/kit` and `@sveltejs/vite-plugin-svelte` needs `vite`. A product that holds other versions of them moves its framework with that install. The install line of `button` alone holds neither.

**Anatomy.** `Form.Field` (`form-item`) holds `Form.Control`, which gives its props to a `Form.Label` (`form-label`, a `label`) and to the control. `Form.Description` (`form-description`) and `Form.FieldErrors` follow. `Form.Fieldset` and `Form.Legend` group fields, and `Form.Button` is a submit `button`. The parts are 8 px apart (`space-y-2`).

**Variants.** None.

**Sizes.** The label is `body` at 500 with a line height of 1, as on `label`. The description is `body` in `muted-foreground`, and an error is `body` at 500 in `destructive`.

**States.** Invalid: formsnap sets `[data-fs-error]` on the label and the legend, which turn `destructive`, and `[aria-invalid="true"]` on the control.

**Tokens.** `form-label`, `form-label-invalid`, `form-description`.

**Accessibility.** formsnap connects the label, the description and the errors to the control with `for`, `aria-describedby` and `aria-invalid`. The rules of `field` apply.

**Example.**

```svelte
<!-- Only the preview uses this form. A product builds each form with Field. -->
<Form.Field {form} name="email">
  <Form.Control>
    {#snippet children({ props })}
      <Form.Label>Email</Form.Label>
      <Input {...props} type="email" bind:value={$formData.email} />
    {/snippet}
  </Form.Control>
  <Form.FieldErrors />
</Form.Field>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the input and the submit button are 44 px high through their own phone keys, and the text renders 15 px. `form-item` has no phone key, because its label stands above its control.

### native-select

**Purpose.** A native select picks one value from a short list with the select of the browser. Use it where the picker of the system serves best, such as a form on a phone, and for a list that needs no icons.

**Anatomy.** A `native-select-wrapper` holds the `native-select` slot and a `native-select-icon`, a 16 px chevron in `muted-foreground`. The `<select>` has the edge and the radius of `input`. `NativeSelect.Option` and `NativeSelect.OptGroup` take the colors of the system (`Canvas`, `CanvasText`).

**Variants.** None.

**Sizes.** `default` is 32 px high, and `sm` is 28 px high with the 6 px radius. The text is `body`, with 10 px padding on the left and 32 px on the right for the chevron. A `class` goes to the wrapper, not to the `<select>`.

**States.** Focus-visible, invalid (`[aria-invalid="true"]`) and disabled (`:disabled`, with the wrapper at 50% opacity) look as on `input`. In dark the ground is `input/30`, and `input/50` on hover.

**Tokens.** `native-select`, `native-select-sm`, `native-select-invalid`, `native-select-icon`, `native-select-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The browser gives the select its keys and its screen reader support. Connect the label with `for` and `id`. The browser gives the select the combobox role and the chosen option as its value, so a label with `for` alone keeps the value: the name is "Fruit" and the value "Apple" (measured in Chromium 148). When no value is chosen yet, the first option is empty and says what to choose, such as "Choose a country".

**Example.**

```svelte
<NativeSelect.Root id="country" bind:value={country}>
  <NativeSelect.Option value="">Choose a country</NativeSelect.Option>
  <NativeSelect.Option value="rs">Serbia</NativeSelect.Option>
</NativeSelect.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the select is 44 px high, and its text renders 16 px on a 22 px line (`native-select-phone`), so that iOS Safari does not zoom on focus. A tap opens the picker of the system.

### select

**Purpose.** A select picks one value from a list in a popup, with icons, groups and a check mark on the chosen item. Above seven options, use a list that the person can search: a `command` in a `popover`.

**Anatomy.** `Select.Trigger` (`select-trigger`) shows the chosen value or a placeholder in `Select.Value`, and a 16 px chevron in `muted-foreground`. `Select.Content` (`select-content`) is a `popover` layer at level 3 of `## Elevation & Depth`, at least 144 px wide and at least as wide as the trigger. It holds `Select.Item` rows, `Select.Group` with `Select.GroupHeading` or `Select.Label`, and `Select.Separator`. The chosen item shows a check mark on its right. `Select.Root` takes `items`, the list of the `value` and the `label` of each item, with `disabled: true` on a disabled item. Bits UI reads the label of the chosen value from `items`. So the trigger shows the label of a stored value before the list opens, and type-ahead works on the closed trigger. Without `items`, the trigger shows the raw value, such as "apple", until the list opens once, and type-ahead on the closed trigger does nothing (measured in Chromium 148, Bits UI 2.19.5).

**Variants.** None. The trigger is as wide as its content (`w-fit`), so a trigger in a form takes `w-full`.

**Sizes.** The trigger is 32 px high, or 28 px with `size="sm"` and the 6 px radius, with 10 px padding on the left and 8 px on the right. An item is 28 px high with the 6 px radius, 6 px padding on the left and 32 px on the right for the check mark. A group has 4 px padding, and its heading is `small` in `muted-foreground`.

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]` on the trigger and the content, which the `data-open:` and `data-closed:` variants match, together with `[data-open]` and `[data-closed]`. Placeholder: `[data-placeholder]` turns the trigger text `muted-foreground`. Highlighted: `[data-highlighted]` gives an item the `accent` ground, and selected (`[data-selected]`, `[aria-selected="true"]`) shows the check mark. Disabled (`[data-disabled]` on an item, `:disabled` on the trigger) draws at 50% opacity, and invalid (`[aria-invalid="true"]` on the trigger) looks as on `input`.

**Tokens.** `select-trigger`, `select-trigger-sm`, `select-trigger-placeholder`, `select-trigger-invalid`, `select-trigger-phone`, `select-content`, `select-label`, `select-item`, `select-item-highlighted`, `select-item-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI renders the trigger as a button with `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, and gives the list the listbox role, with the arrow keys, Home, End and type-ahead. The combobox role belongs to the input of a combobox, not to this trigger (Bits UI 2.19.5). Connect the label to the trigger with `for` and `id`, so that a click on the label focuses the trigger. Also give the label an `id`, and give the trigger `aria-labelledby` with the `id` of the label and its own `id`. The trigger is a button with no value of its own, and a label with `for` alone names it "Fruit", so the person does not hear the value. With both, the name is "Fruit Choose a fruit" before a choice and "Fruit Pear" after it, and Bits UI keeps the two attributes (measured in Chromium 148). An `aria-label` on the trigger also hides the value (measured).

**Example.**

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  const statuses = [
    { value: 'open', label: 'Open' },
    { value: 'done', label: 'Done' }
  ];
  let status = $state('open');
</script>

<Field.Field>
  <Field.Label id="status-label" for="status">Status</Field.Label>
  <Select.Root type="single" items={statuses} bind:value={status}>
    <Select.Trigger id="status" aria-labelledby="status-label status" class="w-full"><Select.Value placeholder="Choose a status" /></Select.Trigger>
    <Select.Content>
      {#each statuses as s (s.value)}
        <Select.Item value={s.value} label={s.label} />
      {/each}
    </Select.Content>
  </Select.Root>
</Field.Field>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the trigger and each item are 44 px high (`select-trigger-phone`, `select-item-phone`), and the text renders 15 px. The list stays a popup under the trigger.

### checkbox

**Purpose.** A check box turns one option on or off in a form that saves later, or selects a row in a list. For a setting that applies at once, use a `switch`.

**Anatomy.** One `checkbox` slot, a `<button>` of 16 by 16 px with a 1 px `input` edge and a fixed 4 px radius (`rounded-[4px]`). It holds a `checkbox-indicator`: a 14 px check, or a minus for the indeterminate state. In dark the ground is `input/30`. An invisible area adds 12 px at each side and 8 px above and below, inside the 1 px edge, so the pointer target is 38 by 30 px (measured).

**Variants.** None.

**Sizes.** One size, 16 px.

**States.** Checked: `[data-state="checked"]`, which Bits UI writes, and which the `data-checked:` variant matches, together with `[data-checked]`. A checked box takes the `primary` fill and edge with a `primary-foreground` check. Unchecked is `[data-state="unchecked"]`, and indeterminate is `[data-state="indeterminate"]` with a minus and no fill. Disabled (`:disabled`, `[data-disabled]`) draws at 50% opacity. Invalid (`[aria-invalid="true"]`) draws a `destructive` edge and a `destructive/20` halo, and a checked box keeps its `primary` edge. Read-only (`[data-readonly]`): the value shows and does not change, such as a box that the parent row of a tree sets (see `### table`). The `readonly` prop of Bits UI keeps the box in the tab order, and a click and Space do not change it (measured). Bits UI does not read `aria-disabled`. A box with `aria-disabled="true"` and no `readonly` changed on Space and on a click (measured). Nova draws no read-only state, so the part `HeldCheckbox` (`@stack-kit/stack/parts/HeldCheckbox`) draws it: the page gives it `checked` and `reason`, and no class. A checked read-only box takes the `muted-foreground` fill and edge and a `background` check (`checkbox-readonly`), at full opacity, and the pointer shows `not-allowed`. An unchecked read-only box keeps the look of an unchecked box, and the pointer shows `not-allowed`. So its state passes 3:1, and it differs from a box that the person sets. Print: the print drops the fill of a checked box, as it drops each ground. Its check had the color of the text on the fill, so it printed white on white, and the box printed empty (measured). So in print `stack.css` draws the check of a checked box in `foreground`, and a product adds no class. With the rule, each checked box of the tree of the preview printed its check, a read-only box too (measured on A4).

**Tokens.** `checkbox`, `checkbox-checked`, `checkbox-invalid`, `checkbox-readonly`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the box the checkbox role and `aria-checked`, which is `mixed` in the indeterminate state, and Space toggles it. Every box has a name: a `field-label` beside it, or an `aria-label` in a list row, such as "Select invoice 2026-041". Bits UI writes `aria-readonly="true"` on a read-only box, but Chromium 148 gives the checkbox role no read-only state (measured). So a box that a rule holds, such as a box that a parent row sets, also takes `aria-disabled="true"` and its reason. It takes the attributes of a button that is disabled with a reason (see `### button`). A tree shows no reason line on a touch screen (see `### table`). Chromium then gives it the state disabled, its value and the reason as its description (measured).

**Example.**

```svelte
<Field.Field orientation="horizontal">
  <Checkbox id="terms" bind:checked={terms} />
  <Field.Label for="terms">Accept the terms</Field.Label>
</Field.Field>

<!-- Read-only: a box that a rule holds, with its reason in a tooltip and in aria-describedby. -->
<HeldCheckbox checked reason="Set by Sales. Change it in the Sales row." aria-label="See Drafts" />
```

**Phone.** The box stays 16 px. Below 768 px its `field` row is 44 px high (`field-phone`), and the label of the row sets the box (see `field`). A box in a list row takes the height of the row, which is 52 px on a phone.

### radio-group

**Purpose.** A radio group picks one of two to seven options that the person sees at once. Above seven options, use a `select`. The one exception is the color picker of a label, whose eight discs fit in one row (see Variants).

**Anatomy.** The `radio-group` slot is a grid with an 8 px gap. Each `radio-group-item` is a round `<button>` of 16 px with a 1 px `input` edge. When checked, it takes a `primary` fill and shows its `radio-group-indicator`, an 8 px dot in `primary-foreground`. An invisible area inside the 1 px edge makes the pointer target 38 by 30 px (measured).

**Variants.** None. A choice card puts each item in a `field` inside a `field-label` (see `field`). A color picker is a radio group of discs in a row, one disc for each `tag-<color>` role. Each disc is an item with no edge, and its role is the fill at rest and when checked, in both modes. The picked disc hides the dot of the item and shows a check in `background` over it, so that the check reads as a cut-out. The Example gives the classes.

**Sizes.** One size, 16 px. In a `field-set` the group stands 12 px under its legend. A disc of a color picker is 16 px too, 8 px from the next disc, in a target of 24 px (`after:-inset-1`), so that two targets meet and do not overlap (measured). The check is 12 px (`size-3`), 2 px inside the disc (measured). A disc has no edge because the `::after` area starts at the padding box: with the 1 px edge, the same class gives a target of 22 px (measured).

**States.** Checked: `[data-state="checked"]`, which Bits UI writes, and which the `data-checked:` variant matches, together with `[data-checked]`. Unchecked is `[data-state="unchecked"]`. Disabled: `[data-disabled]` on the group or on an item, at 50% opacity. Invalid: `[aria-invalid="true"]` draws a `destructive` edge. A disc of a color picker has no edge, so focus-visible draws a 1 px `inset-ring` in `ring` in its place, inside the same 3 px halo (measured). A label always has a color, so a color picker has no invalid state.

**Tokens.** `radio-group-item`, `radio-group-item-checked`, `radio-group-item-invalid`. A disc of a color picker takes the tag role of its color as its fill (see Variants), so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the group the radiogroup role and each item the radio role, and the arrow keys move and check. Name the group with a `field-legend` in a `field-set`, or with an `aria-label`. A disc of a color picker has no visible label, so give each one the name of its color, such as `aria-label="Blue"`, and name the group, such as `aria-label="Color"`. The check is decorative (`aria-hidden="true"`).

**Example.**

```svelte
<RadioGroup.Root aria-label="Density" bind:value={density}>
  <Field.Field orientation="horizontal">
    <RadioGroup.Item value="compact" id="density-compact" />
    <Field.Label for="density-compact">Compact</Field.Label>
  </Field.Field>
</RadioGroup.Root>

<!-- A color picker. d.fill is written out for each color, so that Tailwind finds it,
     such as "bg-tag-blue data-checked:bg-tag-blue dark:bg-tag-blue dark:data-checked:bg-tag-blue". -->
<RadioGroup.Root aria-label="Color" orientation="horizontal" class="flex gap-2 max-md:gap-7" bind:value={color}>
  {#each discs as d (d.value)}
    <div class="relative flex">
      <RadioGroup.Item value={d.value} aria-label={d.name} class="{d.fill} border-0 focus-visible:inset-ring focus-visible:inset-ring-ring after:-inset-1 max-md:after:-inset-3.5 *:data-[slot=radio-group-indicator]:hidden" />
      {#if color === d.value}<CheckIcon class="pointer-events-none absolute inset-0.5 size-3 text-background" aria-hidden="true" />{/if}
    </div>
  {/each}
</RadioGroup.Root>
```

**Phone.** As on `checkbox`: the button stays 16 px, and its `field` row is 44 px high below 768 px (`field-phone`). A disc of a color picker stays 16 px, and below 768 px its target is 44 px (`max-md:after:-inset-3.5`) and the discs stand 28 px apart (`max-md:gap-7`), so that two targets meet and do not overlap (measured at 390 px).

### switch

**Purpose.** A switch turns a setting on or off, and the change applies at once. For a choice that saves with a form, use a `checkbox`.

**Anatomy.** The `switch` slot is a round track of 32 by 18.4 px with a round `switch-thumb` of 16 px. The track is `input` when off (`input/80` in dark) and `primary` when on. The thumb is `background` in light, and in dark it is `foreground` when off and `primary-foreground` when on. An invisible area makes the pointer target 56 by 34 px.

**Variants.** None. The on color is `primary`, so the theme decides it: near-black in `default` and indigo in `graphite`.

**Sizes.** `default` is 32 by 18.4 px with a 16 px thumb, and `sm` is 24 by 14 px with a 12 px thumb. Both are fixed px sizes that no token holds.

**States.** On and off: `[data-state="checked"]` and `[data-state="unchecked"]`, which Bits UI writes. The `data-checked:` variant also matches `[data-checked]`, but a switch never carries that attribute. Disabled (`[data-disabled]`) draws at 50% opacity, invalid (`[aria-invalid="true"]`) draws a `destructive` edge, and focus-visible adds the `ring/50` halo.

**Tokens.** `switch`, `switch-unchecked`, `switch-checked`, `switch-invalid`, `switch-thumb`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the switch the switch role and `aria-checked`, and Space and Enter toggle it. The label names the setting, never the state, such as "Email me about mentions".

**Example.**

```svelte
<Field.Field orientation="horizontal">
  <Field.Content>
    <Field.Label for="mentions">Email me about mentions</Field.Label>
    <Field.Description>At most one email each hour.</Field.Description>
  </Field.Content>
  <Switch id="mentions" bind:checked={mentions} />
</Field.Field>
```

**Phone.** The switch keeps its size. Below 768 px its `field` row is 44 px high (`field-phone`), and the label of the row sets it (see `field`).

### slider

**Purpose.** A slider sets a number in a range by drag, where the position matters more than the exact value, such as a zoom level. Where the exact value matters, add a number `input` beside it.

**Anatomy.** The `slider` slot holds a `slider-track`, a 4 px `muted` bar with round ends, and a `slider-range` in `primary` for the chosen part. Each value has a `slider-thumb`: a 12 px white circle (`bg-white`, in both modes) with a 1 px `ring` edge. An invisible area of 8 px around the thumb makes its pointer target 28 px.

**Variants.** `orientation="horizontal"`, the default, fills its width, and `orientation="vertical"` is at least 160 px high. `type="multiple"` with two values makes a range with two thumbs.

**Sizes.** One size: a 4 px track and a 12 px thumb. The root is 4 px high, and the thumb overhangs it.

**States.** Hover, pressed (`:active`) and focus-visible draw the `ring/50` halo around the thumb, and Bits UI writes `[data-active]` on the thumb that the pointer moves. Disabled: `[data-disabled]` on the root, at 50% opacity. The slider has no invalid state.

**Tokens.** `slider-track`, `slider-range`, `slider-thumb`, `slider-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives each thumb the slider role with `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax`. The arrow keys step, and Home and End jump to the ends. The vendored slider gives a thumb no name, so show the label and the value as text next to the slider.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Slider type="single" min={50} max={200} step={10} bind:value={zoom} />
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the root is 44 px high (`slider-phone`), and a tap anywhere on that strip moves the thumb. The track and the thumb keep their size.

### dialog

**Purpose.** A dialog holds one task that ends in one step, in a layer that blocks the page. The task is a confirmation, one create form, or a list that the person checks before one action. All other work goes into a pane, a sheet or a page. A long form is a page when it has more than one step, such as an import, or when it edits a record that has a page. A form of fields whose values change the page behind it, such as a filter with a date range, is a `sheet`, so that the person sees the page while they fill the form. A filter of a few values stays a `dropdown-menu` in the top bar. A confirmation of an action with a consequence uses `alert-dialog`.

**Anatomy.** `Dialog.Trigger` (`dialog-trigger`) opens `Dialog.Content` (`dialog-content`), a `popover` layer at level 4 of `## Elevation & Depth` over the `dialog-overlay` scrim, which is `bg-black/10` with a small blur. The content is a grid with 16 px padding and 16 px between its parts, a `foreground/10` ring and the 8 px radius. It holds a `dialog-header` with the `dialog-title` and the `dialog-description` 8 px apart, the body, a `dialog-footer`, and a ghost close button of 28 px (`dialog-close`) 8 px from the top right corner.

**Variants.** `showCloseButton={false}` on the content removes the close button, and `showCloseButton` on the footer adds an outline Close button there. The footer is a band of `muted` at 50% with a `border` edge on top, and it reaches the edges of the dialog. It puts its buttons on the right, 8 px apart, with the primary last.

**Sizes.** From 640 px the dialog is at most 384 px wide (`sm:max-w-sm`), and below 640 px it is 32 px narrower than the screen. A dialog that holds a list or a long form is wide: at most 640 px from 640 px (`sm:max-w-160`), the width of a settings form (`spacing.settings-form`). A long form has a field that needs a wide line, such as a text area, a code block or a row of chips. No dialog is wider than 640 px. The body of a wide dialog scrolls between the header and the footer, and from 768 px the dialog is at most 11/12 of the window high (`md:max-h-11/12`). For this, the content takes `flex flex-col` in place of the grid of nova, and the body takes `-mx-4 min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto px-4`, which puts its scroll bar at the edge of the dialog. The title is 16 px at 500 with a line height of 1 (`leading-none`). The description and the body are `body`, and the description is `muted-foreground`.

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]` on the trigger, the overlay, the content, the title, the description and the close button, which the `data-open:` and `data-closed:` variants match. The trigger also carries `aria-expanded`. The content fades in and zooms from 95% in 100 ms, and it is not in the page while it is closed. While the action of the dialog runs, set `escapeKeydownBehavior="ignore"` and `interactOutsideBehavior="ignore"` on the content, so that Esc and a click outside wait for the server.

**Tokens.** `dialog-content`, `dialog-title`, `dialog-description`, `dialog-trigger-phone`, `dialog-close-phone`. The scrim is black at 10% and the footer band is muted at 50%, so neither has a key.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the content the dialog role with `aria-modal="true"`, and names it with the title and the description. Focus moves into the dialog, stays in it, and returns to the trigger when the dialog closes. Esc and a click outside close it, except an Esc from inside a `code-editor` that a person can edit (see `### code-editor`). The primary button repeats the verb of the button that opened the dialog.

**Example.** A dialog of one form is the part `FormDialog` (`@stack-kit/stack/parts/FormDialog`), which holds the form screen of Phone, the scroll of the body in a dialog at most 11/12 of the window high from 768 px, the focus in the first field and, with `runKey`, Cmd+Enter. The page gives it the texts, `onsubmit` and the fields, and no class. Its root is the content of the dialog, so the page gives the part `escapeKeydownBehavior` and `interactOutsideBehavior` while the action runs, as States says.

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import FormDialog from '@stack-kit/stack/parts/FormDialog';
  import { Button } from '@stack-kit/stack/ui/button';
  import * as Field from '@stack-kit/stack/ui/field';
  import { Input } from '@stack-kit/stack/ui/input';

  let open = $state(false);
  let name = $state('');

  function create(e: SubmitEvent) {
    e.preventDefault();
    // The product saves name.trim() here, then closes the dialog.
    open = false;
  }
</script>

<Button variant="outline" onclick={() => (open = true)}>Create project</Button>
<!-- The first field is a text field, so below 768 px the dialog is a form screen (see Phone). -->
<FormDialog bind:open id="project-form" title="Create project" description="The name shows in the sidebar." action="Create project" short="Create" disabled={!name.trim()} runKey onsubmit={create}>
  <Field.Field>
    <Field.Label for="project-name">Name</Field.Label>
    <Input id="project-name" bind:value={name} />
  </Field.Field>
</FormDialog>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a dialog fills the screen, as `## Layout` says, and nova does not do that by itself: below 640 px it draws a box 32 px narrower than the screen in the middle.
