So `stack.css` draws each `dialog-content` at the full width and height of the screen below 768 px, with no radius and with its content at the top, and leaves it as it is from 768 px (measured at 390, 700 and 1280 px). The page gives the content no class for this. The rule comes after the utilities of Tailwind, so a width class of the content, such as `sm:max-w-160`, does not change it. A dialog that fills the screen keeps all four safe-area insets of `## Layout`. `stack.css` writes them as a transparent border, not as padding, so that the padding of the dialog stays: 16 px, and 0 in the command palette. The ground of the dialog fills the border, and the content, the close button and the footer stay inside the safe area (measured in Chromium at 390 px with emulated insets, with no change at 1280 px; not measured on a device). Below 640 px the footer stacks its buttons at full width with the primary on top, and each one is 44 px high (`button-phone`). The trigger and the close button are 44 by 44 px or more (`dialog-trigger-phone`, `dialog-close-phone`). Below 768 px a dialog whose first field is a text field or a text area is a form screen, at either width. The part `FormDialog` makes the form screen and holds its classes, so the page gives it no class. A plain dialog does not become a form screen. The form screen puts the focus in that field when it opens (`onOpenAutoFocus`), because Cancel comes first in the code. The keyboard of the phone opens with it and covers the bottom of the screen (not measured on a device), so the actions go into the header, which stays in view. The header then reaches the edges of the dialog and is 52 px high, as the header of band 1, with a `border` edge below. Its classes are `max-md:-mx-4 max-md:-mt-4 max-md:h-13 max-md:shrink-0 max-md:flex-row max-md:items-center max-md:gap-1 max-md:border-b max-md:border-border max-md:px-1`. It holds Cancel at the start as a `ghost` button (`md:hidden`), then the title (`max-md:min-w-0 max-md:flex-1 max-md:truncate`), then the primary action at the end (`size="sm"`, `md:hidden`). The two buttons are 44 px high (`button-phone`). The description stays for a screen reader only (`max-md:sr-only`), and the footer does not show (`max-md:hidden`). The dialog has no close button (`showCloseButton={false}`), so from 768 px its footer holds Cancel before the primary action.

### alert-dialog

**Purpose.** An alert dialog asks the person to confirm an action with a consequence, such as a delete or the issue of a document. It blocks the page until the person confirms or cancels. Its title names the action and the object, such as "Delete the project Atlas?", and its action button repeats the verb. A confirmation that a person opens from a link in an email, with no session, is a page with the same title, facts and action (see `### Page outside the shell`).

**Anatomy.** `AlertDialog.Content` (`alert-dialog-content`) is a `popover` layer at level 4 over the `alert-dialog-overlay` scrim, with 16 px padding, a `foreground/10` ring and the 8 px radius. Its `alert-dialog-header` holds an optional `alert-dialog-media`, the `alert-dialog-title` and the `alert-dialog-description`. The media is a 40 px `muted` box with the 6 px radius and a 24 px icon. The `alert-dialog-footer` is the footer band of `dialog`, with `AlertDialog.Cancel` (`alert-dialog-cancel`, an outline button) and `AlertDialog.Action` (`alert-dialog-action`, a default button).

**Variants.** `size="default"` is at most 384 px wide from 640 px and 320 px wide below that. `size="sm"` is at most 320 px wide, and its footer puts the two buttons side by side at equal width. A destructive confirmation gives the action `variant="destructive"`, which is `destructive` text on its 10% tint. A summary of the facts goes between the header and the footer: a grid with a 112 px label column (`spacing.property-label`, `w-28`) in `muted-foreground` and rows of at least 28 px (`min-h-7`). The summary is a `<dl>`. Each row is a `<div class="flex min-h-7 gap-2 py-1">` that holds a `<dt class="w-28 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground">` and a `<dd class="min-w-0">`, so a long value wraps beside its label. A row of one line is then 28 px high (measured at 1280 and 390 px).

**Sizes.** The title is 16 px at 500 on a 22 px line, and the description is `body` in `muted-foreground`. The header centers its text below 640 px and always in `sm`. From 640 px the default size aligns the text to the left and puts the media beside the title. The buttons are 32 px high.

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]`, as on `dialog`. Cancel closes the dialog, and the action does not: the product runs the action, then sets `open` to false. Working: the action shows a `spinner` and keeps its label and width (`[data-loading]`, `aria-busy="true"`), and the content sets `escapeKeydownBehavior="ignore"` until the server answers. Blocked: the action is disabled with a reason, as `### button` says, and each blocker shows with an icon, the rule, the fix and a button to the field. Warnings: each warning has a check box, and the action stays disabled with a reason until all are checked. In both states a line above the footer names the reason, such as "Check the 2 warnings to delete." The action takes `aria-disabled="true"` and not `disabled`, so that it stays in the tab order of the dialog after Cancel. Its `aria-describedby` points to the line, which gives it the description (measured in Chromium 148). The line shows, so the action needs no tooltip. Clean: one line with a `success` icon. Failed: the dialog stays open with its facts, and a line in `destructive` names the retry. Done: the dialog closes, and the page shows the result in one line, with no toast.

**Tokens.** `alert-dialog-content`, `alert-dialog-title`, `alert-dialog-description`, `alert-dialog-media`, `alert-dialog-trigger-phone`, `alert-dialog-action-phone`, `alert-dialog-cancel-phone`. The two buttons take the keys of `button` for their variant.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the content the alertdialog role with `aria-modal="true"`, and moves the focus into it. Esc cancels, except an Esc from inside a `code-editor` that a person can edit (see `### code-editor`), and a click outside does nothing (`interactOutsideBehavior="ignore"`). The focus returns to the trigger when the dialog closes.

**Example.**

```svelte
<AlertDialog.Root bind:open>
  <AlertDialog.Trigger class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'destructive' })}>Delete project</AlertDialog.Trigger>
  <AlertDialog.Content>
    <AlertDialog.Header>
      <AlertDialog.Title>Delete the project Atlas?</AlertDialog.Title>
      <AlertDialog.Description>This deletes the project and its 12 files. It cannot be undone.</AlertDialog.Description>
    </AlertDialog.Header>
    <AlertDialog.Footer>
      <AlertDialog.Cancel>Cancel</AlertDialog.Cancel>
      <AlertDialog.Action variant="destructive" onclick={remove}>Delete project</AlertDialog.Action>
    </AlertDialog.Footer>
  </AlertDialog.Content>
</AlertDialog.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px `stack.css` draws an alert dialog of either size as it draws a `dialog`: it fills the screen and keeps the four safe-area insets, and the page gives the content no class for this (measured at 390 and 700 px with emulated insets, with no change at 1280 px). Below 640 px the footer stacks the action above Cancel at full width, and `sm` keeps them side by side. The trigger, the action and Cancel are 44 px high or more (`alert-dialog-trigger-phone`, `alert-dialog-action-phone`, `alert-dialog-cancel-phone`), because they are not `button` slots.

### sheet

**Purpose.** A sheet is a modal layer that slides in from an edge, for a task that keeps the page in view, such as a filter panel. The detail pane of `## Layout` is not a sheet, because the pane is not modal. It only takes the look of the sheet.

**Anatomy.** `Sheet.Content` (`sheet-content`) is a `popover` layer with a `border` edge on its inner side and `shadow-lg`, over the `sheet-overlay` scrim. It holds a `sheet-header` with 16 px padding, in which the `sheet-title` and the `sheet-description` are 2 px apart, then the body, then a `sheet-footer` with 16 px padding at the bottom. The footer stacks its buttons at full width, 8 px apart. A ghost close button of 28 px (`sheet-close`) sits 12 px from the top right corner.

**Variants.** `side="right"` is the default, and `left`, `top` and `bottom` also exist. A side sheet takes the full height and 75% of the width, at most 384 px from 640 px. A top or bottom sheet takes the full width and the height of its content. The corners stay square.

**Sizes.** The title is 16 px at 500 on a 22 px line in `foreground`, and the description is `body` in `muted-foreground`. The content has no padding, so the body sets its own 16 px at the sides (`px-4`).

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]` on the trigger, the overlay, the content and the close button, which the `data-open:` and `data-closed:` variants match. `[data-side]` on the content names its side. The content fades in and slides 40 px in from its side in 200 ms, and the scrim fades in 100 ms.

**Tokens.** `sheet-content`, `sheet-title`, `sheet-description`, `sheet-trigger-phone`, `sheet-close-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the content the dialog role with `aria-modal="true"`, keeps the focus in it and returns the focus to the trigger when it closes. Esc and a click on the scrim close the sheet, except an Esc from inside a `code-editor` that a person can edit (see `### code-editor`). Every sheet has a title, and a design that hides it keeps it for a screen reader (`class="sr-only"`).

**Example.**

```svelte
<Sheet.Root bind:open>
  <Sheet.Trigger class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'outline', size: 'sm' })}>Filter</Sheet.Trigger>
  <Sheet.Content side="right">
    <Sheet.Header>
      <Sheet.Title>Filter</Sheet.Title>
      <Sheet.Description>Show the invoices that match.</Sheet.Description>
    </Sheet.Header>
    <div class="flex flex-col gap-5 px-4"><!-- fields --></div>
    <Sheet.Footer>
      <Button onclick={apply}>Apply the filter</Button>
      <Sheet.Close class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'outline' })}>Cancel</Sheet.Close>
    </Sheet.Footer>
  </Sheet.Content>
</Sheet.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 640 px a side sheet takes 75% of the width, 292 px at a width of 390 px (measured). The trigger and both close buttons are 44 px or more (`sheet-trigger-phone`, `sheet-close-phone`), and the buttons of the footer are 44 px high (`button-phone`). The More tab of `## Layout` opens the sheet that the nova sidebar draws. A sheet that holds a text field puts its actions in its header, as the form screen of `### dialog`.

### drawer

**Purpose.** A drawer is the bottom sheet of a phone: a modal layer that rises from the bottom edge, and that the person can drag down to close. Below 768 px a menu opens in a drawer (see `dropdown-menu`). On a desktop, use a `dialog`, a `sheet` or a `popover` instead.

**Anatomy.** `Drawer.Content` (`drawer-content`) is a `popover` layer with the 8 px radius on its inner corners and a `border` edge on its inner side, over the `drawer-overlay` scrim. A bottom drawer shows a handle of 100 by 4 px in `muted`, 16 px under its top edge. The content holds a `drawer-header` with 16 px padding, which holds the `drawer-title` and the `drawer-description`, then the body, then a `drawer-footer` with 16 px padding, which stacks its buttons at full width.

**Variants.** `direction` on the root sets the edge: `bottom` is the default, and `top`, `left` and `right` also exist. A bottom or top drawer takes the full width and at most 80% of the height. A left or right drawer takes 75% of the width, at most 384 px from 640 px. The header of a bottom or top drawer centers its text.

**Sizes.** The title is 16 px at 500 on a 22 px line in `foreground`, and the description is `body` in `muted-foreground`. The content has no padding, so the body sets its own 16 px at the sides (`px-4`).

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]` on the trigger, the overlay, the content and the close button. `[data-vaul-drawer-direction]` on the content names the edge. While the person drags, the drawer follows the finger, and a drag past a threshold closes it.

**Tokens.** `drawer-content`, `drawer-title`, `drawer-description`, `drawer-trigger-phone`, `drawer-close-phone`. The handle is a muted bar with no key.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the content the dialog role with `aria-modal="true"`, keeps the focus in it and returns the focus to the trigger. Esc and a tap on the scrim close it, except an Esc from inside a `code-editor` that a person can edit (see `### code-editor`). A drag is never the only way to close a drawer: it also holds a Close or Cancel button.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Drawer.Root>
  <Drawer.Trigger class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'ghost', size: 'icon' })} aria-label="More actions"><EllipsisIcon /></Drawer.Trigger>
  <Drawer.Content>
    <Drawer.Header><Drawer.Title>Invoice 2026-041</Drawer.Title></Drawer.Header>
    <div class="flex flex-col gap-1 px-4 pb-4">
      <Button variant="ghost" class="justify-start">Duplicate</Button>
      <Button variant="destructive" class="justify-start">Cancel the invoice</Button>
    </div>
  </Drawer.Content>
</Drawer.Root>
```

**Phone.** At a width of 390 px a bottom drawer takes the full width, and the height of its content up to 80% of the screen (measured). The trigger and a close button are 44 by 44 px or more (`drawer-trigger-phone`, `drawer-close-phone`), and each button inside is 44 px high (`button-phone`).

### popover

**Purpose.** A popover shows a small form or extra content next to its trigger, such as a date picker, a quick edit of one value or a list to search. It does not block the page, and a click outside closes it. A list of actions is a `dropdown-menu`, not a popover.

**Anatomy.** `Popover.Trigger` (`popover-trigger`) opens `Popover.Content` (`popover-content`), a `popover` layer at level 3 of `## Elevation & Depth`. It has 10 px padding, 10 px between its parts, a `foreground/10` ring, `shadow-md` and the 8 px radius. An optional `popover-header` holds the `popover-title` in `body` at 500 and the `popover-description` in `muted-foreground`, 2 px apart.

**Variants.** `side` places the content, `bottom` by default, 4 px from the trigger, and `align` aligns it with the trigger, `center` by default. When the side has no room, the content flips to the other side. A width class on `Popover.Content`, such as `w-80`, changes the width.

**Sizes.** The content is 288 px wide (`w-72`) in `body`, and as high as what it holds.

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]` on the trigger and the content, which the `data-open:` and `data-closed:` variants match, and `aria-expanded` on the trigger. `[data-side]` and `[data-align]` on the content name its place. The content fades in, zooms from 95% and moves 8 px from its side in 100 ms.

**Tokens.** `popover-content`, `popover-title`, `popover-description`, `popover-trigger-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the trigger `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded`. It moves the focus into the content when the popover opens, keeps it there, and returns it to the trigger when the popover closes. Esc and a click outside close the popover, except an Esc from inside a `code-editor` that a person can edit (see `### code-editor`). Bits UI 2.19 gives the content no role and no name, so the trigger promises a dialog that a screen reader does not find. So the page passes both, and the nova content gives them to Bits UI: `role="dialog"` on `Popover.Content`, and `aria-labelledby` with the `id` of its `popover-title`. A content with no title takes an `aria-label` that names it, such as "Pick a due date".

**Example.**

```svelte
<Popover.Root>
  <Popover.Trigger class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'outline', size: 'sm' })}>Due date</Popover.Trigger>
  <Popover.Content align="start" role="dialog" aria-labelledby="due-date-title">
    <Popover.Header>
      <Popover.Title id="due-date-title">Due date</Popover.Title>
      <Popover.Description>The invoice is due on this day.</Popover.Description>
    </Popover.Header>
    <Input type="date" aria-label="Due date" bind:value={due} />
  </Popover.Content>
</Popover.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the popover stays a layer next to its trigger, 288 px wide, which fits a screen of 390 px. The trigger is 44 by 44 px or more (`popover-trigger-phone`).

### hover-card

**Purpose.** A hover card previews the object behind a link, such as a person or a document, while the pointer rests on the link. It adds information and is never the only path to it: the link opens the object. It holds no action.

**Anatomy.** `HoverCard.Trigger` (`hover-card-trigger`) is the link, an `<a>` with an `href`. `HoverCard.Content` (`hover-card-content`) is a `popover` layer at level 3 with 10 px padding, a `foreground/10` ring, `shadow-md` and the 8 px radius.

**Variants.** `side` is `bottom` by default and `align` is `center`, 4 px from the link. The content holds what the product needs, such as an `avatar`, a name at 500 and one line in `small` and `muted-foreground`.

**Sizes.** The content is 256 px wide (`w-64`) in `body`.

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]` on the link and the content, and `aria-expanded` on the link. The card opens 700 ms after the pointer enters the link, and closes 300 ms after the pointer leaves the link and the card (`openDelay` and `closeDelay`). Keyboard focus on the link (`:focus-visible`) also opens it.

**Tokens.** `hover-card-content`, `hover-card-trigger-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the link `role="button"`, `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded`, so a screen reader announces a button. The link opens the same information, so a person who never sees the card loses nothing.

**Example.**

```svelte
<HoverCard.Root>
  <HoverCard.Trigger href="/people/ana">Ana Novak</HoverCard.Trigger>
  <HoverCard.Content>
    <p class="font-medium">Ana Novak</p>
    <p class="text-xs text-muted-foreground">Accounts, since March 2026</p>
  </HoverCard.Content>
</HoverCard.Root>
```

**Phone.** A phone has no hover, and Bits UI ignores a touch on the link, so the card does not open on a phone and a tap follows the link. A link that is a box, such as a flex item, is 44 by 44 px or more (`hover-card-trigger-phone`). A link in running text stays one line, because an inline box takes no minimum height (measured: 19 px high at a width of 390 px).

### tooltip

**Purpose.** A tooltip names a control that shows only an icon, together with its key, such as "Archive E". It also shows the full text of a label that ends in an ellipsis. It also gives the reason of an action that is disabled with a reason, and a line with the same text is the description of the control (see `### button`). It holds text only: never an action, a link or the only copy of information.

**Anatomy.** `Tooltip.Provider` wraps the app once. `Tooltip.Trigger` (`tooltip-trigger`) wraps the control, and `Tooltip.Content` (`tooltip-content`) is a label with `background` text on a `foreground` ground, the 6 px radius and an arrow of 10 px. It has 12 px padding at the sides and 6 px at the top and bottom, and a `kbd` inside it keeps 6 px from the right edge.

**Variants.** `side` is `top` by default, and `bottom`, `left` and `right` also exist. The arrow points at the trigger, and the label touches it with no gap.

**Sizes.** The text is `small`, and the label is at most 320 px wide (`max-w-xs`), so a long text wraps.

**States.** Open: `[data-state="instant-open"]`, or `[data-state="delayed-open"]` after a delay, on the content. Closed: `[data-state="closed"]`. Nova sets no delay on the provider (`delayDuration={0}`), so the label opens at once on hover and on keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`), and it fades in and zooms from 95%.

**Tokens.** `tooltip-content`, `tooltip-trigger-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The control has its own name, such as `aria-label="Archive"`, and the tooltip repeats it, so a screen reader never depends on the tooltip. While the label shows, Bits UI points the `aria-describedby` of the trigger at it. Esc closes the label.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Tooltip.Root>
  <Tooltip.Trigger class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'ghost', size: 'icon-sm' })} aria-label="Archive">
    <ArchiveIcon />
  </Tooltip.Trigger>
  <Tooltip.Content>Archive <Kbd>E</Kbd></Tooltip.Content>
</Tooltip.Root>
```

**Phone.** A phone has no hover, and Bits UI ignores a touch on the trigger, so a tooltip does not show on a phone. The trigger is 44 by 44 px or more (`tooltip-trigger-phone`), and its text takes the 15 px `body` step. The key also reaches a sidebar menu button with a tooltip, because that button renders the `tooltip-trigger` slot.

### dropdown-menu

**Purpose.** A dropdown menu lists the actions or the options of one object or view behind a button, such as the More menu of a row or the display options of a list. Each item is a verb or an option, and a destructive item comes last, after a separator. A list of more than seven items that the person searches is a `command` in a `popover`.

**Anatomy.** `DropdownMenu.Content` (`dropdown-menu-content`) is a `popover` layer at level 3 with 4 px padding, a `foreground/10` ring, `shadow-md` and the 8 px radius. It holds `dropdown-menu-item` rows, `dropdown-menu-checkbox-item` and `dropdown-menu-radio-item` rows with a check at the right, `dropdown-menu-label` and `dropdown-menu-group-heading` captions, and `dropdown-menu-separator` lines. A `dropdown-menu-sub-trigger` row opens a `dropdown-menu-sub-content`, which has `shadow-lg`. An item holds an optional 16 px icon, the label and an optional `dropdown-menu-shortcut` at the right.

**Variants.** `variant="destructive"` on an item draws its text and icon in `destructive`, and its highlight is `destructive` at 10% (20% in dark). `inset` indents an item or a caption by 28 px, so that it aligns with the items that have an icon. The content is at least 128 px wide and as wide as its trigger, so the page gives it a width class from 200 to 320 px, such as `w-56` (224 px).

**Sizes.** An item is 28 px high with the 6 px radius, 6 px padding at the sides and 6 px between the icon and the label, in `body`. A check box or radio item keeps 32 px at its right for the check. A caption is `small` at 500 in `muted-foreground`, and a shortcut is `small` in `muted-foreground` with wide letter spacing (0.1em). An item that a rule blocks holds a second line for its reason, and it is 44 px high (measured).

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]` on the trigger, the content and a sub-trigger, and `aria-expanded` on the trigger. Highlighted: Bits UI writes `[data-highlighted]` and moves the focus to the item, and nova styles `:focus`: the `accent` ground, `accent-foreground` text and an `accent-foreground` shortcut. Checked: `[data-state="checked"]` with `aria-checked="true"` on a check box or radio item. Disabled: `disabled` writes `[data-disabled]` with `aria-disabled="true"` and draws the item at 50% opacity, and the arrow keys skip it (measured). It serves an item with no reason to give. Disabled with a reason: an item that a rule blocks stays reachable with the arrow keys and runs nothing, so it does not take `disabled`. Its `onSelect` calls `preventDefault()`, so Enter and a click keep the menu open (measured). Its label draws at 50% opacity, and a second line in `small` and `muted-foreground` at full opacity gives the reason, which turns `accent-foreground` with the label while the item is highlighted (measured). The part `ReasonMenuItem` (`@stack-kit/stack/parts/ReasonMenuItem`) is such an item: the page gives it `label`, `onSelect` and `reason`, a sentence while the rule blocks and `undefined` while the item may run, and no class. An open sub-trigger keeps the `accent` ground.

**Tokens.** `dropdown-menu-content`, `dropdown-menu-label`, `dropdown-menu-item`, `dropdown-menu-item-destructive`, `dropdown-menu-item-highlighted`, `dropdown-menu-shortcut`, `dropdown-menu-trigger-phone`, `dropdown-menu-item-phone`, `dropdown-menu-checkbox-item-phone`, `dropdown-menu-radio-item-phone`, `dropdown-menu-sub-trigger-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the content the menu role and each item the menuitem, menuitemcheckbox or menuitemradio role. The arrow keys move, Enter and Space pick, a letter jumps to the next item that starts with it, and Esc closes the menu and returns the focus to the trigger. An icon-only trigger has an `aria-label`, such as "More actions". An item that is disabled with a reason carries `aria-disabled="true"`, `aria-labelledby` with the `id` of its label and `aria-describedby` with the `id` of its reason line. So its name is the label alone and its description is the reason. Bits UI writes `aria-disabled="false"` over an `aria-disabled` prop of an item that is not `disabled` (measured), so the `child` snippet of the item sets the three attributes after the props. Chromium then gives the item the menuitem role, the name "Blocked item", the state disabled and the description "Close the 3 open tasks first." (measured in Chromium 148).

**Example.**

```svelte
<DropdownMenu.Root>
  <DropdownMenu.Trigger class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'ghost', size: 'icon' })} aria-label="More actions"><EllipsisIcon /></DropdownMenu.Trigger>
  <DropdownMenu.Content align="end" class="w-56">
    <DropdownMenu.Item><CopyIcon />Duplicate<DropdownMenu.Shortcut>⌘D</DropdownMenu.Shortcut></DropdownMenu.Item>
    <!-- A rule blocks Archive: reachable with the arrow keys, runs nothing, and gives its reason. -->
    <DropdownMenu.Item onSelect={(e) => e.preventDefault()}>
      {#snippet child({ props })}
        <div {...props} aria-disabled="true" aria-labelledby="archive-label" aria-describedby="archive-reason">
          <span class="flex flex-col">
            <span id="archive-label" class="opacity-50">Archive</span>
            <span id="archive-reason" class="text-xs text-muted-foreground">Close the 3 open tasks first.</span>
          </span>
        </div>
      {/snippet}
    </DropdownMenu.Item>
    <DropdownMenu.Separator />
    <DropdownMenu.Item variant="destructive"><TrashIcon />Delete</DropdownMenu.Item>
  </DropdownMenu.Content>
</DropdownMenu.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a menu opens as a `drawer`, as `## Layout` says, and nova does not do that by itself. So render the items as ghost `button` rows in a drawer while `new ScreenQuery('min-width: 768px')` does not match (see `### A screen page in print`), as the example of `drawer` shows. Each row is then 44 px high (`button-phone`). A dropdown menu that stays on a phone has a trigger of 44 by 44 px or more and items of 44 px (`dropdown-menu-trigger-phone`, `dropdown-menu-item-phone`, `dropdown-menu-checkbox-item-phone`, `dropdown-menu-radio-item-phone`, `dropdown-menu-sub-trigger-phone`), and its text takes the 15 px `body` step. A page that is wider than the screen moves under an open menu, because of the scroll lock of Bits UI (see "Page width" in `### Width bands`).

### context-menu

**Purpose.** A context menu repeats the actions of an object on a right click, such as the actions of a row. It is a shortcut, and never the only path: each action in it is also in a menu that a button opens.

**Anatomy.** `ContextMenu.Trigger` (`context-menu-trigger`) is the area that takes the right click, and `ContextMenu.Content` (`context-menu-content`) opens at the pointer. The content and its parts are those of `dropdown-menu` under the `context-menu-` slot names: items, check box and radio items, captions, separators, shortcuts, and a sub-trigger that opens a `context-menu-sub-content`.

**Variants.** `variant="destructive"` and `inset` on an item are as on `dropdown-menu`. The content is at least 144 px wide, so the page gives it a width class, such as `w-56`.

**Sizes.** As on `dropdown-menu`: items of 28 px with the 6 px radius in `body`, and captions and shortcuts in `small` and `muted-foreground`. The sub-content draws a `border` edge in place of the ring.

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` and `[data-state="closed"]` on the trigger area and the content. Highlighted (`[data-highlighted]` with `:focus`), checked (`[data-state="checked"]`) and disabled (`[data-disabled]`) look as on `dropdown-menu`. An item that a rule blocks takes the form of `dropdown-menu`, with its reason as a second line, because Bits UI gives the two menus the same item.

**Tokens.** `context-menu-content`, `context-menu-label`, `context-menu-item`, `context-menu-item-destructive`, `context-menu-item-highlighted`, `context-menu-shortcut`, `context-menu-item-phone`, `context-menu-checkbox-item-phone`, `context-menu-radio-item-phone`, `context-menu-sub-trigger-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The keys inside the menu are those of `dropdown-menu`. A right click is hidden from view and has no key on most keyboards, so the same actions sit in a visible menu.

**Example.**

```svelte
<ContextMenu.Root>
  <ContextMenu.Trigger>{@render row(invoice)}</ContextMenu.Trigger>
  <ContextMenu.Content class="w-56">
    <ContextMenu.Item>Duplicate<ContextMenu.Shortcut>⌘D</ContextMenu.Shortcut></ContextMenu.Item>
    <ContextMenu.Separator />
    <ContextMenu.Item variant="destructive">Delete</ContextMenu.Item>
  </ContextMenu.Content>
</ContextMenu.Root>
```

**Phone.** A phone has no right click, and Bits UI opens the menu after a press of 700 ms on the trigger. The same actions sit in the More menu of the object, which opens as a `drawer` below 768 px. Each item of an open context menu is 44 px high (`context-menu-item-phone`, `context-menu-checkbox-item-phone`, `context-menu-radio-item-phone`, `context-menu-sub-trigger-phone`).

### menubar

**Purpose.** A menubar holds the menus of an editor in one bar, such as File, Edit and View. A web app rarely needs one, because a toolbar of buttons and one `dropdown-menu` serve most views.

**Anatomy.** The `menubar` slot is a bar with a `border` edge, the 8 px radius and 3 px padding. It holds `menubar-trigger` buttons in `body` at 500 with the 4 px radius. Each trigger opens a `menubar-content` 8 px under the bar, with the parts of `dropdown-menu` under the `menubar-` slot names.

**Variants.** `variant="destructive"` and `inset` on an item are as on `dropdown-menu`. In a check box or radio item the check stands at the left, in an indent of 28 px. A caption is `body` at 500, and it is not muted.

**Sizes.** The bar is 32 px high, and a trigger is 24 px high with 6 px padding at the sides. The content is at least 144 px wide, and its items are 28 px high with the 6 px radius.

**States.** Open: the trigger carries `[data-state="open"]` and `aria-expanded="true"`, and it takes the `muted` ground, as it does on hover. Highlighted, checked and disabled items look as on `dropdown-menu` (`[data-highlighted]`, `[data-state="checked"]`, `[data-disabled]`).

**Tokens.** `menubar`, `menubar-trigger`, `menubar-trigger-open`, `menubar-content`, `menubar-item`, `menubar-item-destructive`, `menubar-item-highlighted`, `menubar-shortcut`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the bar the menubar role and each trigger the menuitem role. Only one trigger is in the tab order, and the left and right arrow keys move between the triggers. The keys inside a menu are those of `dropdown-menu`.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Menubar.Root class="max-md:hidden">
  <Menubar.Menu>
    <Menubar.Trigger>File</Menubar.Trigger>
    <Menubar.Content>
      <Menubar.Item>New document<Menubar.Shortcut>⌘N</Menubar.Shortcut></Menubar.Item>
      <Menubar.Separator />
      <Menubar.Item variant="destructive">Close</Menubar.Item>
    </Menubar.Content>
  </Menubar.Menu>
</Menubar.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the bar does not show (`class="max-md:hidden"`, measured), and its menus move into the More menu of the screen, which opens as a `drawer` (see `dropdown-menu`). For this reason the bar, its triggers of 24 px and its items have no phone key.

### command

**Purpose.** The command palette finds and runs any action or page of the app by its name. It opens with ⌘K and from the search in the sidebar head (see `## Layout`). The same `command` inside a `popover` serves a long list that the person searches, such as a choice from more than seven options.

**Anatomy.** The `command` slot is a `popover` panel with 4 px padding and the 8 px radius. A `command-input-wrapper` holds an `input-group` of 32 px on `input` at 30%, with a search icon and the `command-input`. A `command-list` of at most 288 px scrolls, and it holds `command-group` blocks with a heading in `small` at 500 and `muted-foreground`, `command-item` rows, `command-separator` lines and a `command-empty` line. An item holds a 16 px icon, the label and an optional `command-shortcut` at the right.

**Variants.** `Command.Dialog` opens the palette in a `dialog` with no close button, a third of the height from the top (`top-1/3`). Its title and description are hidden, and their default text is English, so pass `title` and `description` in the language of the product. `Command.Root` in a `popover` is the list that the person searches.

**Sizes.** The palette in a dialog is 384 px wide, as `dialog`. An item is 32 px high with 8 px padding at the sides and 8 px between the icon and the label, in `body`. It takes the 4 px radius in a popover and the 8 px radius in the dialog.

**States.** Selected: Bits UI writes `[data-selected]` and `aria-selected="true"` on the item that Enter runs, which takes the `muted` ground and `foreground` text and turns its shortcut `foreground`. The first item is selected when the list opens. Disabled: `[data-disabled]` with `aria-disabled="true"` draws the item at 50% opacity. When no item matches, `command-empty` shows one line in the middle.

**Tokens.** `command`, `command-input`, `command-item`, `command-item-selected`, `command-shortcut`, `command-input-phone`, `command-item-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the input the combobox role, the list the listbox role and each item the option role. The arrow keys move the selection, Enter runs the item, and Esc closes the dialog. The search matches the label and the `keywords` of each item.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Command.Dialog bind:open vimBindings={false} title="Search" description="Find a page or an action">
  <Command.Input placeholder="Search" />
  <Command.List>
    <Command.Empty>No results.</Command.Empty>
    <Command.Group heading="Actions">
      <Command.Item onSelect={newInvoice}><PlusIcon />New invoice<Command.Shortcut>C</Command.Shortcut></Command.Item>
    </Command.Group>
  </Command.List>
</Command.Dialog>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px `stack.css` draws `Command.Dialog` as it draws a `dialog`, because its content is a `dialog-content`: the palette fills the screen and keeps the four safe-area insets with no padding of its own, and the page gives it no class for this (measured at 390 px with emulated insets). Its 8 px radius stays, because nova sets it with `!`. The search field is 44 px high and its text renders 16 px (`input-group-phone`, `command-input-phone`), so that iOS Safari does not zoom on focus, and each item is 44 px high (`command-item-phone`).

### navigation-menu

**Purpose.** A navigation menu is a row of top links with panels that drop down, for the header of a help or documentation area. The app shell navigates with the sidebar (see `## Layout`), so a web app never uses a navigation menu for the pages of its sidebar.

**Anatomy.** The `navigation-menu` root, a `<nav>`, holds a `navigation-menu-list` of `navigation-menu-item` entries. An item holds a `navigation-menu-trigger` with a 12 px chevron, or a `navigation-menu-link`. A trigger opens its `navigation-menu-content` in the shared `navigation-menu-viewport`, a `popover` layer 6 px under the list with a `foreground/10` ring, `shadow` and the 8 px radius.

**Variants.** `viewport={false}` on the root opens each content as its own layer under its trigger. A top link takes the look of a trigger with `class={navigationMenuTriggerStyle()}`. A link in a panel holds a title at 500 and a line in `muted-foreground`, or one label.

**Sizes.** A trigger is 36 px high with 10 px padding at the sides and the 8 px radius, in `body` at 500. A link in a panel has 8 px padding and the 6 px radius, in `body`.

**States.** Open: `[data-state="open"]` on the trigger, the content and the viewport, and `aria-expanded="true"` on the trigger. The open trigger takes the `muted` ground at 50%, and its chevron turns. Hover and focus give a trigger or a link the `muted` ground, and focus-visible adds the 3 px `ring/50` halo. Current page: `active` on a link sets `[data-active]`, which draws the `muted` ground at 50%, and `aria-current="page"`.

**Tokens.** `navigation-menu-trigger`, `navigation-menu-viewport`, `navigation-menu-link`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI names the `<nav>` "main", so give it a name of its own, such as `aria-label="Help"`. The left and right arrow keys move between the triggers, Enter opens a panel, the down arrow moves into it, and Esc closes it and returns the focus to its trigger.

**Example.**

```svelte
<NavigationMenu.Root aria-label="Help" class="max-md:hidden">
  <NavigationMenu.List>
    <NavigationMenu.Item>
      <NavigationMenu.Trigger>Guides</NavigationMenu.Trigger>
      <NavigationMenu.Content>
        <ul class="grid w-72 gap-1">
          <li><NavigationMenu.Link href="/help/invoices">Invoices</NavigationMenu.Link></li>
        </ul>
      </NavigationMenu.Content>
    </NavigationMenu.Item>
  </NavigationMenu.List>
</NavigationMenu.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the navigation menu does not show (`class="max-md:hidden"`, measured), and its links go into the More sheet of the tab bar (see `## Layout`). Nova does not fit it to a phone: at a width of 390 px a panel of 384 px gives a viewport of 392 px, wider than the screen (measured). For this reason its parts have no phone key.

### sidebar

**Purpose.** The sidebar holds the navigation of the app shell: the head, the pages and the foot that `## Layout` describes. It shows at full width from 1024 px, as a rail of icons from 768 px, and as a sheet below 768 px. The pages of a product go into the sidebar, and never into a `navigation-menu`.

**Anatomy.** `Sidebar.Provider` (`sidebar-wrapper`) keeps the state and sets the widths. `Sidebar.Root` (`sidebar`) wraps a `sidebar-inner` on the `sidebar` ground, with a `border` edge on its inner side, and the inner part holds a `sidebar-header`, a `sidebar-content` that scrolls and a `sidebar-footer`. With `collapsible="none"` nova renders one `<div>` with no `data-slot` in place of both, and below 768 px the sheet carries the `sidebar` slot and the ground. The content holds `sidebar-group` blocks with 8 px padding, each with an optional `sidebar-group-label` and a `sidebar-menu` of `sidebar-menu-item` rows. A row holds a `sidebar-menu-button` with a 16 px icon and a label, and optionally a `sidebar-menu-badge` with a count at the right, a `sidebar-menu-action` and a `sidebar-menu-sub` list of `sidebar-menu-sub-button` links. `Sidebar.Trigger` (`sidebar-trigger`) toggles the sidebar, `Sidebar.Rail` is a 16 px strip on its edge that toggles it on a click, and `Sidebar.Inset` (`sidebar-inset`) holds the rest of the screen.

**Variants.** The app shell uses `variant="sidebar"` and `collapsible="icon"`. `floating` draws the sidebar as a raised panel inside an 8 px margin, `inset` raises the main area beside it, and a web app uses neither. `collapsible="offcanvas"` slides the sidebar out of view, and `collapsible="none"` keeps it in the flow at every width. The head holds the workspace switcher, a menu button of size `lg` with a 32 px initials square that opens a `dropdown-menu` of the workspaces with their keys (⌘1, ⌘2). An app with one workspace has no switcher. In its place, a menu button of size `lg` holds the mark and the name of the product. It renders a link (`<a>`) to the first page of the sidebar, with no chevron and no menu. It keeps the 48 px of the switcher, so the search and the New button keep their place in every product (measured). The mark is one color, the text color of the sidebar through `currentColor`, so it follows the mode. The search and the New button follow, each a menu button with its key in a `sidebar-menu-badge`. A menu button with `variant="outline"` takes the `background` ground and a `sidebar-border` edge.

**Sizes.** The sidebar is 240 px wide and the rail is 56 px (`spacing.sidebar`, `spacing.sidebar-collapsed`), and nova draws 256 and 48 px, so set both widths on the provider: `style="--sidebar-width: 15rem; --sidebar-width-icon: 3.5rem"`. A menu button is 32 px high, 28 px in `sm` with `small` text and 48 px in `lg`, with 8 px padding, an 8 px gap, the 6 px radius and `body` text. The mark of the product is 32 by 32 px (`size-8`), in the place of the initials square, and the name beside it is `body` at 600. Nova sets every SVG in a menu button to 16 px (`[&_svg]:size-4`), so an inline SVG mark takes `size-8!` (measured: 16 px without the `!`). In the rail the row is 32 px, and the mark fills it. A product with no mark shows its initials in the square of the switcher. A sub-button is 28 px high, a group label 32 px in `small` at 500, and a badge 20 px in `small` at 500 with tabular figures. In the rail nova keeps the 8 px padding of a group, so a 32 px button sits 8 px from the left edge and 16 px from the right edge, and its icon is 4 px left of the center (measured). Give `Sidebar.Root` the classes `group-data-[collapsible=icon]:px-1 group-data-[collapsible=icon]:bg-sidebar`: 4 px of padding at each side (`spacing.space-1`) puts every button 12 px from both edges (measured), and the ground fills the padding.

**States.** Expanded and collapsed: `[data-state="expanded"]` or `[data-state="collapsed"]` on the `sidebar` slot, and `[data-collapsible="icon"]` while it is a rail. Current page: `isActive` writes `[data-active="true"]` on the menu button, which draws the `sidebar-accent` ground and `sidebar-accent-foreground` text at 500, and the product adds `aria-current="page"`. Hover and pressed draw the same ground, focus-visible draws a 2 px ring in `sidebar-ring`, and disabled (`aria-disabled="true"`) draws the button at 50% opacity. In the rail a menu button shows only its icon, the badges, the actions and the sub lists hide, the group labels fade out, and each menu button shows its `tooltip` on the right. Nova draws the badge of a menu item in `sidebar-foreground`, so give a badge with a count `class="text-muted-foreground"`: `cn` drops the nova class, and the count still takes `sidebar-accent-foreground` when its menu button is under the pointer or current. The mark of the product is still: it does not move on load, on hover or on a change of page, also when the public site of the product moves it.

**Tokens.** `sidebar-inner`, `sidebar-input`, `sidebar-group-label`, `sidebar-menu-button`, `sidebar-menu-button-sm`, `sidebar-menu-button-current`, `sidebar-menu-badge`, `sidebar-menu-sub-button`, `sidebar-menu-sub-button-current`, `sidebar-trigger-phone`, `sidebar-input-phone`, `sidebar-menu-button-phone`, `sidebar-menu-sub-button-phone`. A menu button with a tooltip renders the slot of the tooltip trigger, and the keys of the menu button still describe it. The group label is the sidebar text at 70%, a pair that the contrast check reads. A count takes the muted text color, a pair that the contrast check reads on the sidebar ground. Faint text on the sidebar ground is below 4.5:1 in light (4.48 in default, 4.20 in graphite).

**Accessibility.** Wrap the head, the content and the foot in a `<nav>` with a name in the language of the product, such as `aria-label="Main"`. The link of the product takes the name of the product as its name, and the mark takes `aria-hidden="true"`, also in the rail (measured in Chromium 148). In the rail every menu button shows `tooltipContent` with its label and its key, and the label stays in the button for a screen reader. A menu button with a count of unread items says the count in its name, such as `aria-label="Notices, 4 unread"`, and its `Sidebar.MenuBadge` takes `aria-hidden="true"`. In the rail the badge hides, and an `unread-mark` on the icon shows the unread items (see `### unread-mark`). ⌘B or Ctrl+B toggles the sidebar, and a product may add its own key, such as `[`. Nova names the trigger "Toggle Sidebar" and the phone sheet "Sidebar" in English, so give the trigger an `aria-label` in the language of the product. The title of the sheet cannot change without an edit to the component.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Sidebar.Provider style="--sidebar-width: 15rem; --sidebar-width-icon: 3.5rem">
  <Sidebar.Root collapsible="icon" class="group-data-[collapsible=icon]:px-1 group-data-[collapsible=icon]:bg-sidebar">
    <nav aria-label="Main" class="flex min-h-0 flex-1 flex-col">
      <Sidebar.Header>
        <Sidebar.Menu>
          <!-- An app with one workspace: the mark and the name of the product in the place of the switcher. -->
          <Sidebar.MenuItem>
            <Sidebar.MenuButton size="lg" tooltipContent="Product">
              {#snippet child({ props })}
                <a href="/invoices" {...props}><ProductMark class="size-8! shrink-0" aria-hidden="true" /><span class="truncate font-semibold">Product</span></a>
              {/snippet}
            </Sidebar.MenuButton>
          </Sidebar.MenuItem>
          <Sidebar.MenuItem>
            <Sidebar.MenuButton tooltipContent="Search ⌘K" onclick={openPalette}><SearchIcon /><span>Search</span></Sidebar.MenuButton>
            <Sidebar.MenuBadge class="max-md:top-3!"><Kbd>⌘K</Kbd></Sidebar.MenuBadge>
          </Sidebar.MenuItem>
        </Sidebar.Menu>
      </Sidebar.Header>
      <Sidebar.Content>
        <Sidebar.Group>
          <Sidebar.Menu>
            <Sidebar.MenuItem>
              <Sidebar.MenuButton isActive aria-current="page" tooltipContent="Invoices"><FileTextIcon /><span>Invoices</span></Sidebar.MenuButton>
              <Sidebar.MenuBadge class="text-muted-foreground max-md:top-3!">12</Sidebar.MenuBadge>
            </Sidebar.MenuItem>
          </Sidebar.Menu>
        </Sidebar.Group>
      </Sidebar.Content>
    </nav>
  </Sidebar.Root>
  <Sidebar.Inset>{@render children()}</Sidebar.Inset>
</Sidebar.Provider>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px nova draws the sidebar as a `sheet` from the left, 288 px wide, and the More tab of `## Layout` opens it with `useSidebar().setOpenMobile(true)`. A link in the sheet opens a page, and the sheet closes: put `@stack-kit/stack/parts/CloseSheetOnNavigate` once inside `Sidebar.Provider`. Each menu button is 44 px high: through `tooltip-trigger-phone` when it has a tooltip, through `sidebar-menu-button-phone` when it has none (measured), and through `dropdown-menu-trigger-phone` for the switcher and the user menu. The row of the product mark keeps its 48 px in the sheet (measured at 390 px). The sub-buttons and the search field are 44 px high (`sidebar-menu-sub-button-phone`, `sidebar-input-phone`), the trigger is 44 by 44 px (`sidebar-trigger-phone`), and the text takes the 15 px `body` step. A menu action and a group action stay 20 px, so hide them below 768 px (`max-md:hidden`, measured) and put the same action into the screen of the item. Nova places a `sidebar-menu-badge` for a 32 px row, 6 px from its top (`top-1.5`), so in a 44 px row the count or the key sits 6 px from the top and 18 px from the bottom (measured). Give each badge `max-md:top-3!`, as the example does: the 20 px badge is then 12 px from the top and the bottom of the 44 px row, and it stays 6 px from the top from 768 px (measured at 390, 700 and 1280 px). The class needs `!`, because the nova class reads the size of the button through a peer selector, and that selector has the higher specificity.

### tabs

**Purpose.** Tabs switch between views of one list or one object in place, such as the two to four fixed views of a list in the top bar (see `## Layout`) or the sections of a settings form. Each tab has its own panel, and one panel shows at a time. The pages of the app use the sidebar, and a filter that applies at once uses a `toggle-group`.

**Anatomy.** `Tabs.Root` (`tabs`) holds a `tabs-list` of `tabs-trigger` buttons, and one `tabs-content` panel for each trigger 8 px under the list. The list is a `muted` box with 3 px padding and the 8 px radius, and a trigger holds a label in `body` at 500 with an optional 16 px icon. The current trigger takes the `background` ground with `shadow-sm`, and in dark an `input/30` ground with an `input` edge.

**Variants.** `variant="default"` on the list draws the `muted` box. `variant="line"` draws no box, and a 2 px `foreground` line marks the current trigger under it. `orientation="vertical"` on the root stacks the triggers in a column at the width of the list, and the line of `line` moves to their right side. Four view tabs in the top bar show the icons only below `lg`. Each trigger holds its 16 px icon with `data-icon="inline-start"` and its label in `<span class="max-lg:sr-only">`, so that the label stays in the accessible name. Each trigger also sits in a `tooltip` that holds the label, and the `child` snippet of `Tooltip.Trigger` renders the `Tabs.Trigger`. `Tooltip.Root` takes `disabled={wide.current}`, with `const wide = new ScreenQuery('min-width: 64rem')` (see `### A screen page in print`). So the tooltip opens only while the label is hidden. The query is in rem, as the `lg` line of `stack.css` is. So the label and the tooltip change at the same width at every font size of the browser. The props of the snippet hold the `id` and the `data-slot` of the tooltip trigger, and `data-disabled` while the tooltip is disabled. The roving focus of the tab list skips an element with `data-disabled`, so from `lg` up the arrow keys, Home and End would do nothing. So give `Tabs.Trigger` the props first, then `id={undefined}`, `data-slot="tabs-trigger"` and `data-disabled={undefined}`. The tab then keeps its own `id`, its slot and its place in the roving focus. Below `lg` the tooltip opens on hover and on keyboard focus. At 900 px and at 1280 px the arrow keys move the focus and change the tab, and each tab keeps its own `id`, `aria-controls`, `data-state` and panel (measured in the showcase). With a browser font size of 20 px, `lg` is at 1280 px: at 1100 px the label is hidden and the tooltip opens, and at 1300 px the label shows and the tooltip does not (measured).

**Sizes.** The list is 32 px high and as wide as its triggers (`w-fit`). A trigger is 25 px high, with 6 px padding at the sides, a 6 px gap between the icon and the label, and the 6 px radius. An icon tab is 28 by 25 px, and a list of four icon tabs is 118 px wide (measured). The measured top bar holds a breadcrumb of two levels, the four tabs, and the filter and display buttons, with `gap-3` and `px-3`. With the English labels Board, List, Calendar and Timeline, the list is 310 px wide. At 1024 px beside the full sidebar, the bar then keeps 164 px free, room for one filter chip of 126 px. In band 2 the sidebar is the 56 px rail. At 768 px beside the rail, the icon list keeps 284 px free (measured). That is room for two filter chips of 126 px, and the labelled list would keep 92 px, less than one chip (both computed from the measured widths).

**States.** Current: `[data-state="active"]` and `aria-selected="true"` on the trigger, which the `data-active:` variant matches, and `[data-state="active"]` on its panel; an inactive panel has `hidden`. Nova draws an inactive label in `foreground` at 60% in light, which is 3.58:1 on the list in graphite light (computed with the math of the contrast check), below 4.5:1. So give each trigger `class="text-muted-foreground"`: `cn` drops the 60% class, an inactive label takes `muted-foreground`, and the current label stays `foreground` (measured in both themes and modes). Hover turns the label `foreground`, focus-visible draws the `ring` edge and the 3 px `ring/50` halo, and disabled (`disabled`, `[data-disabled]`) draws the trigger at 50% opacity.

**Tokens.** `tabs-list`, `tabs-trigger`, `tabs-trigger-current`, `tabs-list-phone`, `tabs-trigger-phone`. The inactive label of nova is the foreground at 60% and has no key. With the class of the States part it is the muted text on the muted ground, a pair that the contrast check reads.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the list the tablist role, each trigger the tab role and each panel the tabpanel role, and links each trigger to its panel. The arrow keys move between the triggers, Home and End go to the first and the last, and a trigger shows its panel as soon as it has focus (`activationMode="automatic"`). Give the list an `aria-label`, such as "View".

**Example.**

```svelte
<Tabs.Root bind:value={view}>
  <Tabs.List aria-label="View">
    <Tabs.Trigger value="open" class="text-muted-foreground">Open</Tabs.Trigger>
    <Tabs.Trigger value="paid" class="text-muted-foreground">Paid</Tabs.Trigger>
  </Tabs.List>
  <Tabs.Content value="open">{@render list('open')}</Tabs.Content>
  <Tabs.Content value="paid">{@render list('paid')}</Tabs.Content>
</Tabs.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a trigger is 44 px high and the list is 48 px high (`tabs-trigger-phone`, `tabs-list-phone`, measured), so that the triggers fit inside the list ground. The labels take the 15 px `body` step. The view tabs of the top bar do not show on a phone, because the top bar does not show (`## Layout`, band 1).

### breadcrumb

**Purpose.** A breadcrumb shows where the current page sits, and links to each level above it. It sits at the left of the top bar, before the view tabs (see `## Layout`). The last part is the current page, and it is not a link.

**Anatomy.** `Breadcrumb.Root` is a `<nav>` (`breadcrumb`) with a `breadcrumb-list` of `breadcrumb-item` entries, in `body` and `muted-foreground`, 6 px apart. An item holds a `breadcrumb-link`, or a `breadcrumb-page` for the last part. A `breadcrumb-separator` between two items is a 14 px chevron, and a `breadcrumb-ellipsis` stands in for the middle levels of a long path.

**Variants.** A separator takes another icon as its child, such as a slash. The ellipsis can sit in the trigger of a `dropdown-menu` that lists the levels that it hides.

**Sizes.** The text is `body` on a 20 px line, and the row is 20 px high, because the parts have no padding. The separator is 14 px and the box of the ellipsis 20 px. A list that is too wide wraps onto a second line.

**States.** A link is `muted-foreground` and turns `foreground` on hover, with no underline, because it is a navigation link. The current page is `foreground` at 400, and nova gives it `aria-current="page"` and `aria-disabled="true"`. A link has no focus style of its own, so a keyboard focus shows the outline of the browser in `ring` at 50%.

**Tokens.** `breadcrumb-list`, `breadcrumb-page`.

**Accessibility.** Nova names the `<nav>` "breadcrumb" in English, so give it an `aria-label` in the language of the product. The separators and the ellipsis are hidden from a screen reader (`aria-hidden="true"`). An ellipsis that opens a menu sits in a button with its own name, such as "More levels".

**Example.**

```svelte
<Breadcrumb.Root aria-label="Path" class="max-md:hidden">
  <Breadcrumb.List>
    <Breadcrumb.Item><Breadcrumb.Link href="/projects">Projects</Breadcrumb.Link></Breadcrumb.Item>
    <Breadcrumb.Separator />
    <Breadcrumb.Item><Breadcrumb.Page>Atlas</Breadcrumb.Page></Breadcrumb.Item>
  </Breadcrumb.List>
</Breadcrumb.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the top bar does not show, and the header of the screen shows a back button in place of the breadcrumb (`## Layout`, band 1). So give the breadcrumb `class="max-md:hidden"` (measured: hidden at 390 and 700 px, shown at 1280 px). For this reason its links of 20 px have no phone key.

### stepper

**Purpose.** A stepper shows the steps of a process that a person does in order, such as an import over four pages or a sign-in in four steps on one page. It shows the done steps, the current step, the steps that come next, and a step that failed. A `questionnaire` shows its own progress, and a list of steps that the person reorders is a `reorder-list`. The stepper is a part and not a rule for an ordered list, because its marks, its line and its phone form must look the same in every product. The stepper is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/Stepper`, and gives the name of the list and the steps with their states. STACK builds the part as an ordered list with the check and x icons.

**Anatomy.** The `stepper` slot is an `<ol>` in `body` and `muted-foreground`. Each `stepper-item` is an `<li>` that holds a mark (`stepper-mark`) and the name of its step (`stepper-label`), 8 px apart. The mark is a box of 20 px with the 8 px radius and a 1 px `border` edge, as a count badge of `### badge`. It holds the number in `small` at 500 with tabular figures. A done step shows a 12 px check icon in `foreground` in place of the number. The current step fills its mark with `foreground` and shows its number in `background`, and its name is at 600 in `foreground`. A step that failed shows a 12 px x icon in `destructive` on the `destructive/10` tint, and `destructive/20` in dark, which is the look of a destructive badge. Its name is at 600 in `foreground`, and after a middle dot the word Failed follows in `muted-foreground`. A next step shows its number and its name in `muted-foreground`. A 1 px line in `border` (`stepper-line`) joins two steps.

**Variants.** `orientation="horizontal"`, the default, puts the steps in one row, for a flow of pages, such as an import. A line of 24 px stands between two steps. The row sits in a band of 44 px under the top bar, with a `border` edge below and 24 px of padding at the sides (`px-6 py-3`). Its names are at 400, except the name of the current step. `orientation="vertical"` puts the steps down a page, for the steps on one page, such as a sign-in. Each step is a block with its name at 600, and under the name its body, such as a line of text, a button or a field. A vertical line joins the mark of each step to the mark of the next step. The name of a next step stays in `muted-foreground`.

**Sizes.** The mark is 20 by 20 px (`spacing.badge`), with an icon of 12 px. A horizontal step is 20 px high, and its line is 24 by 1 px, 12 px from each step (measured). In a vertical stepper the name has a line of 20 px, the body starts 8 px under it, and the steps are 20 px apart (`pb-5`). The line runs from 4 px under a mark to 4 px above the next mark (measured).

**States.** The product sets `data-state` on each item and on its mark: `done`, `current`, `failed` or `next`. The current item takes `aria-current="step"`. A step that failed is the current step, because the process stops there, so it keeps `aria-current="step"`. A person can go back to a done step while the process keeps the choices of the person and the step can still change, as in an import before it runs. The mark and the name of that step are then one link to the address of the step. The link takes the underline on hover and the focus ring of `## Shapes`, a 1 px `ring` edge and the 3 px `ring/50` halo (measured). A next step is never a link, because the action of each step checks its input before the next step opens. After a step that nobody can undo, such as the run of an import, the done steps are text. A stepper has no disabled state: a step that a person cannot reach yet is a next step.

**Tokens.** `stepper`, `stepper-item-current`, `stepper-item-phone`, `stepper-mark`, `stepper-mark-current`, `stepper-mark-done`, `stepper-mark-failed`, `stepper-line`. The tint of a failed mark carries an opacity, so it has no key, and the pair of destructive on destructive/10 covers it (see Colors). The line carries no information, so it needs no ratio.

**Accessibility.** The list takes `role="list"`, because Safari drops the list role of a list with no markers (not measured). Its `aria-label` names the process, such as "Import steps". Each mark takes `aria-hidden="true"`, because the place of the item gives the number and the name gives the state. The name of a done step and of a failed step says the state in words, such as "Source, done" and "Import, Failed" (measured in the accessibility tree of Chromium). These words are one text that does not show, beside the name that shows, which takes `aria-hidden="true"`. With the words in a span of their own, Chromium put a space before the comma ("Source , done", measured). The current item takes `aria-current="step"`. The DevTools protocol of Chromium lists no property for `aria-current`, so this file has no measure of what a screen reader says for it. The product gives each word in its own language.

**Example.**

```svelte
<div class="border-b border-border px-6 py-3 max-md:px-4 max-md:py-1">
  <Stepper label="Import steps" steps={[
    { label: 'Source', state: 'done', href: '/import/source' },
    { label: 'Mapping', state: 'current' },
    { label: 'Dry run', state: 'next' },
    { label: 'Import', state: 'next' }
  ]} />
</div>

<!-- In Stepper.svelte: a done step that links back, and the current step. -->
<ol data-slot="stepper" role="list" aria-label="Import steps" class="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-x-3 gap-y-1 text-sm text-muted-foreground max-md:flex-col max-md:flex-nowrap max-md:items-stretch max-md:gap-0">
  <li data-slot="stepper-item" data-state="done" class="relative flex items-center gap-3">
    <span aria-hidden="true" class="absolute top-9 -bottom-2 left-2.25 hidden w-px bg-border max-md:block"></span>
    <a href="/import/source" class="flex items-center gap-2 rounded-md underline-offset-4 outline-none hover:text-foreground hover:underline focus-visible:outline-1 focus-visible:outline-ring focus-visible:outline-solid focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 max-md:self-stretch">
      <span data-slot="stepper-mark" data-state="done" aria-hidden="true" class="flex size-5 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-lg border border-border text-xs font-medium text-foreground tabular-nums [&>svg]:size-3"><CheckIcon /></span>
      <span data-slot="stepper-label"><span aria-hidden="true">Source</span><span class="sr-only">Source, done</span></span>
    </a>
  </li>
  <li data-slot="stepper-item" data-state="current" aria-current="step" class="relative flex items-center gap-3">
    <span data-slot="stepper-line" aria-hidden="true" class="h-px w-6 shrink-0 bg-border max-md:hidden"></span>
    <span class="flex items-center gap-2">
      <span data-slot="stepper-mark" data-state="current" aria-hidden="true" class="flex size-5 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-lg border border-foreground bg-foreground text-xs font-medium text-background tabular-nums">2</span>
      <span data-slot="stepper-label" class="font-semibold text-foreground">Mapping</span>
    </span>
  </li>
</ol>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a horizontal stepper turns into a column (`max-md:flex-col`). Each step is a row of 44 px (`stepper-item-phone`), and a vertical line joins each mark to the next mark (measured at 390 px). The link of a done step fills its row, so the target is 44 px high. The names take the 15 px `body` step and the numbers the 13 px `small` step (measured). The band takes 16 px of padding at the sides and 4 px above and under the steps (`max-md:px-4 max-md:py-1`). A vertical stepper keeps its form, and each item is at least 44 px high.

### pagination

**Purpose.** Pagination moves through a long set of results one page at a time, such as the pages of a table of search results. It sits in the middle under the table. Nova computes the pages from `count` and `perPage`, and shows the first and the last page, the pages next to the current page, and an ellipsis for the rest.

**Anatomy.** `Pagination.Root` (`pagination`) is a `<nav>` with a `pagination-content` list of `pagination-item` entries 2 px apart. The items hold `Pagination.Previous`, the `pagination-link` buttons, `pagination-ellipsis` boxes and `Pagination.Next`. Previous and Next are ghost buttons with a chevron and a label that shows from 640 px, and they set no `data-slot`.

**Variants.** A page link is a ghost button, and the current page is an outline button: a `background` ground with a `border` edge, and in dark an `input/30` ground with an `input` edge. `siblingCount` sets how many pages show at each side of the current page, 1 by default. Previous is disabled on the first page, and Next on the last page.

**Sizes.** A page link is a 32 by 32 px square with the 8 px radius, in `body` at 500. Previous, Next and the box of the ellipsis are 32 px high, so the row is 32 px high.

**States.** Current page: `isActive` writes `[data-active="true"]` and `aria-current="page"` on the link, and Bits UI adds `[data-selected]`. Hover draws the `muted` ground on a ghost link, focus-visible draws the `ring` edge and the 3 px halo, and a disabled Previous or Next (`:disabled`) is drawn at 50% opacity.

**Tokens.** `pagination-link`, `pagination-link-current`, `pagination-link-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Nova names the parts in English: the `<nav>` "pagination", each link "Page 3", Previous "Go to previous page" and Next "Go to next page". Give the root, Previous and Next an `aria-label` in the language of the product. The ellipsis is hidden from a screen reader.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Pagination.Root count={total} perPage={50} bind:page siblingCount={wide.current ? 1 : 0} aria-label="Pages">
  {#snippet children({ pages, currentPage })}
    <Pagination.Content>
      <Pagination.Item><Pagination.Previous aria-label="Previous page" class="max-md:min-h-11 max-md:min-w-11" /></Pagination.Item>
      {#each pages as p (p.key)}
        <Pagination.Item>
          {#if p.type === 'ellipsis'}<Pagination.Ellipsis />{:else}<Pagination.Link page={p} isActive={currentPage === p.value} />{/if}
        </Pagination.Item>
      {/each}
      <Pagination.Item><Pagination.Next aria-label="Next page" class="max-md:min-h-11 max-md:min-w-11" /></Pagination.Item>
    </Pagination.Content>
  {/snippet}
</Pagination.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px each page link is 44 by 44 px (`pagination-link-phone`, measured). Previous and Next set no `data-slot`, so no phone key reaches them: give each `class="max-md:min-h-11 max-md:min-w-11"`, which makes them 44 by 44 px at 390 px and 44 px high at 700 px (measured). With 44 px parts, the row of page 5 of 10 is wider than a screen of 390 px (368 px of 358 px with nova Previous and Next, measured), so set `siblingCount` to 0 below 768 px, as the example does with a `ScreenQuery` (see `### A screen page in print`): seven parts are then 320 px wide.

### accordion

**Purpose.** An accordion stacks sections that each fold open under their title, such as the questions of a help page or the optional groups of a long form. The person opens only the sections that they need. One section that folds uses `collapsible`, and a group of a list that folds uses the group header of `## Layout`.

**Anatomy.** `Accordion.Root` (`accordion`) holds `accordion-item` blocks, each with a `border` line under it except the last. An item holds `Accordion.Trigger` (`accordion-trigger`), a button inside a heading, with the title and a 16 px `accordion-trigger-icon` chevron in `muted-foreground` at the right. `Accordion.Content` (`accordion-content`) holds the text of the section in `body`, with 10 px under it, and its links are underlined.

**Variants.** `type="single"` keeps at most one section open, and `type="multiple"` lets the person open several. `value` sets the open sections. `disabled` on an item draws it at 50% opacity, and the person cannot open or close it. `level` on the trigger sets the level of its heading, 3 by default.

**Sizes.** A trigger is 42 px high: `body` text at 500 on a 20 px line, 10 px of padding above and below, and a transparent 1 px edge. It fills the width with no padding at the sides, and its focus ring takes the 8 px radius. The content has no padding at the sides and 10 px at the bottom.

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` or `[data-state="closed"]` on the item, the trigger and the content, and `aria-expanded` on the trigger. The chevron points down when the section is closed and up when it is open, and the content slides open and closed. Hover underlines the title, focus-visible draws the `ring` edge and the 3 px `ring/50` halo, and disabled (`[data-disabled]`, `disabled`) draws the trigger at 50% opacity.

**Tokens.** `accordion-item`, `accordion-trigger`, `accordion-trigger-icon`, `accordion-content`, `accordion-trigger-phone`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI puts each trigger in an element with the heading role and the level of `level`, and sets `aria-expanded` and `aria-disabled` on the trigger. Enter and Space open and close a section, the arrow keys move between the triggers, and Home and End go to the first and the last. The title is the name of the trigger, so keep it short and different from the other titles on the page.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Accordion.Root type="multiple">
  <Accordion.Item value="tax">
    <Accordion.Trigger>Tax details</Accordion.Trigger>
    <Accordion.Content>The tax number and the VAT status of the client.</Accordion.Content>
  </Accordion.Item>
</Accordion.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a trigger is 44 px high (`accordion-trigger-phone`, measured), and its title takes the 15 px `body` step. Nothing else changes.

### collapsible

**Purpose.** A collapsible shows and hides one section with its own control, such as the advanced options of a form or a group of the sidebar. It has no look of its own: the product draws the trigger and the content. Several sections that fold in a stack use `accordion`.

**Anatomy.** `Collapsible.Root` (`collapsible`) holds `Collapsible.Trigger` (`collapsible-trigger`) and `Collapsible.Content` (`collapsible-content`). The trigger is a `<button>` with no style, so give it the look of a ghost `button` with `buttonVariants`, or use it as the `child` of another part, such as a sidebar group label. The content shows only while the section is open.

**Variants.** `open` binds the state, and `disabled` on the root keeps the state as it is. A chevron in the trigger points right when the section is closed and down when it is open, as on the group header of `## Layout`.

**Sizes.** None of its own. A trigger with the look of an `icon-sm` ghost button is 28 by 28 px with the 6 px radius, and the content takes the size of what it holds.

**States.** Open and closed: `[data-state="open"]` or `[data-state="closed"]` on the root, the trigger and the content, and `aria-expanded` on the trigger. Disabled: `[data-disabled]` on the root and the trigger. A trigger with the look of a ghost button keeps the `muted` ground while the section is open (`aria-expanded`, measured).

**Tokens.** `collapsible-trigger-phone`. The look of the trigger comes from its button variant and the keys of that variant.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI sets `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` on the trigger, and Enter and Space toggle the section. An icon-only trigger has a name that says what it shows, such as "Advanced options", and not the state.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Collapsible.Root bind:open>
  <Collapsible.Trigger class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'ghost', size: 'sm' })}>
    <ChevronRightIcon class={open ? 'rotate-90' : ''} />Advanced options
  </Collapsible.Trigger>
  <Collapsible.Content class="flex flex-col gap-5 pt-2"><!-- fields --></Collapsible.Content>
</Collapsible.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the trigger is at least 44 by 44 px (`collapsible-trigger-phone`, measured), because it is not a `button` slot and the button key does not reach it. Its text takes the 15 px `body` step.

### card

**Purpose.** A card groups the facts and the actions of one object in a box, such as an item of a catalogue, a plan, or a summary on a settings page. A set of objects that the person scans is a list of rows, and a page is never a dashboard of big cards (see Do's and Don'ts). The page that holds the cards, such as a catalogue, belongs to the product.

**Anatomy.** `Card.Root` (`card`) is a box at level 2 of `## Elevation & Depth`: the `card` ground, a `foreground/10` ring, the 8 px radius and no shadow, with 16 px of padding above and below and 16 px between its parts. A `card-header` holds the `card-title`, the `card-description` and an optional `card-action` at the top right. A `card-content` follows, and an optional `card-footer` is a band of `muted` at 50% with a `border` line on top, which reaches the sides and the bottom of the card.

**Variants.** `size="sm"` sets the padding and the gaps to 12 px and the title to `body`. An image as the first child fills the top of the card with no padding, and it takes the top corners. A card has no colored edge and no shadow (see Do's and Don'ts).

**Sizes.** The title is 16 px at 500 on a 22 px line (`leading-snug`), and 13 px at 500 in `sm`. The description and the content are `body`, and the description is `muted-foreground`. The padding at the sides is 16 px, and 12 px in `sm`.

**States.** A card has no state of its own, and its buttons and fields keep their own states. `[data-size="sm"]` marks the small size.

**Tokens.** `card`, `card-title`, `card-title-sm`, `card-description`, `card-footer`. The footer band is muted at 50%, so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** `Card.Title` renders a `<div>`, so put a heading of the right level inside it, such as `<h3>`, and a screen reader then lists the cards by their titles. An action of a card names its object, such as "Edit plan", because a screen reader can reach it apart from the card.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Card.Root>
  <Card.Header>
    <Card.Title><h3>Team plan</h3></Card.Title>
    <Card.Description>Up to 10 people and 50 GB of files.</Card.Description>
    <Card.Action><Button variant="ghost" size="sm">Edit plan</Button></Card.Action>
  </Card.Header>
  <Card.Content>12 EUR for each person, each month.</Card.Content>
  <Card.Footer class="gap-2"><Button size="sm">Choose Team</Button></Card.Footer>
</Card.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the buttons of a card are 44 px high (`button-phone`, measured on the action and the footer buttons), and the text takes the 15 px `body` step. The title keeps 16 px. A grid of cards shows one column on a phone.

### separator

**Purpose.** A separator is a 1 px line between two groups of content, such as two sections of a pane or two groups of buttons in a toolbar. It carries no information, so it needs no contrast ratio. Space divides content first, and a line comes only where space is not enough.

**Anatomy.** One `separator` slot, a `<div>` in `border`: 1 px high at the full width, or 1 px wide at the full height of its row.

**Variants.** `orientation="horizontal"` is the default, and `orientation="vertical"` divides the items of a row. `decorative` hides the line from a screen reader.

**Sizes.** The line is 1 px. The page sets the space around it with a margin class, such as `my-3` for 12 px above and below, and a vertical line takes the height of its row, such as 20 px in a row of `h-5`.

**States.** None. `[data-orientation="horizontal"]` or `[data-orientation="vertical"]` marks the direction.

**Tokens.** `separator`. A menu and the sidebar draw their own separator parts, and those have no key.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the line the separator role and `aria-orientation`, so a screen reader announces it. Set `decorative` when the line only spaces content, so that its role is none.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Separator class="my-3" />
<div class="flex h-5 items-center gap-3"><span>Draft</span><Separator orientation="vertical" /><span>12 files</span></div>
```

**Phone.** Nothing changes below 768 px.

### aspect-ratio

**Purpose.** An aspect ratio box keeps the ratio of its width to its height, so that an image, a video or a map keeps its shape at every width. The box takes the width of its container, and the ratio sets its height. The space is then kept before the content loads, so the page does not move.

**Anatomy.** Bits UI renders a wrapper `<div>` that sets the height through `padding-bottom`, and inside it the `aspect-ratio` slot, which fills the wrapper. The `class` of the component goes to the inner slot, so the radius and the ground of the box go there too. The content, such as `<img class="size-full object-cover">`, fills the slot.

**Variants.** `ratio` sets the ratio as a number, such as `16 / 9`, `4 / 3` or `1`. The box has no look of its own: give it the 8 px radius (`rounded-lg`) and the `muted` ground for the time before the content loads.

**Sizes.** The width comes from the container, and the height is the width divided by the ratio. A grid or flex parent that stretches its items also stretches the wrapper, and the ratio breaks: three boxes of 16/9, 4/3 and 1/1 in one grid row of the preview all measure 245 by 245 px. Give such a parent `items-start`, which gives 245 by 138, 245 by 184 and 245 by 245 px (measured), or put each box in its own `<div>`.

**States.** None. The content inside keeps its own states, such as a `skeleton` while it loads.

**Tokens.** None. The box takes its radius and its ground from the classes that Variants gives.

**Accessibility.** The box adds no role and no name. An image inside it has an `alt` text, or `alt=""` when it only decorates, and a video has captions.

**Example.**

```svelte
<div class="grid grid-cols-3 items-start gap-4">
  <AspectRatio ratio={16 / 9} class="overflow-hidden rounded-lg bg-muted">
    <img src={cover} alt="The front page of the report" class="size-full object-cover" />
  </AspectRatio>
</div>
```

**Phone.** Nothing changes below 768 px: the box follows the width of its container.

### resizable

**Purpose.** A resizable group lets the person drag the line between two or more panes to change their sizes, such as an editor and its preview. The list and the detail pane of the app shell have fixed widths (see `## Layout`), and they do not use it. `autoSaveId` on the group keeps the sizes for the next visit.

**Anatomy.** `Resizable.PaneGroup` (`resizable-pane-group`) holds `Resizable.Pane` parts with a `Resizable.Handle` (`resizable-handle`) between each two. The handle is a 1 px line in `border` with a 4 px area for the pointer. `withHandle` adds a grip in the middle of the line: a bar of 4 by 24 px in `border` with round ends.

**Variants.** `direction="horizontal"` puts the panes side by side, and `direction="vertical"` stacks them and turns the grip. `defaultSize`, `minSize` and `maxSize` on a pane set its size in percent of the group.

**Sizes.** The group fills its container (`size-full`), so the container needs a height. The line is 1 px, the area for the pointer 4 px, and the grip 4 by 24 px.

**States.** Dragging: `[data-active="pointer"]` on the handle, and keyboard focus: `[data-active="keyboard"]`. Focus-visible draws a 1 px `ring` with a 1 px offset. A disabled handle has `[data-enabled="false"]`.

**Tokens.** `resizable-handle`.

**Accessibility.** The handle has the separator role with `aria-valuenow`, and it is in the tab order. The arrow keys move it, and Home and End move it to its limits. Give each pane a `minSize` at which its content still works.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Resizable.PaneGroup direction="horizontal" autoSaveId="editor" class="min-h-96 rounded-lg border">
```
