```svelte
  <Resizable.Pane defaultSize={50} minSize={30}>{@render editor()}</Resizable.Pane>
  <Resizable.Handle withHandle />
  <Resizable.Pane defaultSize={50} minSize={30}>{@render preview()}</Resizable.Pane>
</Resizable.PaneGroup>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a phone shows one pane at a time, as band 1 of `## Layout` says, and a line of 4 px is no touch target. So render one pane in place of the group, with a control that switches between the panes, such as `tabs`.

### scroll-area

**Purpose.** A scroll area scrolls its content inside a box of a fixed size, with a thin scroll bar that follows the theme, such as a long list in a popover or a row of cards. The page and the main list scroll with the scroll bar of the browser. Use it only where a box must keep its size.

**Anatomy.** `ScrollArea` (`scroll-area`) holds the `scroll-area-viewport`, which holds the content, and a `scroll-area-scrollbar` for each axis with a `scroll-area-thumb`. The bar is 10 px wide with 1 px of padding, and the thumb is a pill in `border`.

**Variants.** `orientation` is `vertical` by default, `horizontal` or `both`. `type="hover"`, the default, shows the bars while the pointer is over the area, and `type="always"` keeps them. `scrollbarXClasses` and `scrollbarYClasses` take the classes of each bar.

**Sizes.** The page sets the size of the area with a class, such as `h-72`, and the content scrolls inside it. The thumb is 7 px wide in the 10 px bar (measured), with the full radius, and the viewport takes the radius of the area.

**States.** The bars show on hover and while the content scrolls, and they hide 600 ms after the pointer leaves. The thumb keeps the `border` color in every state. Focus-visible on the viewport draws the 3 px `ring/50` halo.

**Tokens.** `scroll-area-thumb`. The thumb is a divider color with no contrast ratio, so the bar is a help for the pointer only.

**Accessibility.** The content keeps its own focus order, and a keyboard scrolls the area once an element inside it has focus. The wheel and a touch scroll the area as well, so the thumb is never the only way to reach the content.

**Example.**

```svelte
<ScrollArea class="h-72 rounded-md border">
  <ul class="p-2">{#each tags as tag (tag)}<li class="py-1.5 text-sm">{tag}</li>{/each}</ul>
</ScrollArea>
```

**Phone.** A phone scrolls the area with a swipe, and the thumb is no touch target, so it has no phone key. Nothing else changes below 768 px.

### carousel

**Purpose.** A carousel shows one item, or a few items, of a short set at a time, such as the images of an attachment or the steps of an introduction. The person moves through the set with two buttons, the arrow keys or a swipe. A set that the person must compare or search goes into a list or a grid, never into a carousel.

**Anatomy.** `Carousel.Root` (`carousel`) holds `Carousel.Content` (`carousel-content`), a frame that clips a row of `carousel-item` slides, and the `carousel-previous` and `carousel-next` buttons. A slide takes the full width of the frame (`basis-full`), and the slides are 16 px apart. The buttons are round outline buttons of 28 px with a chevron, and they sit outside the frame (`-start-12`, `-end-12`), in the middle of its height.

**Variants.** `orientation="vertical"` stacks the slides, and the buttons sit above and below the frame. `opts` passes the options of Embla, such as `loop` or `align: 'start'`, and a slide with `basis-1/2` or `basis-1/3` shows two or three items at a time. Never add a plugin that moves the slides by itself.

**Sizes.** The page sets the width with a class on the root, such as `w-72`, and a slide takes the full width of the frame. The buttons are 28 by 28 px (`icon-sm`) with a 16 px chevron and the full radius. Keep 48 px free at each side for the buttons (`mx-12`), or above and below in a vertical carousel.

**States.** A button at the end of the set is disabled (`disabled`, `aria-disabled="true"`) and drawn at 50% opacity, unless `loop` is on. Hover draws the `muted` ground, and focus-visible draws the `ring` edge and the 3 px halo. A drag with the pointer or a finger moves the slides.

**Tokens.** `carousel-previous`, `carousel-next`, `carousel-previous-phone`, `carousel-next-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The root has the region role with the role description "carousel", and each slide has the group role with the role description "slide". Give the root an `aria-label`, such as "Images of the invoice", and give the buttons names in the language of the product, because nova names them "Previous slide" and "Next slide" in English. The left and right arrow keys move the slides while a button has focus.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Carousel.Root aria-label="Images of the invoice" class="mx-12 w-72">
  <Carousel.Content>
    {#each images as image (image.id)}
      <Carousel.Item><img src={image.src} alt={image.alt} class="aspect-video rounded-lg object-cover" /></Carousel.Item>
    {/each}
  </Carousel.Content>
  <Carousel.Previous aria-label="Previous image" />
  <Carousel.Next aria-label="Next image" />
</Carousel.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the buttons are 44 by 44 px (`carousel-previous-phone`, `carousel-next-phone`, measured), because they set their own slot and the button key does not reach them. They then sit 4 px from the frame, so keep the 48 px at each side. A swipe moves the slides, and the buttons stay, because WCAG 2.2 asks for a single tap that does the work of a drag (criterion 2.5.7).

### calendar

**Purpose.** A calendar picks one day, or several days, from the grid of a month, such as a due date. In a form it opens from a date field in a `popover`, and the field shows the date in the format of the locale. A heat map of days is a chart (see `## Charts`), not a calendar. The records of a month on its days, such as the invoices by their due date, are a `month-view`.

**Anatomy.** `Calendar` renders a root with 8 px padding on `background`, which turns transparent in a `popover` or a `card`. A nav puts the previous and the next button at the top corners, and each month has a header with its caption and a grid. The grid has a row of head cells with the short names of the days, then a row of 28 px cells for each week, each with a day. No part sets a `data-slot`, so the keys of this component take the name of the part attribute of Bits UI, such as `calendar-day` for `[data-calendar-day]`.

**Variants.** `type="single"` picks one day, and `type="multiple"` several days. `captionLayout="dropdown"` replaces the caption with a select for the month and one for the year, and `"dropdown-months"` or `"dropdown-years"` replaces one of them. `numberOfMonths` shows more months, side by side from 768 px. A Today control is a ghost `button` under the grid that sets `value` and `placeholder` to today.

**Sizes.** A cell and its day are 28 by 28 px (`--cell-size`, set as `--spacing(7)`) with the 6 px radius (`--cell-radius`), and the number of a day is `body` with a line height of 1. The head cells are a fixed 12.8 px in `muted-foreground`, and the navigation buttons are 28 px ghost buttons with the 8 px radius. One month is 196 px of grid with 8 px of padding at each side.

**States.** Selected: `[data-selected]` on the day and the cell, with `aria-selected="true"` on the cell, draws the day on `primary` with `primary-foreground` text. Today: `[data-today]` draws the day on `accent` with `accent-foreground` text, as a square with the 6 px radius, unless it is selected. A day of the month before or after (`[data-outside-month]`) is `muted-foreground`, a disabled day (`[data-disabled]`) is `muted-foreground` at 50% and takes no input, and an unavailable day (`[data-unavailable]`) is struck through. Hover draws `accent` at 50%. Nova sets no ring width on a day, so a keyboard focus shows only the outline of the browser in `ring` at 50% (measured).

**Tokens.** `calendar-root`, `calendar-head-cell`, `calendar-day`, `calendar-day-selected`, `calendar-day-today`, `calendar-day-outside-month`.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the grid the grid role, each cell the gridcell role and each day a full name, such as "Tuesday, October 13, 2026", in the language of `locale`. The arrow keys move by a day or a week, and Enter or Space picks the day. Nova sets `locale` to `en-US`, so pass the locale of the product: the names of the days, the caption and the first day of the week follow it, and `weekStartsOn` sets another first day. Bits UI names the navigation buttons "Previous" and "Next" in English. In a form, a `field-label` names the date field, which is the trigger of the popover. Give the label an `id`, and give the trigger `aria-labelledby` with the `id` of the label and its own `id`, as `### label` says. With `for` alone the name is "Due date" and the date is lost, and with both it is "Due date 13 Oct 2026" (measured in Chromium 148).

**Example.**

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import { DateFormatter, getLocalTimeZone } from '@internationalized/date';
  const df = new DateFormatter('sr-Latn-RS', { dateStyle: 'medium' });
</script>

<Popover.Root bind:open>
  <Popover.Trigger class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'outline' })}>{value ? df.format(value.toDate(getLocalTimeZone())) : 'Pick a date'}</Popover.Trigger>
  <Popover.Content class="w-auto p-0" role="dialog" aria-label="Pick a date">
    <Calendar type="single" bind:value locale="sr-Latn-RS" class="max-md:[--cell-size:--spacing(11)]" />
  </Popover.Content>
</Popover.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px nova keeps the 28 px days, and the cells set no `data-slot`, so a phone key cannot reach them. Give the root `class="max-md:[--cell-size:--spacing(11)]"`: each day and each navigation button is then 44 by 44 px, and a month is 324 px wide, which fits a screen of 390 px (measured: 326 px with the 1 px edge of the preview, and 28 px days at 1280 px). Two months stack below 768 px.

### range-calendar

**Purpose.** A range calendar picks a first day and a last day, such as the period of a report or of a filter. The range includes both days. A product that turns the range into a time span ends it at the end of the last day, not at its start, so that the last day stays in the span.

**Anatomy.** `RangeCalendar` renders the parts of `calendar` under the part attributes `[data-range-calendar-*]`, on a root with 12 px padding. A chosen range draws an `accent` band across its cells, with the 6 px radius at its two ends and at the ends of each week row. The first and the last day sit on `primary`, as a chosen day of `calendar` does.

**Variants.** `numberOfMonths={2}` shows two months, side by side from 768 px. `captionLayout`, `locale`, `weekStartsOn`, `minValue`, `maxValue` and `isDateDisabled` are as on `calendar`, and `minDays` and `maxDays` limit the length of the range.

**Sizes.** As on `calendar`: cells and days of 28 px with the 6 px radius, and navigation buttons of 28 px. One month is 196 px of grid with 12 px of padding at each side.

**States.** The first day: `[data-range-start]`, and the last day: `[data-range-end]`, each on `primary` with `primary-foreground` text. A day inside the range (`[data-range-middle]`) has no ground of its own and shows `foreground` text on the `accent` band of its cell, which `[data-selected]` marks. Today, a day of another month, a disabled day and hover look as on `calendar`, and so does the focus outline.

**Tokens.** `range-calendar-root`, `range-calendar-cell-selected`, `range-calendar-day`, `range-calendar-day-range-start`, `range-calendar-day-range-end`.

**Accessibility.** The roles, the names and the keys are those of `calendar`, and each cell of the range has `aria-selected="true"` (measured). Show the chosen range as text too, such as "1 to 17 Oct 2026", in the format of the locale.

**Example.**

```svelte
<RangeCalendar bind:value={period} numberOfMonths={2} locale="sr-Latn-RS" class="rounded-lg border max-md:[--cell-size:--spacing(11)]" />
```

**Phone.** As on `calendar`: give the root `class="max-md:[--cell-size:--spacing(11)]"`, and each day and each navigation button is then 44 by 44 px (measured). A month is 332 px wide (334 px with the edge of the preview, measured), and two months stack below 768 px.

### month-view

**Purpose.** A month view places the records of a list on the days of one month by one date, such as the invoices by their due date. The person sees which days are full, and opens a day to reach its records. It is a view of a list, beside the list and the board in the view tabs. A `calendar` picks a date in a form, and a heat map of days is a chart (see `## Charts`), so neither shows records. A month view is the wrong view for the times inside a day, which a week view or a day view shows, and this file defines neither. It is also the wrong view when most records have no date. The month view is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/MonthView`, and gives the month, today, the locale, the records of each day and the snippet of one record. STACK builds the part from `button`, `popover` and `skeleton`, and from `@internationalized/date`, as `calendar` does. The part shows the grid from 768 px and the list by day below it, by `bands.md` of `@stack-kit/stack/behaviour/width`.

**Anatomy.** The `month-view` slot holds a head line and the grid. The head line holds the month and the year as a heading in `section` type. On its right, Previous and Next are ghost icon buttons of 28 px, and Today is an `outline` button of size `sm` between them. The grid has a `border` edge and the 8 px radius. Its first row holds the short names of the days (`month-view-head-cell`) in `meta` at 500 and `muted-foreground`, 28 px high. Five or six week rows follow and share the rest of the height, each at least 120 px high (`spacing.month-view-day`, `min-h-30`). Each day (`month-view-day`) has a `border` edge on its right and its bottom, and 4 px of padding. Its number (`month-view-day-number`) sits in a 20 px box at the top left, in `meta` at 500 and `muted-foreground` with tabular figures. The records of the day follow under it, 4 px apart. Each record (`month-view-record`) is a line of 20 px with the 6 px radius. It is a link with the status icon at 14 px and the title in `small`, which ends in an ellipsis. A day of the month before or after (`[data-outside-month]`) takes `muted` at 50% and shows its number and no record.

**Variants.** The first day of the week and the names of the days follow the locale, as on `calendar`, and `startOfWeek` of `@internationalized/date` gives the first day. `en-GB` and `sr-Latn-RS` start on Monday and `en-US` on Sunday, so November 2026 takes six weeks in the first two and five in `en-US` (measured). Overflow: a day holds four lines. A day with more records shows the first three and a line that says how many more, such as "3 more". That line is a ghost button of 20 px in `small` and `muted-foreground` (`month-view-more`), and it opens the day in a `popover`. The popover has the full date as its title, the count as its description, and every record of the day as a line. No date: a product may show the records with no date in a list beside the grid, 200 px wide from 1024 px and under the grid below 1024 px. The list holds a heading with the count, then the records as lines of a day, then "3 more" in the same way. Empty: a month with no record shows one line in `body` and `muted-foreground` under the head line, in a `role="status"` region, such as "No invoice falls due in this month.". The grid still shows.

**Sizes.** The head line is 28 px high (measured). A day is at least 120 px high, which holds the 20 px number and four lines of 20 px, 4 px apart. At 1280 px with the sidebar of the preview, a day is 144 px wide and a record line 135 px (measured). The number box and the disc of today are 20 by 20 px.

**States.** Today: `[data-today]` on the day and on its number draws the number on a disc of `foreground` with `background` text (`month-view-day-number-today`, measured), and the day takes `aria-current="date"`. Focus: the day that has the focus draws the 3 px `ring/50` halo inside its edge (`ring-inset`). Loading: each day shows still `skeleton` lines of 20 px in place of its records, and the grid takes `aria-busy="true"` (measured in the example). A record keeps the states of a link: the `muted` ground on hover and the focus ring.

**Tokens.** `month-view`, `month-view-head-cell`, `month-view-day-number`, `month-view-day-number-today`, `month-view-record`, `month-view-more`, `month-view-record-phone`. The ground of a day of another month is muted at 50%, so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** The grid has the grid role, and the heading of the month names it, such as "November 2026". Each week is a row, each name of a day is a column header with the full name, and each day is a grid cell. A day of the month before or after is a grid cell too, with the date as its name and `aria-disabled="true"`, so that each day stays under its column header. It takes no focus (measured in Chromium). The name of a day gives the date and the count, such as "Wednesday 18 November, today, 6 invoices" or "Thursday 19 November, nothing" (measured in Chromium). The grid is one tab stop: the day that had the focus last, else today, else the first day of the month. The arrow keys move the focus by a day and by a week. A step past the first or the last day of the month shows the month before or after, on the day of the step. Home and End move to the first and the last day of the week inside the month. Page Up and Page Down show the month before or after, on the same day (all measured). Enter or Space on a day with records opens its popover, and the focus moves to the first record. Esc closes it and gives the focus back to the day (measured). The grid stops each of these keys, so that a key handler of the list does not act on it too. A key with Cmd, Ctrl, Alt or Shift passes to the product and the browser (measured with Cmd and Shift). A record in the grid takes `tabindex="-1"`, so Tab leaves the grid in one step, and a pointer still opens a record with one click. `architecture/web-app.md` lists these keys of the component. The heading of the month is a polite live region, so a screen reader says the new month.

**Example.**

```svelte
<MonthView bind:month {today} locale="sr-Latn-RS" {days} count={(n) => `${n} ${n === 1 ? 'invoice' : 'invoices'}`}>
  {#snippet record(inv, tabindex)}
    <a href="/invoices/{inv.key}" {tabindex} class="flex w-full min-w-0 items-center gap-1.5 rounded-md px-1.5 text-xs outline-none hover:bg-muted focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 max-md:text-sm">
      <StatusIcon status={inv.status} class="size-3.5 shrink-0" /><span class="truncate">{inv.client}</span>
    </a>
  {/snippet}
  {#snippet empty()}No invoice falls due in this month.{/snippet}
</MonthView>

<!-- In MonthView.svelte: one day of the grid. The day is a grid cell in the roving tab order. -->
<div role="gridcell" tabindex={isCurrent ? 0 : -1} aria-label="Wednesday 18 November, today, 6 invoices" aria-current="date" data-slot="month-view-day" data-today class="flex min-w-0 flex-col gap-1 border-r border-border p-1 outline-none last:border-r-0 focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 focus-visible:ring-inset">
  <span data-slot="month-view-day-number" data-today aria-hidden="true" class="flex size-5 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-foreground text-2xs font-medium text-background tabular-nums">18</span>
  <ul class="flex min-w-0 flex-col gap-1">
    <li data-slot="month-view-record" class="flex h-5 min-w-0 shrink-0"><!-- A record with tabindex -1. --></li>
    <li class="flex h-5 shrink-0">
      <button type="button" tabindex="-1" data-slot="month-view-more" class={cn(buttonVariants({ variant: 'ghost', size: 'xs' }), 'h-5 w-full justify-start px-1.5 text-xs font-normal text-muted-foreground tabular-nums')}>3 more</button>
    </li>
  </ul>
</div>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the month view is a list by day: the head line, then a head for each day with records, then its records as rows. The head of a day is the group header of `### Lists`: 32 px high on `muted`, with a `border` edge below, sticky at the top of its scroll box. It holds the date in words at 600, "Today" for today, and the count on the right (measured at 390 px). Each record fills a row of 44 px (`month-view-record-phone`), and its text takes the 15 px `body` step. The row takes `box-content`, so that its `border-faint` divider sits outside the 44 px. The link then keeps the whole target (measured: a row of 45 px and a link of 44 px). The buttons of the head line are 44 px high (`button-phone`, measured). A day with no record does not show, and a month with no record shows its empty line.

### time-field

**Purpose.** A time field takes a time of day, such as the time at which a schedule runs. It shows the time in the hour cycle of the locale, such as 03:00 in `sr-Latn-RS` and 03:00 AM in `en-US`. A duration, such as 90 minutes, is a number in an `input-group` with the unit in an addon, not a time field. A filter by period needs no new part: a `toggle-group` holds the fixed periods, such as 7 days and 30 days, and after it a `range-calendar` in a `popover` takes a period that the person picks.

**Anatomy.** The time field is a part of STACK. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/TimeField`, and gives the label, the locale and the value. The part is Bits UI `TimeField` in a `field`, with no shadcn-svelte wrapper. It is not a plain `input`, because Bits UI gives each part of the time a segment with the keys of a spin button, in the hour cycle of the locale. So 3 alone fills the hour and leaves the minutes empty, and it does not turn into 03:00 on blur, as it can in an `input`. `TimeField.Root` holds the value, a `Time` of `@internationalized/date`. The root of the part is the `field` (`time-field`), which holds the label, the box, the description and the `field-error`. `TimeField.Label` names the field, and its `child` snippet renders it as a `Field.Label`, so that it keeps the look of a label. `TimeField.Input` (`time-field-input`) is the box, with the height, the ground, the edge and the radius of `input`. It holds one `TimeField.Segment` (`time-field-segment`) for each part of the time, `hour`, `minute`, `second` or `dayPeriod`, and one `literal` segment for each separator, in the order of the locale. A segment that is not a `literal` is the target, and a `<span>` in it draws the value and the focus ground, so that the target can be larger than the digits. Each `literal` takes `pointer-events-none`, so that a click on a separator goes to the box. No part of Bits UI sets a `data-slot`, so the keys take the name of the part attribute, as on `calendar`: Bits UI marks the box with `[data-time-field-input]` and each segment with `[data-segment]`, which holds the name of its part. The part keeps the error: it writes the error of `onInvalid`, such as "Choose 22:00 or earlier." for a time after `maxValue`, and a new time clears it. A product binds `error` (`bind:error`) and sets it for an error of the form, such as an empty time on submit. Without the binding, a second error with the same text does not show after a new time clears it.

**Variants.** `granularity` is `minute` by default: `hour` shows the hour only, and `second` adds the seconds. `hourCycle` is 12 or 24, and without it the field follows `locale`. `locale` is required, because Bits UI uses `en` for a field with no locale. In a 12-hour locale the field adds a `dayPeriod` segment, which A and P set to AM and PM. `minValue` and `maxValue` limit the time, and `validate` returns a message for any other rule. The error of a time outside the limits names the limit, such as "Choose 22:00 or earlier.", and `limitError` gives other words for it.

**Sizes.** 32 px high, with 10 px side padding and 4 px top and bottom padding, as `input` (measured next to an input at 1280 px: both 32 px). The time is the `input` step with `tabular-nums`, so that the digits keep their width when they change. The field fills the width of its container (`w-full`). In a row with other fields from 768 px, give it a width class, such as `md:w-32`. Below 768 px it keeps the full width, because a 12-hour time needs a box of 138 px there (measured at 390 px). From 768 px a segment that is not a `literal` is 24 by 24 px (`min-h-6 min-w-6`), as `spacing.target-min` asks, with the digits in its center (measured at 1280 px). Its span has 2 px side padding and the 4 px radius, so that the focus ground is 20.9 by 20 px, the size of the digits (measured).

**States.** A segment takes the focus, not the box. The focused segment takes `accent-foreground` text, and its span takes the `accent` ground (`:focus-visible`), and Chromium also matches `:focus-visible` after a click (measured). The box turns its edge `ring` and adds the `ring/50` halo (`:focus-within`). An empty segment shows `––`, and Bits UI gives it `aria-valuetext="Empty"`, which turns it `muted-foreground`. Invalid: when the time is outside `minValue` and `maxValue`, or `validate` returns a message, Bits UI writes `[data-invalid]` on the box and `aria-invalid="true"` on each segment. The box then draws a `destructive` edge and a `destructive/20` halo, as an invalid `input` does. For an error that Bits UI does not know, such as an empty time on submit, the product sets `error`. The part then sets `aria-invalid="true"` on the box, and the box draws the same edge (measured). In both cases the `field` takes `data-invalid`, and a `field-error` under the box names the fix (see Accessibility). Disabled: `disabled` writes `[data-disabled]` on the box and takes each segment out of the tab order, and the box takes the `input/50` ground at 50% opacity. Read-only: `readonly` writes `[data-readonly]` on each segment, which stays in the tab order and takes no change, and the box looks as at rest.

**Tokens.** `time-field-input`, `time-field-input-invalid`, `time-field-segment`, `time-field-segment-focus-visible`, `time-field-segment-placeholder`. The label takes `field-label`. The span of a segment draws the radius of `time-field-segment` and the ground of `time-field-segment-focus-visible`. The key `time-field-segment` adds the name of this part, time-field, before the name of its part attribute, because the DateField of Bits UI writes the same attribute on its segments.

**Accessibility.** Bits UI gives the box the `group` role and each segment the `spinbutton` role with `inputmode="numeric"`, so that a phone shows the number keys. A digit moves the focus to the next segment when the segment cannot take one more digit: 3 gives the hour 03, so 300, 3:00 and 0300 each give 03:00, and the field ignores the colon (measured). 3 alone leaves the minutes empty, and the field has no value until each segment holds a number. Up and Down change the focused segment, Left and Right move between the segments, and Backspace clears a segment. Bits UI ties the label to the box and to each segment with `aria-labelledby`, and a click on the label focuses the first segment (measured). Bits UI 2.19.5 does not tie an error to the field: its `errorMessageId` reaches no element (measured). A segment takes the focus, not the box. So the part gives each segment that is not a `literal` an `aria-describedby` with the `id` of the description and of the `field-error`, as `### field` asks. Bits UI keeps it, and the segment reads the error after its name, such as "hour, Run at" and then "Choose 22:00 or earlier." (measured). The part writes the error of a time outside the limits and the message of `validate`, and a new time clears it (measured). Bits UI 2.19.5 names each segment in English, such as "hour, Run at", and in a 24-hour field it reads the hour with a day period, such as "15 AM" (measured). So where the time matters, also show it in a sentence, such as "Runs every day at 03:00".

**Example.**

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import TimeField from '@stack-kit/stack/parts/TimeField';
  import { Time } from '@internationalized/date';
  let runAt = $state<Time>();
  // The part writes the error of a time after maxValue. The form sets it, such as for an empty time on submit.
  let error = $state<string>();
</script>

<TimeField label="Run at" locale="sr-Latn-RS" bind:value={runAt} bind:error maxValue={new Time(22, 0)} />
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the box is 44 px high (`max-md:h-11`), and its text renders 16 px (`text-base`), so that a phone does not zoom on focus (measured at 390 px: 44 px, as an `input`). The box sets no `data-slot`, so no phone key reaches it, and the classes set the height. Each segment that is not a `literal` is 44 by 44 px (`max-md:min-h-11 max-md:min-w-11`), as `spacing.touch` asks. It fills the box from the top edge to the bottom edge, and the segments do not overlap (measured at 390 px). Its span stays 24.8 by 22 px, so that the focus ground keeps the size of the digits (measured). The box drops its side padding (`max-md:px-0`), so that the digits start 12.6 px from its left edge, and the text of an `input` 11 px (measured). Chromium gives a touch tap near a segment to that segment, so a tap on the colon focuses the minutes (measured). A tap far from a segment, such as at the right of the box, focuses the first segment (`focusFirst`, measured).

### time-zone-field

**Purpose.** A time zone field picks one zone of the IANA time zone database, such as Europe/Belgrade, for a schedule or for the setting of a workspace that counts "today". It is a `command` in a `popover`, as `### select` asks for a list of more than seven options, because the list holds more than 400 zones and the person searches it.

**Anatomy.** The time zone field is a part of STACK. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/TimeZoneField`, and gives the locale, the value and the zone of the workspace. The part also exports `currentZone`, which gives the current name of a renamed zone (see Variants). The root of the part is the `field` (`time-zone-field`), which holds the label, the trigger, the description and the `field-error`. `Popover.Trigger` is an `outline` button with `w-full border-input font-normal`, so that it takes the width, the edge and the weight of a field. It shows the name of the chosen zone, its offset in `muted-foreground` and a 16 px up-down chevron in `muted-foreground`, or a placeholder in `muted-foreground`. `Popover.Content` with `p-0` holds a `Command.Root` with the search input and the list. One line under the list names the reference date in `small` and `muted-foreground`, such as "Offsets on 23 Oct 2026". The list holds a group with the zone of the workspace, then a group with every other zone. A row shows the name of the zone, with a space for each underscore, and its offset at the right in `muted-foreground`, which turns `foreground` on the selected row. The chosen row shows the check mark of `command-item`, which `data-checked="true"` turns on.

**Variants.** The list comes from `Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone')`, with two fixes, both measured in Chromium 148 and Node 26. The list holds no UTC, so the field puts UTC first in the group of every other zone. The list holds ten zones by an old name from the section "Alternate names for the same location" of the file `backward` of the IANA database (tzdb 2026e): Africa/Asmera, America/Godthab, Asia/Calcutta, Asia/Katmandu, Asia/Rangoon, Asia/Saigon, Atlantic/Faeroe, Europe/Kiev, Pacific/Ponape and Pacific/Truk. The field shows and stores the current name of each, such as Asia/Kolkata, and keeps the old city as a keyword. `Intl` accepts the current name, but `resolvedOptions().timeZone` still returns the old one: Europe/Kyiv gives Europe/Kiev, and a browser in the zone of India gives Asia/Calcutta (measured in Chromium 148 and Node 26). So the product gives every name that the field did not pick, a stored one or the zone of the browser, to `currentZone` before it stores the name. The part maps `value` and `workspace` only for display: it shows the current name and checks its row, and it writes `value` only when the person picks a zone (measured). The offset of a zone changes with daylight saving time, so the field computes each offset for one reference date: today, or the date that the form sets, such as the first run of a schedule. `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `timeZoneName: 'shortOffset'` writes the offset in the format of the locale, such as GMT+2 and GMT+5:30.

**Sizes.** The trigger is 32 px high, as `button`, and it fills the width of its container. The popover is 288 px wide (`w-72`), the search field and each row are 32 px high, and the list scrolls from 288 px. The offsets line up at the right of the rows, in front of the space of the check mark (measured).

**States.** Open and closed: as on `popover`. When the list opens, the chosen zone is the selected row, in view (`value` on `Command.Root`, measured). Selected: the row under the keys takes the `muted` ground, as on `command`. Empty: a trigger with no zone shows the placeholder, and a search with no match shows `command-empty`. Invalid: `error` sets `aria-invalid="true"` on the trigger, which draws the invalid edge of `button`, and the part shows the error in a `field-error` under the trigger. Disabled: `disabled` draws the trigger at 50% opacity, and a click does not open it (measured).

**Tokens.** The field has no key of its own. The trigger takes the edge of `input`, and `button-invalid` when it is invalid. The list takes `popover-content`, `command-input`, `command-item` and `command-item-selected`. On a phone the field takes `popover-trigger-phone`, `command-input-phone` and `command-item-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The part gives the popover the `dialog` role and the label "Choose a time zone", as `### popover` says. It gives the trigger `aria-labelledby` with the `id` of the label and its own `id`: a label with only `for` names the button "Time zone", and the person does not hear the zone. With both, the name is "Time zone Europe/Belgrade GMT+2" (measured). It gives the trigger an `aria-describedby` with the `id` of the description and of the `field-error`, as `### field` asks, and the trigger reads the error after its name (measured). Bits UI gives the search the combobox role, the list the listbox role and each row the option role. The arrow keys move the selection, Enter picks the zone and closes the popover, and the focus returns to the trigger (measured). Esc closes the popover. The search matches the name and the keywords of a row: the city, such as New York for America/New_York, and the old city of a renamed zone, such as Calcutta for Asia/Kolkata (measured).

**Example.**

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import TimeZoneField, { currentZone } from '@stack-kit/stack/parts/TimeZoneField';
  // A stored name, or the zone of the browser, goes through currentZone before the product stores it.
  let zone = $state(currentZone(savedZone ?? new Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone));
</script>

<TimeZoneField locale="en-GB" workspace="Europe/Belgrade" bind:value={zone} {error} />
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the trigger is 44 px high (`popover-trigger-phone`), the search field is 44 px high with 16 px text (`command-input-phone`), and each row is 44 px high (`command-item-phone`). The popover stays 288 px wide, which fits a screen of 390 px (measured at 390 px).

### table

**Purpose.** A table shows records in columns that the person compares, such as the invoices of a month or the values under a chart. Use it when the columns carry the meaning. A list that the person scans and opens item by item is the list of `## Layout`, with rows of 32 px and no header row.

**Anatomy.** `Table.Root` renders a `table-container` that scrolls along the x axis, and in it the `table` in `body` and `foreground`. The `flow-table` and `table-tree` parts of STACK put a root `div` around `Table.Root`, with the slot `flow-table` or `table-tree`. It takes the class, the other attributes and `ref` of the product, and the `table` keeps its slot `table`. `Table.Header` (`table-header`) and `Table.Body` (`table-body`) hold `table-row` rows with a `border` line under each row, except the last row of the body. A header row holds `table-head` cells in `body` at 500, a body row holds `table-cell` cells with 8 px of padding, and `Table.Footer` (`table-footer`) is a band of `muted` at 50% with a `border` line on top, in `body` at 500. `Table.Caption` (`table-caption`) sits under the table in `body` and `muted-foreground`.

**Variants.** Nova has none. A column of numbers aligns right (`text-right`) with tabular figures (`tabular-nums`), and each amount has the same number of decimals, so that the decimal separators align. An amount in a row is 500 (`font-medium`) in the format of the locale, and the currency code after it is `muted-foreground` at 400. A negative amount takes a true minus sign (U+2212) in `foreground`, never `destructive`, and a zero is `muted-foreground`. A cell where the row has no such value holds an em dash (U+2014) in `muted-foreground` with `aria-hidden="true"`, and it never stays empty. An empty cell looks like a value that did not load. A hidden text after the dash (`sr-only`) names the gap, such as "Does not apply". An en dash looks like the minus sign of a negative amount, so the mark is the em dash. The part `Gap` (`@stack-kit/stack/parts/Gap`) holds the dash and the hidden text: the page gives it the text as `reason`, and no class. A sortable column holds a ghost `button` of size `sm` with an arrow in its head. A group row holds the rows of one kind together, such as the rules that come with the product and the rules that a person wrote. Each group is a `Table.Body` of its own, and its first row holds one `Table.Head` with a `colspan` over every column. The head holds the name of the group in `body` at 600 (`font-semibold`) and `foreground`, then one short line of why in `muted-foreground` at 400, 8 px after the name (`<span class="ml-2 font-normal text-muted-foreground">`). The row takes `muted` at 50% (`class="bg-muted/50"`), as the footer does. It does not take the `muted` ground of the group header of `### Lists`, because in a table `muted` marks a selected row.

A tree holds records in up to three levels, such as the permissions of a role, the accounts of a ledger or categories. Each row of a tree is a record with a cell in each column. So a parent row holds a control or a value in each column, which a group row cannot. A row at level 1 starts a `Table.Body` of its own, as a group row does. A parent row is a row at level 1, or a row with child rows. At every level it takes the ground of a group row, `muted` at 50%, and its name in `body` at 600. The nova row draws `muted` at 50% while a control in it has `aria-expanded="true"`. So an open fold button gives its row that ground in any case. A child row with no child rows of its own takes the ground of a row and its name at 400. A parent row may hold one short note after its name, as a group row does, such as "every part". The note says that its boxes act on the rows under it. The tree is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/TableTree`, and gives the rows in tree order, the set of the folded rows and the cells of each row.

Fold: a parent row with child rows holds a fold button at the start of its first cell. The button holds a chevron of 16 px in `muted-foreground`, then the name. The chevron points down while the rows under it show, and right while they are folded, as on the group header of `### Lists`. A folded parent row hides each row under it. A parent row under it keeps its own fold, and that fold comes back with it. A row at level 1 with no child rows has no fold button. The product decides which parent rows start folded.

Set by a parent row: a box of a parent row acts on its own row and on each row under it with the value. It also acts on a row that a person adds later. Its state shows the branch. It is checked while the parent row holds the value, and mixed (`indeterminate`) while only some rows under it hold it. It is unchecked while no row of the branch holds it. While a parent row holds the value, each box under it is a read-only box that is checked (see `### checkbox`). Its reason names the highest parent row that holds the value, because the person changes the value there, such as "Set by Sales. Change it in the Sales row." A child row keeps its own value under the lock, and that value shows again when the parent row clears its box. A press on a mixed box sets the value on the parent row. A second press clears it, and the box is mixed again while rows under it still hold the value (measured).

**Sizes.** A header cell is 40 px high (`spacing.table-header`, `h-10`) with 8 px of padding at the sides, and so is the head of a group row. A body row is 37 px high: a line of 20 px, 8 px of padding above and below, and the 1 px line (measured). A cell does not wrap (`whitespace-nowrap`), and a cell with a check box has no padding at its right. In a tree, a parent row at level 1 is 40 px high, as the head of a group row. Each other row is 37 px high (measured at 1440 px). Below 768 px the header row and each row of a tree are 44 px high, as the Phone part gives it. Each level adds 24 px (`spacing.space-6`) at the start of the first cell. The chevron of a parent row at level 1 and 2 starts at 8 and 32 px. Its name starts 24 px later, after the chevron of 16 px and a gap of 8 px. A row with no child rows has no chevron. Its name starts at 32, 56 or 80 px at level 1, 2 or 3. At level 1 and 2 it stands under the names of the parent rows of its level (measured). A tree holds at most three levels. A fourth level puts a name at 104 px, and the person cannot follow which parent row a row belongs to. So a product splits a deeper tree, such as into a page for each row at level 1. The fold button is 20 px high, and an invisible area adds 10 px above and below it and 8 px at each side. So its target is 40 px high (measured: 75 by 40 px for a button of 58 by 20 px).

**States.** Selected: the product sets `data-state="selected"` on the row, which draws the `muted` ground, and the check box in the first cell shows the same state. Hover draws `muted` at 50%, and a row that holds an open menu (`aria-expanded`) keeps that ground. Sorted: the product sets `aria-sort="ascending"` or `"descending"` on the `<th>`, and the arrow in its button points the same way. A group row has no hover and no selection: its ground is already the hover ground, and the product never sets `data-state` on it. In a tree, a parent row has no hover either, for the same reason, and a child row has the hover of a row. Each row of a tree is a record, so each row can take `data-state="selected"`, which draws `muted` over the ground of a parent row too. The selection of a parent row holds that row only, and the rows under it keep their selection. Folded: the fold button takes `aria-expanded="false"`, and each row under it takes `hidden`. Enter and Space on the fold button fold and unfold the rows, and the focus stays on the button (measured). `architecture/web-app.md` lists these keys of the component. The chevron turns in `motion.duration-fast` with `motion.ease-stack`, and it jumps under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`.

**Tokens.** `table`, `table-caption`, `table-row`, `table-row-selected`, `table-head`, `table-cell`, `table-footer`. The ground of the footer, the ground of a group row and the hover ground are muted at 50%, so they have no key. A tree adds no key of its own. The ground of a parent row is muted at 50%, and the chevron and the dash take the muted-foreground role. A box that a parent row sets takes `checkbox-readonly`.

**Accessibility.** The native `<table>` gives each cell its row and its column, so a screen reader reads the head of the column with each cell. The head of a group row takes `scope="rowgroup"`. By the table model of HTML, a row group header is a header of every cell in its `<tbody>`, as the head of a column is a header of every cell under it. `Table.Caption` names the table. A sortable head sets `aria-sort` and its button names the action, such as "Sort by amount", and the check box of a row names its record, such as "Select INV-0042". A tree stays a table, and it takes no treegrid role. A treegrid is one tab stop, and the product writes its keys. The arrow keys, Home and End move between the cells, and Right and Left unfold and fold a row. These keys then lose their work in the browser. In the tree of the preview at 390 px, Right and Left on a fold button scrolled the table sideways by 40 px. Down scrolled the page (measured). A table also gives each cell its headers for a screen reader: the head of its column, of its row and of its row group. So the name cell at level 1 takes `scope="rowgroup"`, as the head of a group row, and each other name cell takes `scope="row"`. Chromium gives both the rowheader role (measured). A table gives a row no level, so the indent shows the level, and each box names its row. Tab moves through the fold buttons and the boxes, row by row. The tree of the preview has 58 tab stops, and 27 with Sales folded (measured). A box of a parent row sets a whole branch with one press. The fold button is a `<button>` with `aria-expanded`, and `aria-controls` names the ids of its child rows. Its name is the name of its row, such as "Sales", and Chromium gives it the state expanded or collapsed (measured). A row under a folded row takes `hidden`, so it leaves the tab order and the accessibility tree (measured). A box names its value and its row, such as "Change Drafts". A box of a parent row names the branch, such as "Change all of Sales". A mixed box gives `aria-checked="mixed"` (measured). A box that a parent row sets takes `readonly` and `aria-disabled="true"`, and it stays in the tab order (see `### checkbox`). Its reason is in a `tooltip` that opens on hover and on keyboard focus, and in `aria-describedby` (measured). This follows a button that is disabled with a reason. The box is the trigger of the tooltip through its child snippet, which gives the box `data-slot="tooltip-trigger"`. So the box sets `data-slot="checkbox"` after the props. Without it, `stack.css` made the box 44 px high below 768 px, and its row grew from 37 to 61 px (measured).

**Example.**

```svelte
<Table.Root>
  <Table.Caption>Invoices of October 2026.</Table.Caption>
  <Table.Header>
    <Table.Row>
      <Table.Head>Invoice</Table.Head>
      <Table.Head aria-sort="descending" class="text-right">
        <Button variant="ghost" size="sm" aria-label="Sort by amount">Amount <ArrowDownIcon /></Button>
      </Table.Head>
    </Table.Row>
  </Table.Header>
  <Table.Body>
    {#each invoices as row (row.id)}
      <Table.Row data-state={selected.has(row.id) ? 'selected' : undefined}>
        <Table.Cell class="font-medium">INV-<span class="tabular-nums">{row.number}</span></Table.Cell>
        <Table.Cell class="text-right tabular-nums"><span class="font-medium">{money.format(row.total)}</span> <span class="text-muted-foreground">{row.currency}</span></Table.Cell>
      </Table.Row>
    {/each}
  </Table.Body>
</Table.Root>

<!-- A tree: a Table.Body for each row at level 1, in a Table.Root with class="max-md:[&_[data-slot=checkbox]]:after:-inset-[15px] max-md:[&_tr>:first-child:has([data-slot=checkbox])]:pl-[14px]". The Table.Row of its Table.Header takes class="max-md:h-[44px]". The first cell starts at pl-2 or pl-8 for a parent row at level 1 or 2, and at pl-8, pl-14 or pl-20 for a row with no child rows at level 1, 2 or 3. -->
<Table.Body>
  <Table.Row id="row-sales" class="bg-muted/50">
    <Table.Head scope="rowgroup" class="pl-2 font-semibold max-md:h-[44px]">
      <button type="button" data-slot="table-fold" aria-expanded={open} aria-controls="row-quotes row-customers" onclick={() => (open = !open)} class="relative inline-flex items-baseline gap-2 rounded-sm font-semibold outline-none after:absolute after:-inset-x-2 after:-inset-y-2.5 max-md:after:-inset-y-[12px] focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 print:after:hidden">
        <ChevronRightIcon aria-hidden="true" class={cn('size-4 shrink-0 self-center text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-fast ease-stack motion-reduce:transition-none', open && 'rotate-90')} />
        Sales
      </button>
      <span class="ml-2 font-normal text-muted-foreground">every part</span>
    </Table.Head>
    <Table.Cell><Checkbox aria-label="See all of Sales" checked={held} indeterminate={!held && some} onCheckedChange={(on) => grant('sales', 'see', on)} /></Table.Cell>
    <!-- A cell with no value. -->
    <Table.Cell><Gap reason="Does not apply" /></Table.Cell>
  </Table.Row>
  <Table.Row id="row-quotes" hidden={!open}>
    <Table.Head scope="row" class="h-auto py-2 pl-14 font-normal max-md:h-[44px]">Quotes</Table.Head>
    <Table.Cell><HeldCheckbox checked reason="Set by Sales. Change it in the Sales row." aria-label="See Quotes" /></Table.Cell>
    <Table.Cell><Gap reason="Does not apply" /></Table.Cell>
  </Table.Row>
</Table.Body>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the text takes the 15 px `body` step, and the header cells stay 40 px and the rows 37 px, except in a tree (measured). A table that is wider than the screen scrolls inside its `table-container`, and the page does not move: at 390 px a table of 559 px scrolled inside a container of 358 px (measured). A list of records shows rows of two lines below 768 px, as `## Layout` says for lists, because a check box in a cell stays 16 px (measured) and no key reaches it, so the whole row is the target there. A tree stays a table below 768 px. It scrolls inside its `table-container`, and each level keeps its 24 px. At 390 px the tree of the preview was 488 px wide in a container of 358 px (measured). Below 768 px each row of a tree is 44 px high (`max-md:h-[44px]` on each name cell, measured). The header row of a tree is 44 px high too (`max-md:h-[44px]` on its `Table.Row`, measured). So the `table-container` does not clip the target of a box in the header row, such as "Select all", at its top, and the target of the first box under it does not cover it at its bottom. A tap reaches the whole 44 by 44 px (measured). Each box takes `max-md:after:-inset-[15px]` through the class of `Table.Root`, so its target is 44 by 44 px, as "Touch targets" in `### Width bands` asks. Through the same class, a first cell that holds a box takes 14 px of padding at its start (`max-md:pl-[14px]`). So the `table-container` does not clip the target at its left edge, and a tap reaches the whole width of 44 px (measured). The fold button takes `max-md:after:-inset-y-[12px]`, so its target is 44 px high (measured). A column that holds boxes is at least 44 px wide (`min-w-[44px]` on its head), so two targets do not overlap. The text takes the 15 px `body` step (measured). A tap shows no tooltip, so the reason of a read-only box does not show as text on a touch screen. The look of the box and the checked box of its parent row, in the same column, show where the value comes from. A screen reader reads the reason as the description.

**Print.** A folded parent row prints its own row only, as on the screen, and the rows under it do not print. The person folds a branch on purpose, as a column of a `board`. The box of the parent row still prints the state of the branch. A mixed box prints its minus, and the chevron prints and points right. To print each row, the person unfolds the rows first. With Reports folded, the PDFs from 1440 and 390 px held no row under Reports. Unfolded, the PDF from 1440 px held both rows (measured on A4). The ground of a parent row does not print (see `### A screen page in print`), so a parent row prints with its name at 600 and its chevron. Each checked box prints its check, a read-only box too (see `### checkbox`). The em dash prints, and its hidden text does not (measured with `pdftotext`).

### selection-bar

**Purpose.** A selection bar holds the count of the selected rows of a list and the actions for them. It takes the place of the content of the top bar while one or more rows are selected, and the top bar comes back when the selection clears. So no row moves when the first row is selected (measured), and the next click lands on the row under the pointer. The command palette lists the same actions first, as `architecture/web-app.md` says. The selection bar is a part of STACK and not of the registry, so a release ships no folder for it in `preview/src/lib/components/ui/`. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/SelectionBar` and gives it the count, the actions and the functions of the page. The part draws its look from `checkbox`, `button`, `tooltip` and `dropdown-menu`, and an action with a lasting effect opens the `alert-dialog` of the page. Its source is `preview/src/lib/stack/SelectionBar.svelte`. While the bar shows, Esc runs the `onclear` of the page after an open layer, such as the More menu, closes (`onEscape('selection')` of `@stack-kit/stack/behaviour/keys`).

**Anatomy.** While one or more rows are selected, the bar claims the `FramePlace` with the name `top-bar` from 768 px and the one with the name `phone-header` below (`bands.md` of `@stack-kit/stack/behaviour/width`), so one bar shows and no row moves. With a count of 0 the bar shows nothing, and the page keeps it in the page, so that it can say "Selection cleared" (see Accessibility). The `selection-bar` slot fills the top bar: 44 px high, on the `background` ground, and the top bar keeps its `border` edge below it. From the left it holds the check box of the page, the count (`selection-bar-count`) and the button that selects every row of the filter. On the right it holds the actions, the More menu and Clear. The check box of the page sits 12 px from the left edge, above the check boxes of the rows (measured). The count is `body` at 500 with tabular figures, such as "3 selected". Each action is an `outline` button of size `sm` with its icon and its verb, as a button of the top bar is. More is an `outline` icon button of size `icon-sm` that opens a `dropdown-menu`. Clear is a `ghost` icon button of size `icon-sm` with the X icon, and it comes last, as the close button of a pane header does. A row of filter chips under the top bar stays while the selection bar shows. The actions, More and Clear sit in `selection-bar-actions`, and More and Clear sit in `selection-bar-more` and `selection-bar-clear`.

**Variants.** At most three actions show as buttons, in the order of their use, and More holds the rest. A destructive action, such as Delete, is the last item of More, after a separator, as `### dropdown-menu` says. The check box of the page selects every row that the list has loaded, which is the page. It is checked when every row of the page is selected, and indeterminate when only some are. A click on it selects every row of the page, or clears the selection when every row of the page is selected. When it clears the selection, the focus moves to the row that had it last, as Clear does. When every row of the page is selected and the filter matches more rows, the count says "All 8 on this page selected". A `ghost` button of size `sm` after it says "Select all 214". After a click the count says "All 214 selected", the button goes, and the focus moves to the check box of the page (measured). An action then runs on every row of the filter, on the server. After Select all, a clear of one row keeps the other rows of the page selected and drops the rows that the list has not loaded, so the count says "7 selected". With no `FramePlace` of that name, the bar renders where the page puts it, and it says the count in its own live region. So a list with no top bar, such as a list in a card, puts the bar in its own header and shows its title only at a count of 0. The `menu` snippet adds items to More, after the actions and before a destructive action.

**Sizes.** The bar is 44 px high (`spacing.top-bar`, `h-11`), with 12 px of padding on the left, 16 px on the right and 8 px between its parts. The buttons are 28 px high, and More and Clear are 28 by 28 px.

**States.** The bar shows while one row or more is selected, and each control keeps its own states. Blocked: when an action does not apply to every selected row, it is disabled with a reason, as `### button` says, and it never runs on a part of the selection. The reason names the rule and the count, such as "Mark as paid takes sent invoices only, and 1 of the 3 is not sent." Chromium gives the button the name "Mark as paid", the state disabled and that description, and a click runs nothing (measured). An action that takes the rows out of the list, such as Archive or Delete, clears the selection. The focus then moves to the row that takes the place of the first row that left (measured). An action that keeps the rows, such as Mark as paid, keeps the selection and the focus, so that a second action can follow (measured). An action that Undo reverses runs at once and shows a `sonner` toast with Undo, such as "2 invoices archived". Undo puts the rows back, selects them again and moves the focus to the first of them (measured). An action that no Undo reverses opens an `alert-dialog` first, as "Actions with a lasting effect" in `architecture/web-app.md` says. Its title names the verb and the count, such as "Delete 2 invoices?", and its action button repeats them. Its description names up to three rows and the count of the rest, or the filter when every row of the filter is selected. The dialog keeps its words while it closes. Cancel gives the focus back to More. After the action the live region names the result, such as "2 invoices deleted", and no toast shows (measured). Clear and Esc clear the selection, and the focus moves to the row that had it last (measured). Esc clears the selection when no layer and no pane is open, as the shared key of `architecture/web-app.md` says. In the work form, Esc clears the selection before it closes the item column (see "Esc and Close" in ### Detail pane). Shift with a click or with Space on the check box of a row selects every row from the last row that the person selected or cleared, as Shift+X does. The keys X, Shift+X, Shift+Down, Shift+Up and Cmd+A of `architecture/web-app.md` work on the row that has the focus (measured in the showcase). The selection holds or clears as `### Selection` of `architecture/web-app.md` says.

**Tokens.** `selection-bar`, `selection-bar-count`, `selection-bar-phone`. The bar keeps the ground and the edge of the top bar, so it adds no color pair. The actions take the keys of `button`, and More takes the keys of the dropdown menu, such as `dropdown-menu-trigger-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The bar has `role="group"`, and `aria-labelledby` points to the count, so Chromium names the group "1 selected" (measured). A polite live region (`role="status"`) says the count after each change: "1 selected", "3 selected", "All 8 on this page selected", "All 214 selected" and "Selection cleared" (measured). In a `FramePlace`, the region of the place says the count, and the bar adds no region (measured). With no `FramePlace` of that name, the bar says the count in its own region, which is visually hidden (measured in a card). The page holds no live region for the count in either form. Each region is in the page before the first row is selected, because a screen reader can miss a live region that comes into the page with its text. So the page keeps the bar in the page at a count of 0. With no `FramePlace`, the root of the bar then holds only its region, in a box of 1 by 1 px (measured). The bar says "Selection cleared" only when the count falls to 0. The check box of the page names what it selects, such as "Select all 8 on this page", and the check box of each row names its row, as `### checkbox` says. Clear has the name "Clear the selection", `aria-keyshortcuts="Escape"` and a tooltip with its key. When the bar leaves, the `onclear` of the page moves the focus to the row that had it last, so the focus never falls to the page.

**Example.**

```svelte
<!-- The frame: each header wraps its content in a FramePlace, and the place holds the live region. -->
<header class="hidden h-11 items-stretch border-b border-border md:flex">
  <FramePlace name="top-bar"><!-- The breadcrumbs, the view tabs, the filter and the display controls. --></FramePlace>
</header>
<header class="flex h-13 items-center border-b border-border md:hidden">
  <FramePlace name="phone-header"><!-- The back button, the title and the actions. --></FramePlace>
</header>

<!-- The page of the list: no header swap and no live region. The bar stays in the page at a count of 0. -->
<script lang="ts">
  // The control of the row that had the focus last. The bar leaves when the selection clears, so the focus goes back there.
  let lastRow: HTMLElement | null = null;
  const onfocusin = (e: FocusEvent) => {
    if (e.target instanceof HTMLElement && e.target.closest('li[data-key]')) lastRow = e.target;
  };
  async function clear() {
    selection = [];
    await tick();
    if (lastRow?.isConnected) lastRow.focus();
  }
</script>

<svelte:document {onfocusin} />
<SelectionBar count={selection.length} page={rows.length} {total} {all} {actions} onpage={selectPage} onall={selectAll} onclear={clear} />

<!-- In SelectionBar.svelte: the check box of the page, the count, Select all, a blocked action, an action, More and Clear. -->
<div data-slot="selection-bar" role="group" aria-labelledby="{id}-count" class="flex h-11 min-w-0 flex-1 items-center gap-2 bg-background pr-4 pl-3 text-sm max-md:gap-1 max-md:px-1">
  <Checkbox id="{id}-page" aria-label="Select all 8 on this page" bind:checked={() => pageAll, (on) => (on ? onpage() : onclear())} bind:indeterminate={() => some, () => {}} />
  <span id="{id}-count" data-slot="selection-bar-count" class="min-w-0 truncate font-medium tabular-nums">All 8 on this page selected</span>
  <Button variant="ghost" size="sm" class="tabular-nums" onclick={selectAll}>Select all 214</Button>
  <div class="ml-auto flex shrink-0 items-center gap-2">
    <Tooltip.Root>
      <Tooltip.Trigger class={cn(buttonVariants({ variant: 'outline', size: 'sm' }), 'aria-disabled:cursor-not-allowed aria-disabled:opacity-50')} aria-disabled="true" aria-describedby="{id}-paid-reason" onclick={(e) => e.preventDefault()}>
        <CircleCheckIcon data-icon="inline-start" />Mark as paid
      </Tooltip.Trigger>
      <Tooltip.Content>{reason}</Tooltip.Content>
    </Tooltip.Root>
    <span id="{id}-paid-reason" class="hidden">{reason}</span>
    <Button variant="outline" size="sm" onclick={archive}><ArchiveIcon data-icon="inline-start" />Archive</Button>
    <DropdownMenu.Root>
      <DropdownMenu.Trigger id="{id}-more" class={buttonVariants({ variant: 'outline', size: 'icon-sm' })} aria-label="More actions"><EllipsisIcon /></DropdownMenu.Trigger>
      <DropdownMenu.Content align="end" class="w-64">
        <DropdownMenu.Item><PrinterIcon />Print</DropdownMenu.Item>
        <DropdownMenu.Separator />
        <DropdownMenu.Item variant="destructive" onSelect={askDelete}><Trash2Icon />Delete…</DropdownMenu.Item>
      </DropdownMenu.Content>
    </DropdownMenu.Root>
    <Button variant="ghost" size="icon-sm" aria-label="Clear the selection" aria-keyshortcuts="Escape" onclick={clear}><XIcon /></Button>
  </div>
</div>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the bar takes the place of the 52 px header (`selection-bar-phone`). It holds Clear on the left, in the place of the back button, then the count, then More on the right. Clear and More are 44 by 44 px (measured at 390 px). Every action is an item of More, and so are the two items that select the page and the filter. A blocked action is an item with its reason as a second line, as `### dropdown-menu` says, and it is 64 px high with the phone type step (measured). The bar has no room for the reason line of a button, so on a touch screen from 768 px (`pointer: coarse`) every action is an item of More too. A phone has no hover, so a long press of 500 ms on a row selects it and shows the check boxes. The row shows no callout of the system (`-webkit-touch-callout: none`). While a row is selected, a tap on a row selects or clears it (measured with touch events and a press of 600 ms at 390 px). The check box of each row stays in the page at opacity 0, so a screen reader reaches it.

### reorder-list

**Purpose.** A reorder list holds items in an order that the person sets, such as the steps of a sequence, the stages of a pipeline or the columns of a view. Each row has a handle that drags with a pointer and lifts with Space. Its More menu holds Move up and Move down, so that each move also works with one click or tap and no drag (WCAG 2.2 success criterion 2.5.7, Dragging Movements, level AA). A list that sorts by a field shows no handle, because a move there has no meaning. A move from one column of a board to another changes a value of the item, and `### board` gives it with the same handle, keys and words. The reorder list is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/ReorderList`, and gives the items, the content of a row and the function that saves the order. STACK builds the part from `button`, `tooltip`, `dropdown-menu` and `sonner`, and from the Pointer events of the browser with no drag library (see Accessibility). The toast of a move offers Undo, and Cmd+Z runs it too while the toast shows (`### sonner`). A rule of the product can stop a move with a sentence that gives the reason (`blocked`). A save that fails puts the row back. The product adds its own items to More, before the moves (`menu`). Fixed rows stand before the items with no handle (`fixed`), and a list that the person may not change shows no handle and no More (`readonly`).

**Anatomy.** The `reorder-list-wrapper` slot is a `<div>` on `background` that holds the `<ol>` of the rows (`reorder-list`). Each `reorder-list-item` is an `<li>` that keeps the place of its row, and it holds the `reorder-list-row`: a row of `### Lists`, with a `border-faint` divider below. The row holds the handle (`reorder-list-handle`) on the left, the content of the product, and a More button on the right. The handle is a `ghost` icon button of size `icon-xs` with the 16 px grip icon in `muted-foreground`, and it turns `foreground` on hover. A position number before the content is optional. A `tooltip` on the handle shows "Move", the name of the item and the key, Space. Two lines that do not show come before the `<ol>`: the keys of the handle, which each handle takes as its description, and a polite live region.

**Variants.** None. One gap is open: the preview holds no list that scrolls, so this file has no measured rule yet for a drag near the top or the bottom edge of such a list.

**Sizes.** The row is 32 px high (`spacing.row`, `h-8`), with 8 px of padding on the left and 16 px on the right. The handle and the More button are 24 by 24 px (`spacing.control-xs`, measured). The drop line is 2 px high (`spacing.space-0-5`, `h-0.5`) and as wide as the list.

**States.** Lifted: the product sets `[data-lifted]` on the row, and on its item with the value `pointer` or `key`. A press on the handle lifts the row to level 3 of `## Elevation & Depth`, with the `popover` ground, the `foreground/10` ring, `shadow-md` and the 8 px radius. The row follows the pointer up and down inside the list, its slot stays on the `muted` ground, and the other rows stand still. The drop line, `foreground` (`reorder-list-drop-line`), shows where the row lands. On the way up, the row lands before the row that its top edge is over. On the way down, it lands after the row that its bottom edge is over. Both apply once the row has moved 8 px. So the line is always outside the lifted row (measured). A line at the middle of the row is under the lifted row, which covers half a row on each side of its middle. The handle shows the `grab` cursor at rest and `grabbing` while its row is lifted (measured). The release drops the row. Esc puts it back (measured), and so does a cancel of the pointer. With the keyboard, Space or Enter on the handle lifts the row in its place, with the same look. The up and down arrow keys move it one place, and Home and End move it to the first and the last place. The other rows change places at once, so no drop line shows. Space or Enter drops the row, and Esc or a focus that leaves the handle puts it back (measured). The tooltip closes while a row is lifted, so that it covers no row on the way (measured). Saved: a drop and a move from More save the order at once, and a `sonner` toast says "Order saved" with Undo. One toast serves the list: a new move replaces it. Its Undo puts back the order before the last move and moves the focus to the handle of the row (measured). A save that fails keeps the order of the person, and the line of a failed write of `architecture/web-app.md` shows under the row. Move up on the first row and Move down on the last row are `disabled`, with no reason to give. Motion: the lift changes the ground and the shadow in `motion.duration-fast` with `motion.ease-stack`. It moves nothing, so it stays under reduced motion. A row that changes its place slides there in `motion.duration-fast` with `motion.ease-stack`, and a dropped row slides from the place where the pointer left it (measured: 0.12 s and `cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 0, 1)`). Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the rows jump to their places (measured). The lifted row follows the pointer with no transition in both settings, because the person moves it.

**Tokens.** `reorder-list`, `reorder-list-item-lifted`, `reorder-list-row-lifted`, `reorder-list-handle`, `reorder-list-handle-phone`, `reorder-list-drop-line`. The lifted row takes the pairs of the popover role, and the drop line the pairs of the foreground role (see Colors), so the part adds no color pair. The ring of the lifted row carries an opacity, so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** The list is an `<ol>` with `role="list"` and an `aria-label` that names it, such as "Steps of the sequence". The role stays explicit, because Safari drops the list role of a list with no markers (not measured). Each handle is a button with the name "Move" and the name of its item, such as "Move Send the setup guide". Its description gives the keys: "Press Space to lift it. Then the up and down arrow keys move it, Space drops it, and Escape puts it back." (measured in Chromium). The polite live region says each step: "Lifted Send the setup guide. Position 3 of 8.", "Moved to position 2 of 8.", "Already at position 1 of 8.", "Dropped at position 5 of 8.", "Move cancelled. Back at position 3 of 8." and "Undone. Send the setup guide is back at position 3 of 8." (measured). The handle keeps the focus through each move with the keys, and after a drop with the pointer (measured). While a row is lifted, its handle stops the arrow keys, Home, End, Space, Enter and Esc. So a key handler of the list or of the window does not move the focus, close the pane or clear the selection (measured with a listener on the window). `architecture/web-app.md` lists these keys of the component. The drag uses the Pointer events of the browser, so a mouse, a pen and a finger take one path, and the product needs no drag library. A drag library moves the rows itself and says its own words, and this file sets the keys and the words of the live region.

**Example.**

```svelte
<ReorderList bind:items={steps} label="Steps of the sequence" name={(s) => s.label} onsave={save}>
  {#snippet item(s, i)}
    <span aria-hidden="true" class="w-4 shrink-0 text-right text-xs text-muted-foreground tabular-nums">{i + 1}</span>
    <span class="truncate font-medium">{s.label}</span>
  {/snippet}
</ReorderList>

<!-- In ReorderList.svelte: the item keeps the slot, the row lifts, the line shows where the row lands, and the handle drags and lifts. -->
<li data-slot="reorder-list-item" data-lifted={lifted?.by} class="relative data-[lifted=pointer]:bg-muted">
  <!-- On the way down the line takes bottom-0 translate-y-1/2. -->
  <span data-slot="reorder-list-drop-line" aria-hidden="true" class="pointer-events-none absolute inset-x-0 top-0 z-20 h-0.5 -translate-y-1/2 bg-foreground"></span>
  <div data-slot="reorder-list-row" data-lifted={up || undefined} class="relative flex h-8 items-center gap-3 border-b border-border-faint pr-4 pl-2 text-sm transition-[background-color,box-shadow] duration-fast ease-stack data-lifted:z-10 data-lifted:rounded-lg data-lifted:border-transparent data-lifted:bg-popover data-lifted:text-popover-foreground data-lifted:shadow-md data-lifted:ring-1 data-lifted:ring-foreground/10 max-md:h-13">
    <Tooltip.Root disabled={lifted !== null} open={lifted ? false : undefined}>
      <Tooltip.Trigger {onkeydown} {onpointerdown} {onpointermove} {onpointerup} {onpointercancel}>
        {#snippet child({ props })}
          <Button {...props} data-slot="reorder-list-handle" variant="ghost" size="icon-xs" class="cursor-grab touch-none text-muted-foreground hover:text-foreground in-data-lifted:cursor-grabbing print:hidden" aria-label="Move {name}" aria-describedby={hint}><GripVerticalIcon class="size-4" /></Button>
        {/snippet}
      </Tooltip.Trigger>
      <Tooltip.Content>Move {name} <Kbd>Space</Kbd></Tooltip.Content>
    </Tooltip.Root>
    <!-- The content of the product, then More with Move up and Move down. -->
  </div>
</li>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the row is 52 px high (`spacing.row-phone`, `max-md:h-13`). The handle is 44 by 44 px (`reorder-list-handle-phone`), and so is the More button (`dropdown-menu-trigger-phone`, measured at 390 px). The handle takes `touch-action: none` (`touch-none`), so a finger on it drags the row and the page does not scroll. A finger on the rest of the row scrolls the page. With touch events, a drag of 90 px on the handle moved the row from position 2 to position 4, and the page did not scroll (measured at 390 px). More holds Move up and Move down for a person who does not drag, and its items are 44 px high (measured).

### board

**Purpose.** A board shows the items of a list in columns, one column for each value of one field, such as the stage of a lead or the status of a ticket. The person sees how many items each value holds, and moves an item to another value with a pointer, with the keys or with a menu. A move to another column changes that field, and a move inside a column changes the order that the person sets. A board is the right view when the field has a short fixed set of values in a known order, such as the stages of a pipeline. Each column then holds tens of items, not hundreds. A board is the wrong view when the person compares items by their fields, which a `table` does, or scans for one item, which the list of `### Lists` does. It is also the wrong view for a field with no order, such as a person or a label, and for a field with more than about eight values. The board is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/Board`, and gives the columns, the content of a card and the function that saves a move. STACK builds the part from `button`, `tooltip`, `dropdown-menu`, `toggle-group`, `scroll-area` and `sonner`. It uses the Pointer events of the browser and no drag library, as `reorder-list` does. The toast of a move offers Undo, and Cmd+Z runs it too while the toast shows (`### sonner`). A rule of the product can keep a card out of a column with a sentence that gives the reason (`blocked`). A save that fails puts the card back. The product adds its own items to More (`menu`).

**Anatomy.** The `board` slot is a region on `background` that scrolls sideways when its columns do not fit, with 12 px of padding and 8 px between the columns. Each `board-column` is a `<section>` 288 px wide (`spacing.board-column`, `w-72`) and as high as the board, on `muted` at 50% with a `border` edge and the 8 px radius. Its ground is never a status color. The column head (`board-column-head`) is 32 px high, as the group header of `### Lists` (`spacing.group-header`). It holds the status icon at 16 px, the name in `body` at 600, and the count (`board-column-count`) in `small` and `muted-foreground` with tabular figures. On its right it holds Fold and the actions of the product, such as New, as ghost icon buttons of 28 px. They show at all times, because a touch screen has no hover. Under the head the cards of the column scroll in a box of their own, 8 px apart, with 8 px of padding at the sides. Each `board-item` is an `<li>` that keeps the place of its card, and it holds the `board-card`. A card is a box at level 2 of `## Elevation & Depth`: the `card` ground, the `foreground/10` ring, the 8 px radius and 8 px of padding. The head line of a card holds the handle (`board-card-handle`), the identifier in `small` at 500, and More on the right. The body under it starts at the left edge of the identifier. It holds the title as a link that covers the card, then at most three fields as badges, then a foot line in `meta`. Two lines that do not show come first in the `board` slot: the keys of the handle, which each handle takes as its description, and a polite live region.

**Variants.** A folded column is a rail (`board-rail`) 40 px wide (`spacing.board-rail`, `w-10`) and as high as the board, with the ground and the edge of a column. It holds the status icon, then the name at 600 and the count, which run from top to bottom. Tailwind 4.3.3 has no class for `writing-mode`, so a style block writes `writing-mode: vertical-rl`, as a style block writes the safe-area insets. The whole rail is one button, and a click, Enter or Space unfolds the column. With no Fold, the person cannot unfold a column, so the rail is a `<section>` with the name and the count, and it is no button and no tab stop (measured). A column may start folded, such as a column of closed items, and the product decides which column does. A folded column takes no drop, and the left and right arrow keys pass over it, so a move never hides the card. Move to in More still reaches it, and the focus then moves to the rail (measured). Limit: a product may set the most cards that a column should hold, and the head then shows the count and the limit, such as "4 / 3". Over the limit, the count turns `foreground`, with a 14 px warning icon in `warning` before it. A limit never blocks a move, and the live region names the column that passes its limit. Sorted: when the display orders the cards of each column by a field, such as the amount, a move changes the column only. The card then lands at its place in that order. Up, Down, Home and End do not move it, and More holds no Move up and no Move down. Cut: a column may hold only its first cards, such as the first 10 of 62. Its head then shows the whole count, and a Show all button follows its cards.

**Sizes.** A column is 288 px wide, and a card in it is 270 px wide (measured). A card with a title of one line, one badge and a foot line is 112 px high (measured). The head is 32 px high and the rail 40 px wide. The handle and More are 24 by 24 px (`spacing.control-xs`), and the buttons of the head are 28 by 28 px. The drop line is 2 px high (`spacing.space-0-5`) and as wide as a card.

**States.** Lifted: the product sets `[data-lifted]` on the card, and on its item with the value `pointer` or `key`, as on `reorder-list`. A press on the handle lifts the card to level 3 of `## Elevation & Depth`: the `popover` ground, the `foreground/10` ring, `shadow-md` and the 8 px radius. The card follows the pointer over the columns as a fixed layer, so that the scroll box of its column does not clip it. It stays under the heads of the columns.
