Its slot keeps the height of the card on the `muted` ground, and the other cards stand still. The column under the middle of the card is the target, and the drop line, `foreground` (`board-drop-line`), shows where the card lands in it. When the card is higher than the place that it came from, it lands before the first card whose bottom is under its top edge. When it is lower, it lands after the last card whose top is over its bottom edge. So the line is always outside the lifted card (measured). In its own column the line shows once the card has moved 8 px, and an empty column shows the line at its top. The release drops the card. Esc puts it back (measured), and so does a cancel of the pointer. With the keyboard, Space or Enter on the handle lifts the card in its place, with the same look. The up and down arrow keys move it one place in its column, and Home and End move it to the first and the last place. The left and right arrow keys move it to the column before or after, at the same position, or last when that column is shorter. The other cards change places at once, so no drop line shows. Space or Enter drops the card, and Esc or a focus that leaves the handle puts it back (measured). The tooltip closes while a card is lifted, so that it covers no card on the way. Saved: a drop and a move from More save at once, and a `sonner` toast names the card and the column, such as "LEAD-0044 moved to Proposal", with Undo (measured). One toast serves the board: a new move replaces it. Its Undo puts the card back and moves the focus to the handle, or to the rail when the column of the card is folded (measured). A save that fails keeps the place of the person, and the line of a failed write of `architecture/web-app.md` shows under the card. Move up on the first card and Move down on the last card are `disabled`, with no reason to give. Motion: a card that changes its place slides there in `motion.duration-fast` with `motion.ease-stack`. A dropped card slides from the place where the pointer left it (measured: 0.12 s and `cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 0, 1)`). Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the cards jump to their places (measured). The lifted card follows the pointer with no transition in both settings. One gap is open, as on `reorder-list`: a drag near the edge of a column or of the board does not scroll it yet.

**Tokens.** `board`, `board-column-head`, `board-column-count`, `board-card`, `board-card-lifted`, `board-card-handle`, `board-item-lifted`, `board-drop-line`, `board-rail`. The ground of a column and of a rail is muted at 50%, and the ring of a card carries an opacity, so they have no key. The warning icon of a limit takes the pair of the icon of a state. The other parts take the pairs of the card, popover, muted and foreground roles (see Colors), so the board adds no color pair.

**Accessibility.** The board is a region with an `aria-label` that names it, such as "Board of the leads". Each column is a `<section>` that its head names with its count, such as "Proposal, 4 leads, over the limit of 3", and its cards are an `<ol>` with `role="list"`. The rail is a button with the name "Unfold Lost, 1 lead" (measured in Chromium). With no Fold, the rail is a section with the name and the count, such as "Lost, 1 lead". Each handle is a button with the name "Move" and the name of its card, such as "Move LEAD-0041". Its description gives the keys: "Press Space to lift it. Then the arrow keys move it in its column and to the column before or after, Space drops it, and Escape puts it back." (measured). The polite live region says each step. The words are "Lifted LEAD-0044. New, position 2 of 3.", "Moved to Contacted, position 1 of 3.", "Already at New, position 1 of 3." and "Already in the last column.". A drop says "Dropped in Proposal, position 2 of 5. Proposal is over its limit of 3.", and Esc says "Move cancelled. Back in New, position 2 of 3.". Undo says "Undone. LEAD-0044 is back in New, position 2 of 3.", and Up on a sorted board says "The cards sort by amount, so a move changes the column only." (all measured). The handle keeps the focus through each move with the keys, also into another column, and after a drop with the pointer (measured). Tab on a lifted card puts the card back. When the control that Tab aimed at went with the card, the focus goes to the handle, so it never falls to the page (measured). While a card is lifted, its handle stops the arrow keys, Home, End, Space, Enter and Esc, as on `reorder-list`, and `architecture/web-app.md` lists these keys of the component. The tab order runs column by column and card by card: the handle, the title and More.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Board bind:columns label="Board of the leads" field="Stage" name={(l) => l.key} count={(n) => `${n} ${n === 1 ? 'lead' : 'leads'}`} onmove={save} onfold={fold}>
  {#snippet icon(c)}<StatusIcon status={c.id} />{/snippet}
  {#snippet lead(l)}<span class="text-xs font-medium">{l.key}</span>{/snippet}
  {#snippet card(l)}
    <a href="/leads/{l.key}" class="line-clamp-2 font-medium outline-none after:absolute after:inset-0 after:rounded-lg focus-visible:after:inset-ring-3 focus-visible:after:inset-ring-ring/50">{l.title}</a>
    <div class="flex flex-wrap gap-1"><Badge variant="outline" class="tabular-nums">{money.format(l.amount)}</Badge></div>
  {/snippet}
</Board>

<!-- In Board.svelte: a column, its head, and a card. The slot of a card that the pointer lifted takes bg-muted. -->
<section data-slot="board-column" aria-labelledby="{id}-head" class="flex h-full w-72 shrink-0 flex-col rounded-lg border border-border bg-muted/50">
  <div data-slot="board-column-head" class="flex h-8 shrink-0 items-center gap-2 pr-1 pl-3 text-sm print:mb-3 print:break-after-avoid"><!-- The icon, the name, the count, Fold and the actions. --></div>
  <div class="relative flex min-h-0 flex-1 flex-col overflow-y-auto px-2 pt-1 pb-2">
    <ol role="list" aria-labelledby="{id}-head" class="flex flex-col gap-2">
      <li data-slot="board-item" data-lifted={lifted?.by} class="relative shrink-0 rounded-lg data-[lifted=pointer]:bg-muted">
        <!-- The line before a card. After the last card it takes -bottom-1 translate-y-1/2. -->
        <span data-slot="board-drop-line" aria-hidden="true" class="pointer-events-none absolute inset-x-0 -top-1 z-20 h-0.5 -translate-y-1/2 rounded-full bg-foreground"></span>
        <div data-slot="board-card" data-lifted={up || undefined} class="relative flex flex-col gap-1.5 rounded-lg bg-card p-2 text-sm text-card-foreground ring-1 ring-foreground/10 transition-[background-color,box-shadow] duration-fast ease-stack data-lifted:z-50 data-lifted:bg-popover data-lifted:text-popover-foreground data-lifted:shadow-md print:break-inside-avoid">
          <div class="flex min-h-6 items-center gap-1">
            <Button data-slot="board-card-handle" variant="ghost" size="icon-xs" class="relative z-10 cursor-grab touch-none text-muted-foreground hover:text-foreground in-data-lifted:cursor-grabbing" aria-label="Move {name}" aria-describedby={hint}><GripVerticalIcon class="size-4" /></Button>
            <!-- The identifier, then More with Move up, Move down and Move to. -->
          </div>
          <div class="flex min-w-0 flex-col gap-1.5 pr-1 pl-7"><!-- The body of the product. --></div>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ol>
  </div>
</section>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the board shows one column at a time, at the full width. A switch above it holds one item for each column, a folded column too, with the icon, the name and the count. The switch is a single `toggle-group` in a `scroll-area` that scrolls sideways, and each item is 44 px high (`toggle-group-item-phone`, measured at 390 px). The board opens on the first column that holds a card. A card has no handle on a phone, because a finger cannot drag a card to a column that it does not see. More holds Move up, Move down and Move to. It is 44 by 44 px (`dropdown-menu-trigger-phone`), and its items are 44 px high (measured). A card that Move to takes to another column leaves the screen, and the column stays on the screen, also when the move empties it. The focus then moves to More of the card that takes its place, or to the item of the column in the switch when the column is empty (measured with each card of a column of three). A card is 332 px wide at 390 px in the showcase (measured).

**Print.** In print a board takes its phone layout, because `ScreenQuery` answers as at 658 px (see `### A screen page in print`). The phone layout shows one column at a time, and paper has no switch. So the switch does not print, and every column prints in turn down the page, in the order of the board. Each column prints its head with the name and the count, and then every card that it holds. A folded column prints its head only, as its rail shows it on a wide screen. To print its cards, the person unfolds the column first. A phone has no Fold, so a person who needs those cards prints from a wide screen. Show all does not print, so a cut column prints its first cards. A line under them gives how many print, such as "First 10 of 62 cards". With only the active column, the example board printed 5 of the 28 cards that its wide layout shows. With every column, it printed all 28 on 6 pages of A4, from 1920 and 390 px (measured). In print the board and each column are blocks (`print:block`). A flex column keeps its height when a card moves to the next page. So the next column printed over that card: on the example board, 82 pairs of words overlapped (measured). The head takes `print:break-after-avoid`, so that it prints on the page of its first card. The top padding of the list lets a page end inside the list, under the padding. `break-after` on the head does not control that place. With the padding, a head stood alone at the foot of a page (measured). So in print the list takes `print:pt-0`, and the head takes the space as `print:mb-3`. In the seven states of the example board, from 1920 and 390 px, no card crossed a page end. No head stood apart from its first card (measured).

### day-log

**Purpose.** A log by day lists the entries of a log in groups by the day of their time. Examples are the events of a project, the runs of a job and the hours that a person booked. An entry is an event at one time, or a stretch of time with a start and an end or with a duration. It is a log in the sense of "Formats" in `architecture/web-app.md`, so each entry with a start shows its exact time. The activity of a record is an `item` of size `xs`, and a conversation is a `message-scroller`, so neither is a log by day. A log that the person compares by its columns, such as an audit trail with many fields, is a `table`. The log by day is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/DayLog`, and gives the entries, the time zone, the locale, now, the time of an entry and the snippet of its content. STACK builds the part from `tooltip`.

**Anatomy.** The `day-log` slot is a column on `background` in `body` type. Its first line, the caption (`day-log-caption`), names the time zone of the log once, such as "Times in Europe/Belgrade", in `small` and `muted-foreground`. The days follow, the newest day first, and in each day the newest entry first. The product passes the entries in one list (`entries`). The log groups them itself by the date part of their start, in the time zone that the caption names, so that a head and its entries always agree. The head of a day (`day-log-head`) is the group header of `### Lists`: 32 px high on `muted`, with a `border` edge below, sticky at the top of its scroll box. It holds the name of the day at 600, then the date (`day-log-head-date`) and the count in `muted-foreground`, with a middle dot between them. On the right it holds the total of the day (`day-log-head-total`) in `small` and `muted-foreground`. The name is Today or Yesterday in the words of the locale, from `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` with `numeric: 'auto'`, and the weekday for an older day. Each entry (`day-log-entry`) is a row with a `border-faint` divider below and 6 px of padding above and below. From the left, it holds a gutter of 4 px for the bar of a span, then the time (`day-log-time`) in `muted-foreground` with tabular figures. The content of the product follows, and the duration (`day-log-duration`) in `muted-foreground` comes last, on the right. The content is an icon at 14 px in a 20 px box and a sentence, as an activity line of `item`. The time column is 64 px wide (`w-16`) for a single time, and 128 px (`w-32`) when an entry of the log has an end.

**Variants.** Exact time: the head of a day holds the date part of the exact time, `YYYY-MM-DD`, and each entry holds the time part, `HH:mm:ss`, as "Formats" in `architecture/web-app.md` says. The `datetime` attribute of each time holds the whole instant. Start and end: an entry with an end shows both times with an en dash, such as "09:00:00–10:20:30", and its duration from `Intl.DurationFormat` in the style `short`, such as "1 hr, 20 mins". An entry that still runs ends in "now", and its duration adds "so far". An end on a later day shows its date part too, on a second line. An entry with a duration alone, such as time that a person booked to a day, keeps the time column empty. It has no start, so it keeps its place in the list of the product: it joins the day of the entry before it and shows right after it. At the head of the list it shows right before the entry after it. Totals: the total of a day is the sum of the durations of its entries, such as "4 hrs, 20 mins", and a day with no duration shows no total. Span: a stretch that holds over several entries, such as a mode or a maintenance window, draws a bar. The bar is 4 px wide in `input` (`day-log-span`), in the gutter beside each entry of the span. It has round ends at the newest and the oldest entry. The label of the span stands above the newest entry in `small` at 500 and `muted-foreground`. An entry that starts or ends a span, such as a change of mode, keeps the columns of the other entries. It may hold a second line in `muted-foreground` that says what it changed, and it takes no box and no ground of its own. Empty day: a day with no entry, such as a day that a filter emptied, keeps its head when the product names it in `days`. It shows one line in `small` and `faint-foreground`, as an empty group of a list does.

**Sizes.** An entry of one line is 33 px high: a line of 20 px, 6 px of padding above and below, and the 1 px divider (measured). The head is 32 px high. The time `HH:mm:ss` is 57.56 px wide at the `body` step with tabular figures, so it fits in the 64 px column. A start and an end fit in the 128 px column (measured at 1280 px). The bar of a span is 4 px wide (`w-1`).

**States.** Hover: an entry whose content is a link that covers the entry takes `muted` at 50% (`has-[a:hover]:bg-muted/50`, measured). The focus of that link draws the 3 px `ring/50` halo inside the edge. The tooltip of a time shows the full date and time of the locale with `dateStyle: 'full'` and `timeStyle: 'full'`, such as "Wednesday, 18 November 2026 at 10:30:00 Central European Standard Time" (measured). The time is no tab stop, so its tooltip opens on hover only. The time stands over a link that covers the entry (`relative z-10`), so that its tooltip opens there too. The entry takes `isolate`, so that the sticky head of its day stays over the time (both measured). The head gives the date and the caption the time zone, so the tooltip is never the only copy. Loading: the log shows `skeleton` rows at the height of its entries, as `### Empty, loading and error` of `architecture/web-app.md` says.

**Tokens.** `day-log`, `day-log-caption`, `day-log-head`, `day-log-head-date`, `day-log-head-total`, `day-log-entry`, `day-log-time`, `day-log-duration`, `day-log-span`. The hover ground of an entry is muted at 50%, so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** Each day is a `<section>` that its head names, and its entries are an `<ol>` with the same name. The head takes `tabindex="-1"`, so that the product can move the focus to the head of a day that it adds, such as after Load older (measured in the example). A log that adds entries while the person reads it says each new entry in a polite live region, as "Names and announcements" in `architecture/web-app.md` says. The bar of a span is hidden from a screen reader, and the label of the span and the entries that start and end it give its words.

**Example.**

```svelte
<!-- entries: every entry in one list. days: the days that keep their head with no entry, such as a day that the filter emptied. -->
<DayLog {entries} {days} timeZone="Europe/Belgrade" locale="sr-Latn-RS" {now} time={(w) => w.time} count={(n) => `${n} ${n === 1 ? 'task' : 'tasks'}`}>
  {#snippet entry(w)}<span class="font-medium">{w.task}</span>{/snippet}
  {#snippet empty()}No time booked on this day.{/snippet}
</DayLog>

<!-- In DayLog.svelte: the head of a day and one entry with a start and an end. -->
<h3 tabindex="-1" data-slot="day-log-head" class="sticky top-0 z-10 flex h-8 items-center gap-2 border-b border-border bg-muted pr-4 pl-3 outline-none focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 focus-visible:ring-inset">
  <span class="font-semibold">Today</span>
  <span class="text-muted-foreground"><time datetime="2026-11-18" data-slot="day-log-head-date" class="tabular-nums">2026-11-18</time> · 3 tasks</span>
  <span data-slot="day-log-head-total" class="ml-auto text-xs text-muted-foreground tabular-nums">4 hrs, 20 mins</span>
</h3>
<li data-slot="day-log-entry" class="relative isolate flex gap-3 border-b border-border-faint pr-4 pl-1 has-[a:hover]:bg-muted/50">
  <span data-slot="day-log-span" aria-hidden="true" class="w-1 shrink-0"></span>
  <div class="flex min-w-0 flex-1 items-start gap-3 py-1.5">
    <span data-slot="day-log-time" class="relative z-10 w-32 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground tabular-nums"><time datetime="2026-11-18T08:00:00.000Z">09:00:00</time>–<time datetime="2026-11-18T09:20:30.000Z">10:20:30</time></span>
    <div class="min-w-0 flex-1"><span class="font-medium">Write the October report</span></div>
    <span data-slot="day-log-duration" class="shrink-0 text-muted-foreground tabular-nums">1 hr, 20 mins</span>
  </div>
</li>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the head keeps 32 px and drops the count, so that the name, the date and the total fit on one line (measured at 390 px). In a log with ends, each entry takes two lines: the times and the duration, then the content at the full width, 55 px high in all (measured). A log of single times keeps one line. Its time column is 68 px wide (`max-md:w-17`), because the time is 66.42 px wide at the 15 px `body` step (measured). An entry whose content is a link is a target, so its link covers the whole entry.

### badge

**Purpose.** A badge is a short label beside an item: a status, a count, an identifier that links to an item, or a mark such as Proposal. A badge has no action of its own, except as a link or as the trigger of a menu. A card or a row that blocks work shows a badge with an icon and a word, such as Blocked, and never a colored edge (see Do's and Don'ts).

**Anatomy.** One `badge` slot, a `<span>`, or an `<a>` with `href`, with the text in `small` at 500 on one line. An icon of 12 px goes before or after the text, with `data-icon="inline-start"` or `"inline-end"`, 4 px from it. A badge clips its content and never wraps.

**Variants.** `default` is a `primary` fill, `secondary` a `secondary` fill, `outline` a `border` edge with `foreground` text, `ghost` has no ground, `link` is `primary` text, and `destructive` is `destructive` text on its 10% tint (20% in dark). The status badge is an `outline` badge with `rounded-full`, the status role of the product at 10% for the fill and at 35% for the edge, the icon in that role and the word in `foreground` (measured with a feedback role in place of a product role). A product checks the icon on the 10% fill at 3:1 and the word on it at 4.5:1 with the command `stack-contrast` (see `## Colors`). The status badge of the state of a job, a device, a connection or a check takes `success`, `warning` or `destructive` in place of the product role (see `## Colors`). The count badge adds `min-w-5 justify-center px-1 tabular-nums`, the link chip is an `outline` badge with `href` and the icon of its kind that opens its item in the pane, and the proposal badge adds `border-input`. A label badge is an `outline` badge with the dot of its label before the name: a 6 px `<span>` with `rounded-full`, its `tag-<color>` role as the fill and `data-icon="inline-start"`, so that it takes the padding and the gap of an icon, 6 px from the edge and 4 px from the name (measured). A filter chip is an `outline` badge with the field in `muted-foreground`, an operator word and the value, and a ghost `button` of size `icon-xs` beside it removes the filter.

**Sizes.** A badge is 20 px high (`spacing.badge`, `h-5`) with 8 px of padding at the sides, and 6 px on the side of an icon. Its radius is 8 px, and the status badge is round. A count badge of one digit is 20 by 20 px (measured).

**States.** Invalid (`[aria-invalid="true"]`) draws a `destructive` edge. A badge that is a link takes `primary/80` on hover in `default`, `secondary/80` in `secondary`, the `muted` ground in `outline` and `ghost`, and the underline in `link`, and focus-visible draws the `ring` edge and the 3 px halo. A status badge that opens a menu takes the open state of its trigger (`[data-state="open"]`) and shows a chevron.

**Tokens.** `badge`, `badge-secondary`, `badge-outline`, `badge-invalid`. The destructive variant is a tint and the status badge takes a role of the product, so neither has a key, and a product checks the pairs of its own status roles.

**Accessibility.** A screen reader reads the word of a badge, so the word, not the color, carries the status, and an icon in it is decorative. The dot of a label badge is decorative too (`aria-hidden="true"`), and the name carries the label. A count badge with no word around it needs one in its name, such as `aria-label="3 unread"`. A count badge inside a control, such as a tab or a menu button, takes `aria-hidden="true"`, and the control says the count in its name (see `### unread-mark`). A status badge that opens a menu names the status and the action, such as "Status Paid, change".

**Example.**

```svelte
<Badge variant="outline" class="rounded-full border-product-paid/35 bg-product-paid/10">
  <CircleCheckIcon data-icon="inline-start" class="text-product-paid" />Paid
</Badge>
<Badge variant="secondary" class="min-w-5 justify-center px-1 tabular-nums" aria-label="3 unread">3</Badge>
<Badge variant="outline" href="/invoices/INV-0042"><FileTextIcon data-icon="inline-start" />INV-0042</Badge>
<Badge variant="outline" class="border-input">Proposal</Badge>
<Badge variant="outline"><span data-icon="inline-start" class="size-1.5 rounded-full bg-tag-blue" aria-hidden="true"></span>Billing</Badge>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a badge stays 20 px high, and its text takes the 13 px `small` step (measured). A badge that is a link is a target, so give it `class="max-md:min-h-11"`: it is then 44 px high below 768 px and 20 px from 768 px (measured). A status badge that opens a menu is the `child` of the trigger, so it takes the slot of the trigger, such as `dropdown-menu-trigger`, and the phone key of that slot.

### unread-mark

**Purpose.** An unread mark shows that the person has not read an item yet, such as a case, a notice or a message. It ends line 1 of a row in a list with a read state (see "Unread mark" in `### Lists`). It also ends line 1 of an unread `mail-message`. On an icon, it shows that the page or the pages behind the icon hold an unread item. "Read state" in `### Detail pane` says when an item counts as read, and the mark of its row goes at that moment. A message of a mail thread keeps its mark while its item shows (see `### mail-message`). A count of unread items is a count, so it takes a count badge or a `sidebar-menu-badge`, and not this part. The unread mark is a part of STACK and not of the registry, so a release ships no folder for it in `preview/src/lib/components/ui/`. A product imports it as `@stack-kit/stack/parts/UnreadMark`, and `@stack-kit/stack/parts/SplitRow` draws it with its `unread` prop.

**Anatomy.** One `unread-mark` slot: a `<span>` of 8 by 8 px (`size-2`) with the full radius, in `foreground`. It draws the dot as an edge of 4 px on each side and no ground (`border-4 border-foreground`), because print drops every ground. A dot drawn as a ground printed nothing, and the edge printed at gray 10 (measured). A screen reader does not read the dot (`aria-hidden="true"`), so the name of the control that opens the item holds the word (see Accessibility). An unread row also sets its title at 500, so the state shows as a shape and as a weight.

**Variants.** In a line: the mark stands at the end of line 1, 8 px after the time (`gap-2`), in the middle of the line. A list with a read state keeps the place of the mark in every row. A read row holds an empty `<span>` of the same size (`size-2 shrink-0`). On an icon: the mark stands over the top right corner of the icon, 2 px past its top edge and its right edge (`absolute -top-0.5 -right-0.5`). A ring of 2 px in the ground of the button parts it from the icon (`ring-2`). The ring is `ring-background` in the tab bar of band 1. In the sidebar it is `ring-sidebar`, and `ring-sidebar-accent` under the pointer and on the current page. A mark on an icon serves where a count cannot show. The rail of the sidebar hides every badge, and the More tab of band 1 stands for several pages.

**Sizes.** The mark is 8 by 8 px in every band. In a line of 20 px it stands 6 px from the top and from the bottom. It ends at the right padding of the row, 16 px from its edge (measured). On an icon of 16 or 20 px, it covers 6 by 6 px of the corner (measured). Its ring adds 2 px around it.

**States.** Unread: the mark shows. Read: the mark of a row goes at once, with no motion, when the item counts as read, and Mark as unread gives it back. The mark of a message in a mail thread goes when another item takes the column (see `### mail-message`). The mark keeps `foreground` on every ground of a row: `background` at rest, `muted/50` under the pointer, `muted` for the current row, and `selected`. A message in the item column of band 2 sits on `popover`. In dark mode `foreground` is light, so the mark is a light dot on the dark ground. In print the page takes light mode (see `### A screen page in print`), so the mark prints dark on the paper (measured from 1440 and 390 px).

**Tokens.** `unread-mark`. The contrast check reads the foreground role at 3:1 on each ground of the mark (see Colors). The ring takes the ground of its button, so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** The dot is hidden, so the control that opens the item says the word. In a row, the button of line 1 starts with `<span class="sr-only">Unread,</span>`, and Chromium names it "Unread, Ana Turner assigned CASE-0142 to you" (measured). A hidden word at the end of the name took a space before its comma in Chromium, so the word comes first (measured). When the item counts as read, the word goes with the mark. A count says its word in the name of its control, through `aria-label`, such as "Notices, 4 unread". The badge of the count then takes `aria-hidden="true"`. A control with a mark on its icon adds the word to its name in the same way, such as "More, unread" (measured). The tooltip of a menu button in the rail repeats the count, such as "Notices, 4 unread".

**Example.**

```svelte
<!-- A row in a list with a read state. SplitRow draws the place of the mark, the mark, the weight and the word. -->
<SplitRow id="row-{n.key}" unread={n.unread} current={n.key === shown} onopen={() => open(n.key)}>
  {#snippet line1()}<span class="font-medium">{n.actor}</span>{' '}{n.text}{/snippet}
  {#snippet end1()}<time datetime={n.at.toISOString()}>{age(n.at)}</time>{/snippet}
  {#snippet line2()}{n.kind} · {n.record}{/snippet}
</SplitRow>

<!-- In SplitRow.svelte: line 1 of an unread row. A read row holds <span class="size-2 shrink-0" aria-hidden="true"></span> in the place of the mark. -->
<button type="button" id="row-N-0412" class="min-w-0 flex-1 cursor-pointer truncate text-left font-medium outline-none after:absolute after:inset-0">
  <span class="sr-only">Unread,</span><span class="font-medium">Ana Turner</span> assigned CASE-0142 to you
</button>
<span class="shrink-0 text-xs text-muted-foreground tabular-nums"><time datetime="2026-11-18T12:52:00.000Z">13:52</time></span>
<span data-slot="unread-mark" aria-hidden="true" class="size-2 shrink-0 rounded-full border-4 border-foreground"></span>

<!-- The More tab of band 1, while a page of its sheet holds an unread item. -->
<button type="button" aria-label="More, unread">
  <span class="relative flex">
    <EllipsisIcon class="size-5" />
    <span data-slot="unread-mark" aria-hidden="true" class="absolute -top-0.5 -right-0.5 size-2 rounded-full border-4 border-foreground ring-2 ring-background"></span>
  </span>
  <span class="truncate">More</span>
</button>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the mark keeps 8 px at the end of line 1 of the phone row (measured at 390 px). It is no target. The tab of a page with unread items shows their count on its icon. The count is a count badge of `badge` (`secondary`, `min-w-5 justify-center px-1 tabular-nums`), 20 by 20 px for one digit. It stands 12 px from the left edge of the 20 px icon and 6 px above it (`absolute -top-1.5 left-3`, measured). The More tab shows the mark on its icon while a page of its sheet holds an unread item. One count of several pages has no meaning. The sheet shows the count beside each page, as the sidebar does.

### avatar

**Purpose.** An avatar shows a person or an agent as a picture, or as initials when there is no picture, such as the author of a message or the person who owns an item. The avatar is round, as a shape that is round by nature (see `## Shapes`). A group of avatars shows the people of an item, with a count for the rest.

**Anatomy.** `Avatar.Root` (`avatar`) holds `Avatar.Image` (`avatar-image`) and `Avatar.Fallback` (`avatar-fallback`), which shows the initials in `muted-foreground` on `muted` until the image loads, or when it fails. A 1 px ring in `border` lies over the edge, so that a light picture keeps its shape on a light ground. `Avatar.Badge` (`avatar-badge`) is a dot in `primary` at the lower right, and `Avatar.Group` (`avatar-group`) overlaps its avatars by 8 px, each with a 2 px ring of `background`, and ends with `Avatar.GroupCount` (`avatar-group-count`).

**Variants.** `size` is `sm`, `default` or `lg`, and an avatar of an agent and an avatar of a person look the same. The 20 px size `xs` is no value of the prop: the Example gives `sm` the classes of that size. The badge can hold an icon of 8 px, such as a check. A product that shows a presence state with the badge also says the state in words.

**Sizes.** The avatar is 24 px in `sm`, 32 px in `default` and 40 px in `lg` (`spacing.control-xs`, `spacing.control-md`), with the full radius. The initials are `body`, and `small` in `sm`. The badge is 8, 10 and 12 px across in the three sizes (measured). The `xs` size is 20 px (`spacing.chip`), the line height of `body`, for an avatar in a line of `body` text, such as an activity line or the head of a comment. It holds one initial in `meta`, its badge is 6 px across, and a group of `xs` avatars overlaps by 6 px, with a 20 px count (measured). Its badge hides an icon, as the badge of `sm` does. Each class of `xs` names the variant of the class of the component that it replaces, so that `cn` drops the 24 px value. A plain `size-5` on an `sm` avatar loses to `data-[size=sm]:size-6`, and the avatar stays 24 px (measured).

**States.** Bits UI writes `data-status` on the root, the image and the fallback: `loading` until the image loads, and while there is no image, `loaded` when it shows, and `error` when it fails. The fallback shows until the status is `loaded` (measured: no fallback beside a loaded image).

**Tokens.** `avatar`, `avatar-sm`, `avatar-xs`, `avatar-fallback`, `avatar-badge`, `avatar-group-count`. The 20 px avatar renders as a small avatar, so no selector matches `avatar-xs`, and the key records the size only.

**Accessibility.** An avatar next to the written name takes `alt=""`, so that a screen reader does not read the name twice. An avatar with no name beside it gives the full name in `alt`, and in an `aria-label` on the root for the case of the initials. The count of a group says what it counts, such as `aria-label="4 more people"`.

**Example.**

```svelte
<div class="flex items-center gap-2">
  <Avatar.Root size="sm"><Avatar.Image src={person.photo} alt="" /><Avatar.Fallback>{person.initials}</Avatar.Fallback></Avatar.Root>
  <span class="font-medium">{person.name}</span>
</div>

<!-- The xs size, in a line of body text. Its badge: class="group-data-[size=sm]/avatar:size-1.5".
     Its group: class="-space-x-1.5", and on the count class="group-has-data-[size=sm]/avatar-group:size-5 text-2xs font-medium". -->
<p class="flex items-center gap-2 text-sm">
  <Avatar.Root size="sm" class="data-[size=sm]:size-5"><Avatar.Image src={person.photo} alt="" /><Avatar.Fallback class="group-data-[size=sm]/avatar:text-2xs font-medium">{person.initials.slice(0, 1)}</Avatar.Fallback></Avatar.Root>
  <span><span class="font-medium">{person.name}</span> <span class="text-muted-foreground">changed the due date</span></span>
</p>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the avatar keeps its size, and the initials take the 15 px `body` step, or 13 px in `sm` (measured). In `xs` the initial takes the 12 px `meta` step, and a line of `body` text stays 20 px high around the avatar (measured at 390 px). An avatar is no target: as the trigger of the user menu it sits in a `button` or a menu trigger, which has its own phone key.

### item

**Purpose.** An item is one row of content with media, a title, a description and actions, such as a setting with its switch, a notification channel with its state, a file, or a line in the activity of a record. A group of items is a short list inside a pane, a card or a settings page. The rows of the main list of the app shell follow `## Layout`, not this component.

**Anatomy.** `Item.Root` (`item`) is a row that wraps, with the 8 px radius and `body` text. It holds `Item.Media` (`item-media`), `Item.Content` (`item-content`) with `Item.Title` (`item-title`, `body` at 500) and `Item.Description` (`item-description`, `body` in `muted-foreground`, at most two lines), and `Item.Actions` (`item-actions`). `Item.Header` and `Item.Footer` take a full line above and below, `Item.Group` (`item-group`) stacks items 16 px apart, and `Item.Separator` (`item-separator`) divides them.

**Variants.** `default` has no ground and no edge, `outline` has a `border` edge, and `muted` has the `muted` ground at 50%. The media is `default`, `icon` (16 px) or `image` (a 40 px square with the 4 px radius), and with `child` the item renders a link or a button. A setting is an item with an icon, a title, one line of description and its control in `Item.Actions`, such as a `switch`, and its state also shows as a word, such as On. An activity line is an item of size `xs` with the icon of its event at 14 px, the actor and the objects at 500, the verb and the time in `muted-foreground`. A change of status takes the icon of the new status and every other line a neutral icon, a product joins the icons with a 1 px line in `border`, and a stretch of more than 8 lines shows its last 3 and a line that opens the rest.

**Sizes.** `default` and `sm` have 10 px by 12 px of padding and a 10 px gap, and `xs` has 8 px by 10 px, an 8 px gap and the description in `small`. An item with a title and two lines of description is 83 px high, and an `xs` item of one line is 36 px high (measured). The image media is 40 px, 32 px in `sm` and 24 px in `xs`.

**States.** An item that is a link takes the `muted` ground on hover (`[a]:hover:bg-muted`), and focus-visible draws the `ring` edge and the 3 px halo. The item has no selected or disabled state of its own, and its controls keep their own states.

**Tokens.** `item`, `item-outline`, `item-title`, `item-description`, `item-media-image`. The muted variant is muted at 50%, so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** `Item.Group` has the `list` role, and an item sets no role, so give each item in a group `role="listitem"`. A control in `Item.Actions` names its item, such as "Email notifications", because a screen reader can reach it apart from the title. An item that is a link takes its title as its name.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Item.Group>
  <Item.Root variant="outline" role="listitem">
    <Item.Media variant="icon"><MailIcon /></Item.Media>
    <Item.Content>
      <Item.Title>Email</Item.Title>
      <Item.Description>A summary each morning. On.</Item.Description>
    </Item.Content>
    <Item.Actions><Switch bind:checked={email} aria-label="Email notifications" /></Item.Actions>
  </Item.Root>
</Item.Group>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a button in `Item.Actions` is a `button` slot and 44 px high (`button-phone`, measured), so the item grows with it, and the text takes the 15 px `body` step. An item that is a link with one line and no media is 42.6 px high there, so give it `class="max-md:min-h-11"`, which makes it 44 px (measured). An activity line of size `xs` grows from 36 px to 38.6 px with the phone type step (measured), and it is no target.

### empty

**Purpose.** An empty state takes the place of the content of a list, a pane or a page that has nothing to show yet, such as an inbox with no message. One sentence says why it is empty and what to do, and one button runs that action. An empty group inside a list is one line in `small` and `faint-foreground` with no button, and not this component.

**Anatomy.** `Empty.Root` (`empty`) holds `Empty.Header` (`empty-header`) with an optional `Empty.Media` (`empty-icon`), `Empty.Title` (`empty-title`, `body` at 500) and `Empty.Description` (`empty-description`, `body` in `muted-foreground`), then `Empty.Content` (`empty-content`) with the action. Nova centers all of it in a box with 24 px of padding, and `stack.css` moves the content to the top left (see Variants). For a dashed edge with the 8 px radius, the root takes `border p-6!`. The rule of `stack.css` comes after the utilities of Tailwind and sets the side and bottom padding to 0, so a plain `p-6` does not keep the text off the edge.

**Variants.** The media is `default`, an icon as it comes, or `icon`, a 32 px box on `muted` with the 8 px radius and a 16 px icon. An empty state is never centered (see Do's and Don'ts), so `stack.css` starts the content at the left edge, 32 px under the top of the region (`space-8`), and the page gives the parts no class for this. An empty state has no media and no illustration. The action is one `outline` button with its Kbd. An empty full page has 48 px of padding: its root takes `p-12!`, because the rule of `stack.css` comes after the utilities of Tailwind.

**Sizes.** The title and the description are `body`, and the description has a line height of 1.625 (`text-sm/relaxed`), which is 21.1 px. The header and the content are at most 384 px wide (`max-w-sm`), and they are 16 px apart.

**States.** None of its own. A list shows its empty state only when it has loaded and holds no item. While it loads, it shows `skeleton` rows.

**Tokens.** `empty`, `empty-icon-icon`, `empty-title`, `empty-description`.

**Accessibility.** The sentence is plain text, so put it in a heading of the right level when the region has no other heading. When a filter empties a list, a `role="status"` region announces the sentence. The button names its action with a verb and the record, such as "Create invoice", as `### button` writes a label. When the list has a create button, the empty state repeats its label (see "Create button" in `### Lists`).

**Example.**

```svelte
<Empty.Root>
  <Empty.Header>
    <Empty.Description>No invoices in October. Create one from a client.</Empty.Description>
  </Empty.Header>
  <Empty.Content>
    <Button variant="outline" size="sm">Create invoice <Kbd.Root>C</Kbd.Root></Button>
  </Empty.Content>
</Empty.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the button is 44 px high (`button-phone`, measured), and the text takes the 15 px `body` step. The rule of `stack.css` keeps the content at the left edge and 32 px under the top at 390 px, as from 768 px (measured).

### kbd

**Purpose.** A Kbd shows a key or a combination of keys, such as the key of an action in its tooltip, its menu item or its button. It is for display only: it takes no pointer and no focus. The key of an action shows wherever the action shows, so that a person learns it.

**Anatomy.** `Kbd.Root` (`kbd`) is a `<kbd>` with the key in `small` at 500 in `muted-foreground`, on `muted`, with the 4 px radius and no edge. `Kbd.Group` (`kbd-group`) holds the keys of a combination 4 px apart. An icon in a Kbd is 12 px.

**Variants.** Nova has none, and a Kbd changes with its place. In a tooltip it takes `background` text on `background` at 20% (10% in dark) over the `foreground` ground of the tooltip (measured). In a `default` button it takes no fill, a 1 px edge of `primary-foreground` at 35% and `primary-foreground` text, through `class="border border-primary-foreground/35 bg-transparent text-primary-foreground"` (measured).

**Sizes.** A Kbd is 20 px high (`spacing.kbd`, `h-5`) and at least 20 px wide, with 4 px of padding at the sides. The type is `small`, 12 px on a 16 px line, in Inter and never monospace.

**States.** None. A sequence shows each key in its own Kbd with a word from a message key between them, such as G then I. A combination holds its keys in one `Kbd.Group`.

**Tokens.** `kbd`. Its ground in a tooltip and its edge in a primary button carry an opacity, so they have no key.

**Accessibility.** A screen reader reads the text of the `<kbd>`, and a glyph such as ⌘ has no clear name, so give the control its key in `aria-keyshortcuts`, such as `aria-keyshortcuts="Meta+K"`. A Kbd shows glyphs on macOS and words on other systems, such as Ctrl and Shift.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Tooltip.Content>Search <Kbd.Group><Kbd.Root>{mac ? '⌘' : 'Ctrl'}</Kbd.Root><Kbd.Root>K</Kbd.Root></Kbd.Group></Tooltip.Content>
<Button aria-keyshortcuts={mac ? 'Meta+S' : 'Control+S'}>Save <Kbd.Root class="border border-primary-foreground/35 bg-transparent text-primary-foreground">{mac ? '⌘S' : 'Ctrl+S'}</Kbd.Root></Button>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a Kbd stays 20 px high, and its text takes the 13 px `small` step (measured). A phone has no keyboard as a rule, so hide the Kbd of a button there with `max-md:hidden`, and keep the key in `aria-keyshortcuts`.

### alert

**Purpose.** An alert shows one message about the current screen or form, such as a sync that stopped, a limit that comes close, or the result of a check. As a banner it spans the width under the top bar, across the list and the pane, one at a time and the strongest first (see `## Layout`). The order of the connection banners and the kind of each one are in `architecture/web-app.md`. A message about one field is a `field-error` under that field, and a done action shows in the screen and needs no alert.

**Anatomy.** `Alert.Root` (`alert`) is a grid with an optional 16 px icon in the first column, `Alert.Title` (`alert-title`, `body` at 500) and `Alert.Description` (`alert-description`, `body` in `muted-foreground`, with its links underlined). `Alert.Action` (`alert-action`) sits at the top right, 8 px from the edges, and the root keeps 72 px free at its right for it. The root has 8 px by 10 px of padding, the `card` ground, a `border` edge and the 8 px radius.

**Variants.** `default` draws `card-foreground` on `card`, and `destructive` draws the title and the icon in `destructive` and the description in `destructive` at 90%. A feedback banner takes the tint of its role on the `default` variant: `rounded-none border-0 border-b border-warning/35 bg-warning/10 text-warning *:data-[slot=alert-description]:text-warning` (measured), and the same classes with `success`, `info` or `destructive`. A neutral banner keeps the colors of the `default` variant, the key words of a banner are 600 (`font-semibold`), and its action is an `outline` button of size `xs`. The part `Banner` (`@stack-kit/stack/parts/Banner`) holds these classes: the page gives it `tint`, one of `success`, `warning`, `info` and `destructive`, or none for a neutral banner, then `title`, the description as its children, and `icon` and `action` snippets, and no class. A `strong` element in the description marks the key words. The part sets the ARIA role from the tint: a `destructive` banner has the `alert` role, and every other banner has the `status` role, as the Accessibility part asks. A page gives `role` only for a message that must speak at once, such as `role="alert"` for a warning, and that role wins over the role from the tint.

**Sizes.** The icon is 16 px, and the title and the description are lines of 20 px, 2 px apart. A banner of one line is at least 36 px high (`min-h-9`).

**States.** None of its own. An alert shows while its cause lasts, the product removes it, and its action keeps the states of a button.

**Tokens.** `alert`, `alert-destructive`, `alert-title`, `alert-description`. The tint of a banner and the description of the destructive variant carry an opacity, so they have no key.

**Accessibility.** Nova gives every alert the `alert` role, which a screen reader announces at once. Keep that role for a failure, and pass `role="status"` for any other message, so that it waits for the current speech. The title says the kind of message in words, because the icon and the color carry no meaning alone.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Banner tint="warning" title="The bank sync stopped 2 hours ago.">
  {#snippet icon()}<TriangleAlertIcon />{/snippet}
  <strong>New payments</strong> do not show until it runs again.
  {#snippet action()}<Button variant="outline" size="xs">Sync now</Button>{/snippet}
</Banner>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the action button is 44 px high (`button-phone`, measured), and the alert grows to hold it. The text takes the 15 px `body` step, and a banner spans the width of the screen under the 52 px header of band 1.

### sonner

**Purpose.** A toast tells the person that an action ran, and it offers Undo. Show a toast only when Undo exists, and keep all other feedback in the screen where the change happens (see Do's and Don'ts). A failure that needs a decision is an `alert` or a `dialog`, not a toast.

**Anatomy.** Mount one `Toaster` in the layout, and call `toast()` from `svelte-sonner`. Each toast is a box of 356 px on `popover` with `popover-foreground` text, a `border` edge, the 8 px radius, 16 px of padding and the shadow of svelte-sonner, with an optional 16 px icon, the title at 500, the description and an action button. No part sets a `data-slot`, so the key of a toast takes the name of its part attribute, `[data-sonner-toast]`. The component takes its mode from mode-watcher, so the product tells mode-watcher its mode, as "The mode of mode-watcher" in `## Themes` says.

**Variants.** `toast.success`, `toast.info`, `toast.warning`, `toast.error` and `toast.loading` add their icon in the color of the text, because nova leaves `richColors` off, and the words of the toast say its kind. `action` adds a button, and `closeButton` on the `Toaster` adds a round close button at the top left corner. svelte-sonner sets a system font and a fixed 13 px. It gives the description a gray of its own, which follows `data-sonner-theme`, the mode of mode-watcher, and not the theme of the product. So `stack.css` gives the text of each toast the `body` step in `font-sans`, and its description `muted-foreground`, and the `Toaster` takes no class for this. The size and the color of the description have `!important`, because svelte-sonner writes them outside the layers of Tailwind. The selectors are in `:where()`, so a class with `!` that a product gives in `toastOptions.classes` still wins.

**Sizes.** A toast is 356 px wide, 24 px from the bottom and the right of the window, and at most 3 show at a time. With the rule of `stack.css` its text is the `body` step in Inter, and its description is `muted-foreground` (measured). The action button is 24 px high with the 4 px radius, and the close button is 20 px.

**States.** svelte-sonner writes `data-type` (`success`, `info`, `warning`, `error`, `loading` or `default`), `data-expanded`, `data-front` and `data-removed` on each toast. A toast closes after `duration`, which is 4000 ms by default, so give a toast with Undo 10000 ms. Hover pauses the timer and expands the stack, and keyboard focus alone does not: Alt+T moves the focus to the toasts and expands them, which pauses them, and Esc collapses them.

**Tokens.** `sonner-toast`.

**Accessibility.** The toasts sit in a `<section>` named "Notifications alt+T" in English, with `aria-live="polite"`, so a screen reader reads a toast after the current speech, and `important: true` makes it assertive. Pass `containerAriaLabel` and `closeButtonAriaLabel` in the language of the product. Undo also has a key in the product, so that a person can undo after the toast is gone.

**Example.**

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import { toast } from 'svelte-sonner';
  import { ScreenQuery } from '$lib/screen-query.svelte.js';
  function archive(invoice: Invoice) {
    move(invoice, 'archive');
    toast('The invoice moved to Archive', { action: { label: 'Undo', onClick: () => move(invoice, 'inbox') } });
  }
  // tabBar is true while the screen shows the 56 px tab bar of a phone.
  const phone = new ScreenQuery('max-width: 767px');
  const bar = $derived(phone.current && tabBar ? ' + var(--spacing) * 14' : '');
</script>

<Toaster
  duration={10000}
  containerAriaLabel="Notifications"
  offset={{ bottom: `calc(24px${bar} + env(safe-area-inset-bottom))` }}
  mobileOffset={{ bottom: `calc(16px${bar} + env(safe-area-inset-bottom))` }}
/>
```

**Phone.** Below 600 px svelte-sonner puts the toasts 16 px from the edges and makes each one as wide as the screen less 32 px, which is 358 px at 390 px (measured). svelte-sonner knows neither the 56 px tab bar of `## Layout` (`spacing.tab-bar-phone`) nor the bottom safe area, so with no offset a toast covers the tab bar (by 40 px at 390 px, measured). Give the `Toaster` the `offset` and the `mobileOffset` of the example. Each one adds `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` to the bottom gap of svelte-sonner, and 56 px while the screen shows the tab bar. svelte-sonner reads `mobileOffset` below 600 px and `offset` from 600 px, and the tab bar shows up to 767 px, so both carry it. svelte-sonner writes the offset into its own style, so the offset can hold `env()`, which no Tailwind class writes. A toast then sits 16 px above the tab bar below 600 px, 24 px above it from 600 to 767 px, and 16 or 24 px above the bottom inset on a screen with no tab bar (measured in Chromium at 390, 700 and 1280 px with emulated insets; not measured on a device). The action button and the close button set no `data-slot`, so no phone key reaches them, and `stack.css` gives each of them a minimum of 44 by 44 px (`spacing.touch`) below 768 px, and the page gives them no class. The action is then 46.8 by 44 px and the close button 44 by 44 px at 390 and 700 px, and they stay 24 px and 20 px high at 1280 px (measured). The text rule of `stack.css` reads the `body` step, so the text takes the 15 px `body-phone` step there (measured at 390 px).

### progress

**Purpose.** A progress bar shows how far a task or a quantity has come, such as the share of a limit that is used or the steps of an import that are done. It always has a label row with the figure as text, such as "3 of 5" or "72%", because the bar alone gives no exact value. A meter of a quota or of a usage window is the same bar with the outlined track of the variants. A meter can also show its thresholds, the value that it will reach and an amount that is not yet known. A progress bar shows one share. The parts of a whole in a row, such as the child tickets of a ticket by status, are words and no bar of segments (see `### Parts of a whole`).

**Anatomy.** `Progress` renders the root (`progress`), a track in `muted` with the full radius, and in it the `progress-indicator` in `primary`, which moves in from the left with `value` and `max`. Bits UI gives the root the `progressbar` role with its values, and a `role="meter"` prop does not change it (measured). The Meter part draws the label row above the bar and an optional line under it. A meter can take three more parts, which the Meter part draws too: a threshold tick (`progress-tick`), a forecast marker (`progress-forecast`) and a pending segment (`progress-pending`). `Progress` takes no children, so the parts sit next to the bar in a box with `relative`. The ticks and the marker sit in a box with `absolute inset-x-px -inset-y-0.75` over the bar, and the pending segment sits in a box with `absolute inset-px overflow-hidden rounded-full`. Both boxes start inside the 1 px edge. So a part at a percent of their width stands where the fill ends at that value (measured).

**Variants.** The nova bar is a track of 4 px in `muted`. A meter is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/Meter`, and gives the label, the value, the figure and the ticks. The product formats the figure and the value of each tick as text. The part draws the outlined track, `h-1.5 border border-input bg-background`, which is 6 px high with an `input` edge that passes 3:1 on the main ground, and `large` gives `h-2` in place of `h-1.5`, an 8 px meter for a large view (measured). A value past `max` fills the whole bar, and the figure keeps the real value. The safe fill is `primary` or `muted-foreground`, and `primary` stays valid for every meter. A product picks `muted-foreground` when its meters show the use of a limit, such as a quota. Then a meter shows a color only past a threshold, and a `primary` with a hue, such as the indigo of `graphite`, does not read as a status. `fill="neutral"` gives that fill, with the class `*:data-[slot=progress-indicator]:bg-muted-foreground`. A product picks one safe fill for all its meters. A product that sets limits gives each limit as a tick with a role. Past it, the part draws the fill in `warning` or `destructive`, with `*:data-[slot=progress-indicator]:bg-warning`, and adds an icon and the name of the tick to the label row. A threshold tick is a 1 px line in `foreground` across the track at a threshold, such as the warning level of a quota. A meter holds up to four ticks, each with a name. The fill may change its role at a tick, such as to `warning` at a warning tick. A forecast marker shows the value that the meter will reach. It is a dashed 1 px line in `foreground` across the track, under a triangle in `foreground` that points down at the value. A pending segment shows an amount that is not yet known, after the value, such as the usage that a provider has not reported yet. It follows the fill, with the hatch of `### Patterns` in `muted-foreground` on the track. Each part states its value as text, so no part carries information by color alone. The label row names the ticks, and the line under the bar states the forecast and the pending amount, such as "Forecast 9.1 GB by 30 October · 0.9 GB pending".

**Sizes.** The nova track is 4 px high (`spacing.space-1`, `h-1`) and as wide as its container. The meter is 6 px or 8 px high, with its 1 px edge inside that height, so the fill of the 6 px meter is 4 px high (measured). A tick is 1 px wide and stands 3 px above and 3 px under the track. So it is 12 px high on the 6 px meter and 14 px high on the 8 px meter (measured). Where the fill covers the middle of a tick, the two ends show the tick on the ground under the meter. Two ticks stand at least 4 px apart at the smallest width of the meter. The forecast marker is as high as a tick, with dashes of 2 px and gaps of 2 px. Its triangle is 9 px wide and 6 px high, and its tip touches the top end of the line. With a forecast marker the bar takes `mt-1.5`, so the label row stands 12 px above the track and 3 px above the triangle (measured). The pending segment is as high as the fill. Its hatch is pattern 1 with the marks of the color pattern style: lines at 45 degrees, 2 px wide, at a pitch of 4 px.

**States.** Bits UI writes `data-state="loading"` while the value is below `max`, `"loaded"` at `max`, and `"indeterminate"` when `value` is null, with `data-value` and `data-max`. Nova draws no look for a state, and a null value draws an empty track. So a task with no known end shows a word, such as Importing, and never a bar that moves. The parts of a meter have no state. When the value reaches a tick, the label row names that tick as the last tick passed. The fill takes the role of the last tick passed that has a role.

**Tokens.** `progress`, `progress-indicator`, `progress-indicator-neutral`, `progress-tick`, `progress-forecast`, `progress-pending`. The part draws the edge and the ground of the meter. `progress-indicator-neutral` holds the second safe fill. The root of the part sets the data-slot meter. The parts of a meter set no data-slot, so each key takes its name after progress, as the parts of a chart do. The key of the pending segment holds the color of the hatch marks, and the track shows between the marks.

**Accessibility.** Give the bar a name with `aria-label`, or with `aria-labelledby` on its label, such as "Storage". Set `aria-valuetext` when the figure has a unit, such as "7.2 of 10 GB", so that a screen reader reads the figure with its unit. A meter keeps the `progressbar` role, so its name and its value text carry the meaning. The parts are `aria-hidden`, and their text carries them. The label row names the ticks in the order of the track, each with its value, such as "Warning at 8 GB, limit at 9.5 GB". Its figure names the last tick that the value passed, such as "8.6 of 10 GB, past warning". `aria-valuetext` is that figure: the value with its unit, and the last tick passed. It never holds the forecast or the pending amount, because neither is the value. `aria-describedby` points to the names of the ticks and to the line under the bar, so that a screen reader reads them after the value.

**Example.**

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  const uid = $props.id();
</script>

<div class="flex flex-col gap-1.5">
  <div class="flex justify-between text-xs"><span id="{uid}-label">Storage</span><span class="text-muted-foreground tabular-nums"><span class="text-foreground">5.8 of 10 GB</span> · <span id="{uid}-ticks">Warning at 8 GB</span></span></div>
  <div class="relative mt-1.5">
    <Progress value={58} aria-labelledby="{uid}-label" aria-valuetext="5.8 of 10 GB" aria-describedby="{uid}-ticks {uid}-note" class="h-1.5 border border-input bg-background *:data-[slot=progress-indicator]:bg-muted-foreground" />
    <div class="pointer-events-none absolute inset-px overflow-hidden rounded-full" aria-hidden="true">
      <svg class="block size-full">
        <defs>
          <pattern id="{uid}-hatch" data-pattern="1" data-pattern-style="color-pattern" data-pattern-ink="var(--muted-foreground)" width="4" height="4" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" patternTransform="rotate(45)">
            <line x1="2" y1="0" x2="2" y2="4" class="stroke-muted-foreground" stroke-width="2" shape-rendering="crispEdges" />
          </pattern>
        </defs>
        <rect x="58%" width="9%" height="100%" fill="url(#{uid}-hatch)" />
      </svg>
    </div>
    <div class="pointer-events-none absolute inset-x-px -inset-y-0.75" aria-hidden="true">
      <svg class="absolute top-0 block h-full w-px overflow-visible" style="left: 80%">
        <line x1="0.5" x2="0.5" y1="0" y2="100%" class="stroke-foreground" stroke-width="1" shape-rendering="crispEdges" />
      </svg>
      <span class="absolute -top-1.5 bottom-0 w-2.25 -translate-x-1" style="left: 91%">
        <svg class="block size-full overflow-visible">
          <path d="M0 0H9L4.5 6Z" class="fill-foreground" />
          <line x1="4.5" y1="6" x2="4.5" y2="100%" class="stroke-foreground" stroke-dasharray="2 2" shape-rendering="crispEdges" />
        </svg>
      </span>
    </div>
  </div>
  <p id="{uid}-note" class="text-xs text-muted-foreground tabular-nums">Forecast 9.1 GB by 30 October · 0.9 GB pending</p>
</div>
```

**Phone.** Nothing changes below 768 px: the bar takes the width of its container, the 6 px and 8 px meters keep their heights at 390 px (measured), and the bar is no target. The parts keep their sizes, and a tick keeps its place as a percent of the track. So two ticks come closer on a narrow meter: keep them 4 px apart at 390 px. The label row wraps when the names of the ticks do not fit (measured at 390 px).

### skeleton

**Purpose.** A skeleton holds the place of content that loads, such as the rows of a list on its first load, so that the layout does not move when the content arrives. A list with cached data shows the cache and no skeleton. The skeleton is still: nothing in this file pulses or shimmers.

**Anatomy.** One `skeleton` slot, an empty `<div>` on `muted` with the 6 px radius. The page gives its size, and the round shape of a place for an avatar, with classes on the slot.

**Variants.** None. A row of a list shows bars of 12 px (`h-3`) in a row of 32 px, with the widths of its columns, such as `w-24` for a date. Nova pulses the bar (`animate-pulse`), and `stack.css` stops the pulse of every skeleton, so the bar needs no class for it (measured: no animation in both themes and both modes).

**Sizes.** The bar has no size of its own: it takes the height and the width of its classes. Its radius is 6 px, and `rounded-full` draws the place of an avatar.

**States.** None. The product replaces the skeleton with the content, or with the empty state or the error when there is no content.

**Tokens.** `skeleton`.

**Accessibility.** A skeleton has no text and no role, so a screen reader skips it. Mark the region with `aria-busy="true"` until the content arrives, and give a load that takes long a sentence in a `role="status"` region.

**Example.**

```svelte
<div aria-busy="true">
  {#each { length: 8 } as _, i (i)}
    <div class="flex h-8 items-center gap-3 pr-4 pl-3">
      <Skeleton class="h-3 w-20" /><Skeleton class="h-3 flex-1" /><Skeleton class="h-3 w-24" />
    </div>
  {/each}
</div>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a row of skeletons takes the size of the row that it holds the place of, such as the 52 px row of two lines of `## Layout`. Nothing else changes.

### spinner

**Purpose.** A spinner shows that an action of the person runs, and its one place is the working state inside a button, such as Save while the server answers (see Do's and Don'ts). A list or a pane that loads shows `skeleton` rows, and an upload shows its percent in words. The spinner turns, so it shows only while the person waits for their own action.

**Anatomy.** `Spinner` is the Lucide icon `loader-2`, an `<svg>` with the `status` role, 16 px (`size-4`), that turns (`animate-spin`). It sets no `data-slot` and no part attribute, so it has no key and takes the color of the text around it. In a button, `data-icon="inline-start"` puts it in the place of the icon.

**Variants.** None. A size class on the spinner, such as `size-3` in a button of size `xs`, sets another size.

**Sizes.** The spinner is 16 px by default. The box of a turning icon is larger than the icon and changes while it turns, from 16.8 px to 19 px for a 16 px icon in eight runs (measured), so never align other content to it.

**States.** None of its own. A button with a spinner is `disabled` while the action runs, and it keeps its label and its width (see `### button`).

**Tokens.** None. The spinner takes the color of its text, currentColor.

**Accessibility.** The `status` role and the name "Loading" in English make a screen reader announce it, so pass `aria-label` in the language of the product, such as "Saving". Nova keeps it turning under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, and `stack.css` stops every `animate-spin` there, the loading icon of a toast too, so the spinner needs no class for it (measured).

**Example.**

```svelte
<Button disabled={saving}>
  {#if saving}<Spinner data-icon="inline-start" aria-label="Saving" />{/if}
  Save
</Button>
```

**Phone.** Nothing changes below 768 px: the spinner keeps the size of its class.

### figure-band

**Purpose.** A band of key figures opens a summary page, such as the page of today or the summary of a campaign. It holds one to four figures, and a person reads the state of one area in it at a glance. A figure that counts the items of a list also opens that list. A key figure takes no card, as `## Do's and Don'ts` asks. So a page with one key figure also shows it in a band, under the title of the page. A fifth figure goes into a list or a table further down the page. A digest and a printed report show their key figures with the same parts (see `### Content kinds` and `### Figures and totals`). The band is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports it from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/FigureBand`, and gives the figures as text. The product formats each value.

**Anatomy.** The `figure-band` slot is a box that reads its own width (`@container`). It holds the list of the figures as a grid, with no ground of its own, no edge around it and no radius. Each figure (`figure-band-item`) has a 1 px `border` line under it, so a line lies under each row of figures. A figure holds three parts, 4 px apart (`gap-y-1`). The label (`figure-band-label`) is in `small` and `muted-foreground`. The value (`figure-band-value`) is in the `figure` step at 600 in `strong-foreground`, with tabular figures, and it never wraps. The note (`figure-band-note`) is in `small` and `muted-foreground`. It gives the change against a period in words, such as "3 more than yesterday" or "The same as last week". The value stands above the note, so the note does not repeat it. A change in percent names the value of the period before, such as "Up 17% from 3,322 in the 14 days before". A figure with no period gives its next fact there, such as "1 is due today". A change takes no color and no arrow alone, because a rise is not good for every figure. The figures of one row share three rows of the grid (`grid-rows-subgrid`). So a label of two lines moves every value of its row down together, and the values of the row stay on one line. An optional sparkline stands after the value, 12 px from it (`gap-x-3`), with its bottom on the base line of the value (see `### Sparkline`). In a figure that is too narrow, the sparkline moves under the value. The product sets the order of the figures, and the order never changes with a value. So each figure keeps its place on each visit.

**Variants.** The band reads the width of its own box, and not the window, because it also stands in a pane and in the item column. From a box of 672 px (`@2xl`), every figure of the band stands in one row: `@2xl:grid-cols-3` for three figures and `@2xl:grid-cols-4` for four. Below 672 px, two figures stand in a row (`grid-cols-2`). A third figure alone in its row takes the whole row (`col-span-2 @2xl:col-span-1`), so the line under it crosses the band. A band of one figure has one column in a box of each width: the list takes `grid-cols-1` in place of `grid-cols-2`. Its figure stands at the start of the band, where the first figure of a band of two stands. The figure is as wide as its parts, up to the width of the band (`justify-self-start`), and the line under it crosses the band. So the hover ground of a figure that is a link covers the figure, not the row. Like every band, a band of one takes no card and no ground. A figure that counts the items of a list is one link to that list. The address holds the filter of the figure, such as the invoices that are overdue. The link holds the three parts, so it covers the whole figure. A figure with no list is plain text, and one band can hold both kinds. The band crosses its region from edge to edge, as a band of `### Reading column` does. Each figure takes the side padding of the region. So the label of the first figure starts on the edge of the content above and under the band. On a page that padding is 16 px below 768 px and 24 px from 768 px (`px-4 md:px-6`). In a pane and in the item column it is 16 px (`px-4`). In a padded part, the band takes the negative margin of that padding. That is `-mx-4 md:-mx-6` on a page, and `-mx-4` in a pane and in the item column. In the item column the band stands above the content and the properties panel, so it takes the width of the column. A band right under an edge, such as the edge of the top bar, has no line over its first row. A band under text, such as a heading, takes a `border` line over its first row (`border-t`).

**Sizes.** Each figure has 12 px of padding above and under its parts (`py-3`). With a label and a note of one line each, a figure is 99 px high, the line under it included. Below 768 px it is 103 px high (measured at 1440 and 390 px). STACK put a band of four figures into the example, in a frame as wide as the window. On a page the band was 712 px wide at 768 px, 784 px at 1024 px and 1,200 px at 1440 px. There the four figures stood in one row. A page with a side column gave the band 720 px at 1280 px. In the item column the band was 443 px wide at 1024 px, 671 px at 1312 px and 799 px at 1440 px. So the four figures stood in two rows of two up to 1312 px, and in one row at 1440 px. At 390 px the band was 390 px wide, with two figures of 195 px in a row (measured). A band of one in the showcase was 1,006 px wide at 1280 px, and its figure 210 px. At 375 px they were 341 and 207 px (measured). Of these, the narrowest figure is a figure of four on a page at 768 px. It is 178 px wide and keeps 130 px for its value inside the padding. So a value is at most 130 px wide. At the `figure` step, €12,480 is 113.19 px wide and €128,450 is 130.75 px (measured). A larger value takes a shorter form, such as the compact notation of `Intl.NumberFormat`, which writes €128k in `en-GB`. That notation writes 128.450 € in `de-DE` (computed in Node 26), so a product checks the width in each locale that it ships. The sparkline is 80 by 24 px. The skeleton of a value is 28 by 80 px (`h-7 w-20`), and the skeleton of a note is 12 by 112 px (`h-3 w-28`).

**States.** Hover: a figure that is a link takes `muted` at 50% (`hover:bg-muted/50`), as a row of `### Lists` does. Focus: the focus ring of a row, inside the edge of the figure (`focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 focus-visible:ring-inset`). Loading: the band keeps its labels and its grid, and a still `skeleton` takes the place of each value, each note and each sparkline. The box takes `aria-busy="true"`, and a figure becomes a link when its value arrives. At 1440 px the band was 99 px high while it loaded and after it loaded. In the showcase at 390 px, it grew from 206 to 224 px when the values came, because two notes took two lines (measured). A band with cached figures shows the cache and no skeleton, as `### skeleton` says. Zero: a figure with nothing to count shows 0 and its note, such as "None since Monday", and it keeps its link. The band never hides a figure and never shows an empty band, so each figure keeps its place. Error: the error shows inline in the place of the band, as "Empty, loading and error" in `architecture/web-app.md` says. It says what failed and how to fix it, and it has a retry button.

**Tokens.** `figure-band`, `figure-band-label`, `figure-band-value`, `figure-band-note`. The hover ground is muted at 50%, so it has no key. The pair of strong-foreground on muted/50 covers the value under the pointer (see Colors). The pair of muted-foreground on muted/50 covers the label and the note.

**Accessibility.** The list takes `role="list"`, because Safari drops the list role of a list with no markers (not measured). Its `aria-label` names the figures, such as "Figures of today". The parts of a figure come in the order of the screen: the label, the value, then the note. So the name of a link is that text, such as "Overdue 3 1 is due today". A sparkline keeps its `img` role and its name (see `### Sparkline`). In a link, that name joins the name of the link after the value. An example is "Page views 3,887 Page views per day, 13 to 26 October: from 231 to 313 Up 17% from 3,322 in the 14 days before". STACK measured both names in the accessibility tree of Chromium. While the band loads, its box has `aria-busy="true"`, and a figure is no link, so a screen reader reaches no link without a value.

**Example.**

```svelte
<FigureBand label="Figures of today" figures={[
  { label: 'Open invoices', value: '41', note: 'The same as last week', href: '/invoices?status=open' },
  { label: 'Paid this month', value: '€12,480', note: '€1,050 less than September', href: '/invoices?status=paid&month=2026-10' },
  { label: 'Overdue', value: '3', note: '1 is due today', href: '/invoices?status=overdue' },
  { label: 'Failed bank imports', value: '0', note: 'None since Monday', href: '/imports?state=failed' }
]} />

<!-- In FigureBand.svelte: the box, the list and one figure that opens a list. -->
<!-- A band of one takes `grid-cols-1` in place of `grid-cols-2`, and `justify-self-start` on its figure. -->
<div data-slot="figure-band" class="@container print:break-inside-avoid">
  <ul role="list" aria-label="Figures of today" class="grid grid-cols-2 @2xl:grid-cols-4 print:grid-cols-4">
    <li data-slot="figure-band-item" class="row-span-3 grid min-w-0 grid-rows-subgrid border-b border-border">
      <a href="/invoices?status=overdue" class="row-span-3 grid cursor-pointer grid-rows-subgrid gap-y-1 px-4 py-3 outline-none hover:bg-muted/50 focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 focus-visible:ring-inset md:px-6">
        <span data-slot="figure-band-label" class="text-xs text-muted-foreground">Overdue</span>
        <span class="flex flex-wrap items-baseline gap-x-3 gap-y-1">
          <span data-slot="figure-band-value" class="text-3xl font-semibold whitespace-nowrap text-strong-foreground tabular-nums">3</span>
        </span>
        <span data-slot="figure-band-note" class="text-xs text-muted-foreground tabular-nums">1 is due today</span>
      </a>
    </li>
  </ul>
</div>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px each figure takes 16 px at its sides (`px-4`). In a box under 672 px, such as 390 px, two figures stand in a row, and the figure of a band of one stands at the start. The label and the note take the 13 px `small` step, and the value keeps 28 px, because the `figure` step has no phone value. A figure with a label and a note of one line each is 103 px high (measured at 390 px). So a figure that is a link is a target larger than 44 by 44 px.

**Print.** On paper every figure of the band stands in one row, as the key figures of a printed report do (see `### Figures and totals`). The column of A4 with the margins of `### Paper` is 657 px wide, under the line of 672 px. So a list of three or four figures takes `print:grid-cols-3` or `print:grid-cols-4`, and a third figure takes `print:col-span-1`. The box takes `print:break-inside-avoid`, so the band never splits over two pages. From windows of 1440 and 390 px, a PDF of A4 with those margins showed each band in one row, 657 px wide. The side padding was 16 px, and no value ran into it (measured with `page.pdf()`). The hover ground and a skeleton print with no ground (see "No ground" in `### A screen page in print`).

### attachment

**Purpose.** An attachment shows one file that belongs to a message, an item or a form, with its name, its kind or size, its upload state and its actions, such as Remove. `Attachment.Group` lines up several attachments in a row that scrolls. The files of a record with many files go into a `table` or a group of `item` rows.

**Anatomy.** `Attachment.Root` (`attachment`) is a box on `card` with `card-foreground` text, a `border` edge and the 8 px radius. It holds `Attachment.Media` (`attachment-media`, a 40 px square on `muted` with the 8 px radius, with an icon or an image), `Attachment.Content` with `Attachment.Title` (`attachment-title`, 500, one line with an ellipsis) and `Attachment.Description` (`attachment-description`, `small` in `muted-foreground`), and `Attachment.Actions` with `Attachment.Action` buttons (`attachment-action`), which are ghost buttons of 24 px. `Attachment.Trigger` (`attachment-trigger`) is a button that covers the box and opens the file.

**Variants.** `orientation="horizontal"`, the default, is a row of at least 160 px, and `vertical` is a card of 120 px with the media on top and the actions at its top right. `size` is `default`, `sm` or `xs`, and `Attachment.Media variant="image"` shows a picture.

**Sizes.** The default size has 8 px of padding and `body` text, and a row is 58 px high (measured). `sm` has 6 px of padding, `small` text and 32 px media (47 px high), and `xs` has 4 px of padding, `small` text and 28 px media with the 6 px radius (43 px high). The title is 13 px on a 16.25 px line (`leading-tight`).

**States.** Nova writes `data-state` from the `state` prop. `idle` draws a dashed edge, the mark of content that nobody confirmed yet (see Do's and Don'ts), `uploading` and `processing` dim an image to 60%, and nova shimmers the title there, `error` draws the edge in `destructive` at 30%, the media in `destructive` on its 10% tint and the description in `destructive` at 80%, and `done` is the rest look. The shimmer loops, so `stack.css` stops it on every attachment title, which then shows in its plain color with no class of the page (measured). Say the state and the percent in the description, such as "Uploading, 40%". A box with a link or a button inside takes `muted` at 50% on hover, and focus inside it draws a 1 px ring of `ring` at 50%.

**Tokens.** `attachment`, `attachment-sm`, `attachment-media`, `attachment-title`, `attachment-description`, `attachment-action-phone`. The grounds and the edges of the states carry an opacity, so they have no key.

**Accessibility.** An action has only an icon, so give it a name with the file, such as "Remove report.pdf", and give the trigger a name such as "Open report.pdf". The state is text in the description, so a screen reader reads it, and a `role="status"` region announces the end of an upload or a failure.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Attachment.Root state={file.state}>
  <Attachment.Media><FileTextIcon /></Attachment.Media>
  <Attachment.Content>
    <Attachment.Title>{file.name}</Attachment.Title>
    <Attachment.Description>{file.state === 'uploading' ? `Uploading, ${file.percent}%` : file.size}</Attachment.Description>
  </Attachment.Content>
  <Attachment.Actions>
    <Attachment.Action aria-label="Remove {file.name}" onclick={() => remove(file)}><XIcon /></Attachment.Action>
  </Attachment.Actions>
</Attachment.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px each action is 44 by 44 px (`attachment-action-phone`, measured), because it sets its own slot and the button key does not reach it. A row then grows to 62 px (measured), and in a vertical attachment the action covers the top right of the media. The title takes the 15 px `body` step and the description the 13 px `small` step.

### bubble
