**Purpose.** A bubble holds the text of one message in a conversation, such as a chat with an agent or a comment on a ticket. It sits inside a `message`, which adds the author, the time and the side. The answer of an agent with long text or code uses the `ghost` variant, so that it reads as a document.

**Anatomy.** `Bubble.Root` (`bubble`) is a column at most 80% wide that holds `Bubble.Content` (`bubble-content`), a box with 8 px by 12 px of padding, the 8 px radius and `body` text on a line height of 1.625. `Bubble.Reactions` (`bubble-reactions`) is a round pill on `muted` with a 3 px ring of `card`, over the top or the bottom edge of the box. `Bubble.Group` (`bubble-group`) stacks the bubbles of one author 8 px apart.

**Variants.** `default` is `primary-foreground` on `primary`, `secondary` is `secondary-foreground` on `secondary`, `muted` is `foreground` on `muted`, `outline` is `foreground` on `background` with a `border` edge, and `ghost` has no ground, no padding and the full width. `destructive` is `destructive` on its 10% tint (20% in dark), and `tinted` is `foreground` on a light tint of the hue of `primary`, which nova computes and the contrast check cannot read (measured from 10.6:1 to 16.2:1 in the four blocks). Use one variant for the person, such as `default`, and one for the others, such as `secondary` or `ghost`.

**Sizes.** A bubble of one line is 39 px high: a line of 21.1 px, 16 px of padding and the 2 px of its transparent edge (measured). `align="end"` puts the bubble at the right, and reactions are 24 px high.

**States.** With `child`, the content renders a button or a link. Hover then draws `primary/80` on `default`, the `muted` ground on `outline` and `ghost` (in dark `input/30` and `muted` at 50%), and a mix of the ground with 5% of `foreground` on `secondary` and `muted`, and focus-visible draws the `ring` edge and the 3 px halo. `[data-align]` and `[data-variant]` on the root mark the side and the variant.

**Tokens.** `bubble-content`, `bubble-content-secondary`, `bubble-content-muted`, `bubble-content-outline`, `bubble-reactions`. The destructive and tinted grounds carry an opacity or a computed color, so they have no key.

**Accessibility.** A bubble is plain text, and the `message` around it gives the author and the time. A bubble that is a button names its action in its text or with `aria-label`, such as "Edit the message". A reaction that a person can press is a `button` with a name in words, such as "Thumbs up, 2", and not the emoji alone.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Bubble.Root align="end"><Bubble.Content>Send the invoice to Ana today.</Bubble.Content></Bubble.Root>
<Bubble.Root variant="ghost"><Bubble.Content>Done. INV-0042 went to ana@example.org at 10:02.</Bubble.Content></Bubble.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the text takes the 15 px `body` step, and a bubble of one line is 42.4 px high (measured). A bubble that is a button or a link takes `class="max-md:min-h-11"`, which makes it 44 px high below 768 px (measured). A bubble of plain text is no target, so it has no phone key, which would add space under every short message.

### marker

**Purpose.** A marker is a quiet line of meta text between other content, such as a date between the messages of a conversation, an event in a log such as "Ana closed the ticket", or the start of the unread messages. It is no heading and no control. A link in it is underlined.

**Anatomy.** `Marker.Root` (`marker`) is a row of `body` text in `muted-foreground` with an optional `Marker.Icon` (`marker-icon`, 16 px, hidden from a screen reader) and `Marker.Content` (`marker-content`), 8 px apart. A link in it is underlined and turns `foreground` on hover.

**Variants.** `default` is the line alone. `separator` draws a 1 px line in `border` at each side of the text, with the text in the middle, and `border` draws a `border` line under the text with 8 px of padding. Use `separator` for a date between messages, and `default` for an event.

**Sizes.** A marker of one line is 20 px high, and 29 px with the `border` variant (measured). Its icon is 16 px, and its text keeps the `body` step.

**States.** None. A link in it keeps the states of a link.

**Tokens.** `marker`, `marker-border`. The lines of the separator variant are dividers in the border role, with no ratio.

**Accessibility.** The icon is hidden (`aria-hidden="true"`), so the text carries the whole meaning. A marker that starts the unread messages says so in its text, such as "New since 10:02". A date marker gives the date in words, and not only as a line.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Marker.Root variant="separator"><Marker.Content>Tuesday, 13 October</Marker.Content></Marker.Root>
<Marker.Root><Marker.Icon><CircleCheckIcon /></Marker.Icon><Marker.Content>Ana closed the ticket, 10:02</Marker.Content></Marker.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the text takes the 15 px `body` step, and a marker of one line stays 20 px high (measured). A link in a marker is a link in a line of text, which WCAG 2.2 exempts from the target size of criterion 2.5.8.

### message

**Purpose.** A message shows one entry of a conversation with its author: the avatar, a header with the name and the time, the bubbles and a footer, such as the chat with an agent beside a ticket. The place of the chat on the screen belongs to the product. A long conversation scrolls inside a `message-scroller`. A mail in a thread, which a person reads as a document, is a `mail-message`.

**Anatomy.** `Message.Root` (`message`) is a row in `body` with `Message.Avatar` (`message-avatar`), a column of at least 32 px on `muted` with the full radius that holds an `avatar`, and `Message.Content` (`message-content`), a column with 10 px gaps. The content holds `Message.Header` (`message-header`), the bubbles and `Message.Footer` (`message-footer`). The header and the footer are `small` at 500 in `muted-foreground` with 12 px of padding at the sides, and none beside a `ghost` bubble, and `Message.Group` (`message-group`) stacks messages 8 px apart.

**Variants.** `align="start"` puts the message at the left. `align="end"` turns the row around and puts the content at the right, for the messages of the person. An agent and a person use the same parts, and the name in the header says which one wrote.

**Sizes.** The header and the footer are lines of 16 px. The avatar column is at least 32 px wide and sits at the bottom of the row, beside the last bubble.

**States.** None of its own: `[data-align="start"]` or `[data-align="end"]` marks the side. A message that failed says so in its footer in words, such as "Not sent", with a `button` that retries.

**Tokens.** `message`, `message-avatar`, `message-header`, `message-footer`.

**Accessibility.** The header gives the author and the time in text, so a screen reader reads who wrote each bubble. A message with no header, after a message of the same author, still gives its author in a `sr-only` text. The time is a `<time>` element with a `datetime` attribute, and an action of a message, such as Copy or Retry, is a `button` with a name in words.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Message.Root>
  <Message.Avatar><Avatar.Root size="sm"><Avatar.Fallback>AG</Avatar.Fallback></Avatar.Root></Message.Avatar>
  <Message.Content>
    <Message.Header>Agent, <time datetime="2026-10-13T10:02">10:02</time></Message.Header>
    <Bubble.Root variant="ghost"><Bubble.Content>The draft of INV-0042 is ready.</Bubble.Content></Bubble.Root>
    <Message.Footer><Button variant="ghost" size="xs">Copy</Button></Message.Footer>
  </Message.Content>
</Message.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px a button in the footer, such as Copy or Retry, is a `button` slot and 44 px high (`button-phone`, measured with a button in a footer), so the footer grows with it. The header and the footer take the 13 px `small` step, and the bubbles the 15 px `body` step (measured).

### mail-message

**Purpose.** A mail message shows one message of a mail thread, such as the thread of a case in a support desk or a shared inbox. The thread stands in the item column of the work form, or on the page of its record. A person checks the sender and the recipients before a reply, and reads the body as a document. So the part has no bubble and no side, and its header holds the addresses. A chat, such as the chat with an agent beside a ticket, takes `message` and `bubble`. A variant of `message` would restyle a component of the registry: its header in `small`, its avatar beside the last bubble and its side. So STACK adds this part, and `message` keeps its chat form. The mail message is a part of STACK and not of the registry. A product imports one message from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/MailMessage` and a thread from `@stack-kit/stack/parts/MailThread`, and gives the mails, now and the time zone. STACK builds the parts from `avatar`, `button`, `badge`, `tooltip`, `attachment` and `unread-mark`.

**Anatomy.** The `mail-thread` slot is a `<section>` with a heading that names the thread and its count, such as "Messages · 4". The heading takes `small` at 600 and `muted-foreground`, as the heading of a section of a pane does. The messages follow in the order of their time, the oldest first, and a reply field of the product follows the last message. Each message is an `<article>` (`mail-message`) with a `border` divider below it, and the last message has none. Its content has 16 px above and below it, and its parts stand 12 px apart (measured). The header (`mail-message-header`) starts with line 1. Line 1 holds an `avatar` of size `xs`, the name of the sender at 500 (`mail-message-sender`) and the address in `muted-foreground` (`mail-message-address`). The time (`mail-message-time`) in `small` and `muted-foreground` ends line 1, and the `unread-mark` of an unread message follows it. A sender with no name in the mail shows its address at 500. The recipients (`mail-message-recipients`) follow in `small` and `muted-foreground`, 28 px from the edge, under the name (`pl-7`). They hold To, then Cc and Bcc, with a middle dot between the groups. Each recipient shows its name and its address in angle brackets, such as "Ana Turner <ana.turner@example.org>", or its address alone. The body (`mail-message-body`) is the plain text part of the mail as running text, at the left edge of the article. It takes the `reading` step and is at most 720 px wide (`max-w-180`). Each paragraph, which a blank line ends, is a `<p>`, and 12 px stand between two paragraphs (`gap-3`). The quoted part of an earlier message (`mail-message-quote`) follows the body, folded behind a `ghost` button of size `xs`. The files follow as an `Attachment.Group` of `attachment` parts of size `sm`, each with its name, its size and a trigger that opens the file.

**Variants.** Folded: each message that the person has read folds to one line (`mail-message-fold`), except the last message of the thread. So the newest message and every unread message stay open. The line is a button with the avatar, the name at 500, the first words of the body in `muted-foreground` and the time. It ends in an ellipsis. A click, Enter or Space opens the message, and the focus moves to the heading of the message (measured). Quote: the product finds the quoted part at the end of the body, such as the lines after "On 12 Nov 2026, Ana Turner wrote:". The thread already shows that earlier message, so the quote folds behind the button "Show the quoted text". "Hide the quoted text" folds it again. An open quote takes the `reading` step in `muted-foreground`, with a `border` edge of 1 px on its left and 12 px of padding (`border-l border-border pl-3`). Sent by: a message that the product sent names who sent it, in a line after the recipients (`mail-message-sent-by`), such as "Sent by Marko Ellis". When an agent sent it for a person, the line names the agent and the person, as "Words" in `architecture/web-app.md` asks. An example is "Sent by Mail agent for Marko Ellis". When a person sent the draft of an agent, the line names both: "Sent by Marko Ellis · Draft by Mail agent". A product may add one more fact after a middle dot, such as the version of the draft. Bcc: only a message that the product sent shows Bcc, because a message that came in does not hold it. Another language: the body keeps the `lang` of the mail, and a language that writes from right to left adds `dir="rtl"` (see `### Content in another language`). A body in a language that the product does not know takes `dir="auto"`. The header and the files keep the sides of the interface. Translation: a translation from an agent follows the body in a box (`mail-message-translation`). The box has a dashed edge in `input`, the 8 px radius and 12 px of padding. A badge names the agent, such as "Translated by Mail agent", and the text takes `muted-foreground` and the `lang` of the interface. That is the mark of content that no person confirmed (see `## Do's and Don'ts`). HTML: a mail with only an HTML part never shows that part as HTML in the page. It shows it in the frame of "Content that the product did not write" in `architecture/web-app.md`. A file that came in is content that the product did not write too, so it opens from the host of that content.

**Sizes.** Line 1 is 20 px high, the avatar is 20 px, and the time is 12 px on the 20 px line. The mark stands 6 px under the top of line 1, 8 px after the time (measured). The recipients and the line of the sender are `small`, 12 on 16 px. The body is `reading`, 14 on 21 px, and it starts 12 px under the header (measured). A folded line is 36 px high: a line of 20 px with 8 px above and below (`py-2`, measured). A file of size `sm` is 47 px high (measured).

**States.** Unread: an unread message shows the `unread-mark` at the end of line 1, and it never folds. "Read state" in `### Detail pane` says when the item of the thread counts as read. Each message that was unread then keeps its mark while the item shows, so the person sees what is new. The marks go when another item takes the column (measured). Hover: a folded line takes `muted` at 50% (`hover:bg-muted/50`), and its focus draws the 3 px `ring/50` halo inside its edge. Not sent: a reply that did not go out says so under the body, in `body` and `destructive` with an icon. Its words are "Not sent." and the reason, such as "The address h.weber@exmaple.de does not exist." An `outline` button of size `xs`, Retry, follows, as in the footer of `message`. The time shows its age, as "Formats" in `architecture/web-app.md` asks for a conversation, and its tooltip gives the full date and time.

**Tokens.** `mail-message`, `mail-message-fold`, `mail-message-fold-phone`, `mail-message-sender`, `mail-message-address`, `mail-message-time`, `mail-message-recipients`, `mail-message-sent-by`, `mail-message-body`, `mail-message-quote`, `mail-message-translation`. The files take the keys of `attachment`, the avatar the keys of `avatar`, and the mark its own key. The hover ground of a folded line is muted at 50%, so it has no key.

**Accessibility.** Each message is an `<article>` that the name of its sender names (`aria-labelledby`), so a screen reader goes from message to message. The name is a heading one level under the heading of the thread, such as an `h3` under an `h2`. The heading of a folded message holds its button, as an accordion does, with `aria-expanded="false"` and `aria-controls`. Chromium names that button with the name, the first words and the time (measured). The first words stop after 120 characters, so that the name stays short. The heading of an unread message starts with the word: "Unread, Hans Weber" (measured). The avatar stands beside the written name, so it takes `aria-hidden="true"`. The button of a quote takes `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`. The group of the files has a name, such as "Files of the message from Hans Weber". The time is no tab stop, so its tooltip opens on hover only.

**Example.**

```svelte
<!-- The thread in the item column: the oldest message first, and each read message but the last folded. -->
<MailThread mails={thread} {now} timeZone="Europe/Belgrade" locale="sr-Latn-RS" />

<!-- In MailMessage.svelte: a folded message. Its heading holds the button, and its content prints. -->
<article data-slot="mail-message" aria-labelledby="{id}-from" class="border-b border-border text-sm last:border-b-0">
  <h3 class="print:hidden">
    <button type="button" data-slot="mail-message-fold" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="{id}-content" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center gap-2 py-2 text-left outline-none hover:bg-muted/50 focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 focus-visible:ring-inset">
      <!-- The avatar, then <bdi id="{id}-from"> with the name at 500, the first words in muted-foreground and the time. -->
    </button>
  </h3>
  <div id="{id}-content" class="hidden flex-col gap-3 py-4 print:flex"><!-- The header, the body and the files. --></div>
</article>

<!-- An open message that the person has not read, with a translation from an agent. -->
<article data-slot="mail-message" aria-labelledby="{id}-from" class="border-b border-border text-sm last:border-b-0">
  <div class="flex flex-col gap-3 py-4">
    <header data-slot="mail-message-header" class="flex flex-col gap-1">
      <div class="flex items-start gap-2">
        <Avatar.Root size="sm" aria-hidden="true" class="data-[size=sm]:size-5"><Avatar.Fallback class="font-medium group-data-[size=sm]/avatar:text-2xs">H</Avatar.Fallback></Avatar.Root>
        <div class="min-w-0 flex-1 wrap-break-word">
          <h3 id="{id}-from" tabindex="-1" class="inline"><span class="sr-only">Unread,</span><bdi data-slot="mail-message-sender" class="font-medium">Hans Weber</bdi></h3>
          <span data-slot="mail-message-address" class="text-muted-foreground"> h.weber@example.de</span>
        </div>
        <time data-slot="mail-message-time" datetime="2026-11-18T09:05:00.000Z" class="shrink-0 text-xs leading-5 text-muted-foreground tabular-nums">10:05</time>
        <span data-slot="unread-mark" aria-hidden="true" class="mt-1.5 size-2 shrink-0 rounded-full border-4 border-foreground"></span>
      </div>
      <p data-slot="mail-message-recipients" class="pl-7 text-xs wrap-break-word text-muted-foreground">To <bdi>Support</bdi> &lt;support@example.com&gt;</p>
    </header>
    <div data-slot="mail-message-body" lang="de" class="flex max-w-180 flex-col gap-3 text-md wrap-break-word"><p>Guten Tag,</p></div>
    <div data-slot="mail-message-translation" lang="en-GB" class="flex max-w-180 flex-col items-start gap-3 rounded-lg border border-dashed border-input p-3 text-md text-muted-foreground">
      <Badge variant="outline" class="border-input">Translated by Mail agent</Badge>
      <p>Good day,</p>
    </div>
  </div>
</article>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the thread is a part of the inner screen of its item. A folded line is 44 px high (`mail-message-fold-phone`, measured at 390 px), because the line is a target. The name and the address wrap in the header, and the time keeps its place at the end of line 1. The name takes the 15 px `body` step, and the time and the recipients take the 13 px `small` step. The body, an open quote and a translation take the `reading-phone` step, 15 on 22 px, as large as the name (measured). Retry is 44 px high (`button-phone`) and stays on the line of the reason (measured).

**Print.** Every message prints open, because paper cannot open a fold. The folded line takes `print:hidden`, and the content of the message takes `print:flex` beside its `hidden`. A folded quote stays folded, because the thread prints the earlier message whole. The button of a quote and Retry take `print:hidden`. From 1440 and 390 px, the six messages of the showcase printed with their bodies, and no button text printed (measured).

### message-scroller

**Purpose.** A message scroller holds a conversation in a box of a fixed height, keeps the newest message in view while new messages arrive, and offers a button that goes back to the end. Use it for a chat or a log that grows at its end. A list of records uses the scroll of the page or a `scroll-area`.

**Anatomy.** `MessageScroller.Provider` holds the state, and `MessageScroller.Root` (`message-scroller`) fills the box. `MessageScroller.Viewport` (`message-scroller-viewport`) scrolls, with a fade at its bottom edge, and holds `MessageScroller.Content` (`message-scroller-content`), a column of `MessageScroller.Item` entries (`message-scroller-item`) 24 px apart. `MessageScroller.Button` (`message-scroller-button`) is an icon button with an arrow, on `background` with a `border` edge and the 6 px radius, 16 px above the bottom edge in the middle.

**Variants.** `direction="end"`, the default, scrolls to the newest message, and `direction="start"` scrolls to the first message and turns the arrow. `defaultScrollPosition` on the provider sets where a conversation opens, and `messageId` on each item lets `scrollToMessage` reach it. `scrollAnchor` on an item keeps it in place while older messages load above it.

**Sizes.** The scroller takes the size of its container, which needs a height, such as `h-72`. The button is 28 by 28 px (`spacing.control-sm`). An item off the screen keeps an estimated height of 160 px (`contain-intrinsic-size`) until it shows (measured).

**States.** The button shows only when the view is away from the end, with `[data-active="true"]`, and `[data-active="false"]` hides it with a fade and makes it `inert`. The viewport writes `[data-scrollable]`, and `[data-autoscrolling]` while it follows new content.

**Tokens.** `message-scroller-button`, `message-scroller-button-phone`.

**Accessibility.** The viewport has the `region` role, the name "Messages" in English and `tabindex="0"`, so a keyboard can scroll it, and the product passes `aria-label` in its own language. The content has the `log` role, which announces the new messages. The button has a hidden name, "Scroll to end" or "Scroll to start", in English, so give it children with a name in the language of the product.

**Example.**

```svelte
<div class="h-96 rounded-lg border">
  <MessageScroller.Provider defaultScrollPosition="end">
    <MessageScroller.Root>
      <MessageScroller.Viewport aria-label="Chat with the agent" class="p-3">
        <MessageScroller.Content>
          {#each messages as m (m.id)}<MessageScroller.Item messageId={m.id}>{@render message(m)}</MessageScroller.Item>{/each}
        </MessageScroller.Content>
      </MessageScroller.Viewport>
      <MessageScroller.Button><ArrowDownIcon /><span class="sr-only">Go to the newest message</span></MessageScroller.Button>
    </MessageScroller.Root>
  </MessageScroller.Provider>
</div>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the button is 44 by 44 px (`message-scroller-button-phone`, measured), because it sets its own slot and the button key does not reach it. A swipe scrolls the viewport, and the conversation fills the height of the screen between the header and the message field.

### questionnaire

**Purpose.** A questionnaire asks a few questions one at a time, each with choices or a short answer, such as the setup of a new workspace or a short survey. It shows its progress, it moves with Back, Skip and Next, and it submits at the last question. A form that the person fills in on one view is a form of `field` rows, not a questionnaire.

**Anatomy.** `Questionnaire.Root` (`questionnaire`) holds `Questionnaire.Progress` (`questionnaire-progress`, "Question 1 of 3" in `small` at 500 in `muted-foreground`), one `Questionnaire.Item` (`questionnaire-item`) for each question, and `Questionnaire.Actions` (`questionnaire-actions`). An item holds `Questionnaire.Title` (`section` at 500), `Questionnaire.Description` (`body` in `muted-foreground`), `Questionnaire.Choices` with `Questionnaire.Choice` rows or a `Questionnaire.Input`, and `Questionnaire.Error` in `destructive`. The actions are buttons: `Previous` and `Skip` are `outline`, and `Next` and `Submit` are `default`.

**Variants.** A choice is a radio button by default, and `multiple` on the item makes its choices check boxes. A choice row has an `input` edge, the 8 px radius, an indicator of 16 px (round for a radio button, 4 px for a check box), the label and an optional `Questionnaire.ChoiceDescription`. `shortcuts="letters"` on the root shows a key mark of 20 px at the right of each choice, in `font-mono` at 10 px, which is the one monospace text of the components.

**Sizes.** A choice row is at least 44 px high at every width (`min-h-11`), with 10 px by 12 px of padding (measured). The input and the buttons are 32 px high from 640 px and 44 px below 640 px (`min-h-11 sm:min-h-0`). The parts of an item are 16 px apart, and the choices and the actions 8 px.

**States.** A choice writes the presence attributes `[data-checked]` or `[data-unchecked]`, with no `data-state`. A checked choice takes the `muted` ground, an edge of `primary` at 40% and a filled indicator in `primary`, `[data-disabled]` draws a choice at 50% opacity, and `[data-invalid]` with `aria-invalid="true"` draws the edge in `destructive` and shows the error, which takes the `alert` role. The item writes `[data-active]` and `data-status` (`answered` or `unanswered`), and the root writes `data-current` and `data-total`.

**Tokens.** `questionnaire-progress`, `questionnaire-title`, `questionnaire-description`, `questionnaire-choice`, `questionnaire-choice-checked`, `questionnaire-choice-indicator`, `questionnaire-choice-indicator-checked`, `questionnaire-choice-shortcut`, `questionnaire-input`, `questionnaire-input-invalid`, `questionnaire-error`.

**Accessibility.** The progress has the `progressbar` role, the name "Questionnaire progress" and the value text "Question 1 of 3", all in English, so set its name and its text in the language of the product. Each choice is a native radio button or check box (measured) under its label. The item lists its keys in `aria-keyshortcuts`: up and down move between the choices, left goes back, right goes on when the question has an answer, Ctrl+Enter or ⌘+Enter goes on, and a letter picks its choice.

**Example.**

```svelte
<Questionnaire.Root {items} shortcuts="letters" class="max-w-md">
  <Questionnaire.Progress aria-label="Setup progress" />
  <Questionnaire.Item name="role" required>
    <Questionnaire.Title>What is your role?</Questionnaire.Title>
    <Questionnaire.Choices>
      <Questionnaire.Choice value="owner">Owner</Questionnaire.Choice>
      <Questionnaire.Choice value="accountant">Accountant</Questionnaire.Choice>
    </Questionnaire.Choices>
    <Questionnaire.Error>Choose a role.</Questionnaire.Error>
  </Questionnaire.Item>
  <Questionnaire.Actions>
    <Questionnaire.Previous class="max-md:min-h-11!">Back</Questionnaire.Previous>
    <Questionnaire.Next class="max-md:min-h-11!">Next</Questionnaire.Next>
    <Questionnaire.Submit class="max-md:min-h-11!">Finish</Questionnaire.Submit>
  </Questionnaire.Actions>
</Questionnaire.Root>
```

**Phone.** Below 640 px nova makes the input and the buttons 44 px high (measured at 390 px). From 640 to 767 px they are 32 px (`sm:min-h-0`, measured at 700 px), and a phone key cannot reach them, because their utility class beats it. So give each of them `class="max-md:min-h-11!"`: 44 px at 390 and 700 px, and 32 px at 1280 px (measured), with `!` because the build of Tailwind writes `max-md:` before `sm:`.

### chart

**Purpose.** A chart shows how numbers compare or change. Use a bar chart with one bar for each category to compare a few categories, such as the orders of each quarter. Use stacked bars or stacked areas for categories over time, such as the tickets closed each month by type. Use a donut for the parts of one whole, such as the visits of a month by source. Use a heat map for a count over two dimensions, such as the sign-ins of a week by weekday and time of day, or for the clicks over a screenshot of a page. Use a line chart for one or two metrics over time, such as the page views of each day. Use a sparkline for the shape of one metric over time in a row or beside a key figure. One key figure is text in the `figure` step, a quota is a meter of `### progress`, and a flow from one page or state to the next is a table (see `### Flow`), not a chart. In a row, a card or a header, the parts of one whole are words, not a chart (see `### Parts of a whole`). A chart is black and white, in the pattern style, unless its product or the person takes another style. `## Charts` gives the rules that every chart follows. `@stack-kit/stack/parts/PatternChart` draws a bar chart, stacked bars, stacked areas and a donut by these rules (`kind`). A stacked bar of one category is a bar chart over time, such as the spend of each day, and it has no legend. `tick` gives the short name of an x value on the axis of a stacked chart. A height class in its `class`, such as `h-56`, sets the height of the chart container, and the other classes place the part.

**Anatomy.** A chart is a `<figure>` with a `<figcaption>` that names it and a summary of one or two sentences. `Chart.Container` (`chart`) holds the plot, in `small`. Nova gives the container a ratio of 16 to 9 (`aspect-video`), and a class with a fixed height, such as `h-64 w-full`, replaces it. A chart of LayerChart from `layerchart/svg`, such as `BarChart`, `AreaChart`, `LineChart` or `PieChart`, draws the plot in the container. A bar chart, an area chart and a line chart have a value axis with three ticks, a grid line for each tick in `border` at 50%, a base line at 0 in `border`, and axis labels in `small` and `muted-foreground`. Each category has its shapes, and labels on the chart name them: the value above each bar, and on a donut a leader line to the name and the value of each slice, with the total in the center. `Chart.Tooltip` draws a box of at least 128 px on `background`, with a `border` edge at 50% and the 8 px radius. The box has 6 px by 10 px of padding and `shadow-xl`, a heading at 500 and one row for each category (measured). The product writes five more parts from `## Charts`: the svg of the patterns, the tile, the labels on the chart, the legend of a stacked chart or of a heat map, and the data table. A line chart adds four parts from `### Line chart`: the change text above the plot and the reference lines (`chart-reference`). The other two are the numbered discs of the annotations (`chart-annotation`) with their guides (`chart-annotation-line`), and the list of the annotations under the chart. Only the container sets a `data-slot`, so the key of each other part takes its name after `chart`, such as `chart-legend-tile`.

**Variants.** A chart has one of three styles (see `### Styles`). The pattern style is the default: the fill of category n is its pattern, `url(#<prefix>-pattern-<n>)`, with marks in `chart-ink` on the ground under the chart. The color pattern style has the same fill, and the chart writes the marks of pattern n in `var(--chart-n)`, with the thicker marks of `### Patterns`. The color style fills category n with `var(--chart-n)`. In every style each shape has a 1 px edge in the ground under the chart. A chart is one of eight kinds. A bar chart with one bar for each category is a `BarChart` with `x="key"`, `y="value"`, `c="key"` and a `cRange` of the fills in the order of the data. Stacked bars are a `BarChart` with `seriesLayout="stack"`, and stacked areas are an `AreaChart` with `seriesLayout="stack"`. Each category of a stacked chart is a series, and the series takes its fill in its `props`. A series carries only its fill, because LayerChart gives the props of a series to each mark of it, also to the top line of an area. A donut is a `PieChart` with `innerRadius={0.6}` and `props.pie` set to `{ sort: null }`, so that the slices follow the order of the categories from 12 o'clock. Each slice takes its fill in the `props` of its datum. A heat map is a `Chart` of `layerchart/svg` with a `<rect>` for each cell. Its cells take the five steps of `### Heat map`, and not the fills of the categories. A line chart is a `LineChart` with one series for each line, and the series takes the stroke of its line in its `props`. An area of one category is an `AreaChart` with one series, which takes its fill in its `props`. The chart draws the top line of that area apart, as the line of its category (see `### Line chart`). A sparkline is a `LineChart` of one series with no axis, no grid, no rule, no highlight and no tooltip (see `### Sparkline`).

**Sizes.** The container takes its height from a class, such as 256 px (`h-64`), and the width of its column. The plot of a bar chart leaves 20 px above it for the values, and a value stands 6 px above its bar. A donut leaves 28 px above and under the ring and 96 px at each side for its labels. The ring is 40% of the outer radius wide, and the outer radius is 100 px in a container of 256 px (measured). A leader line runs 12 px out from the ring and 16 px to the side, and its label stands 4 px past it. The name and the value of a label are 15 px apart, and two labels of one side are at least 34 px apart. A legend item is 22 px high (measured): a 16 px line, 2 px of padding above and below, and a clear 1 px edge. Legend items are 12 px apart in a row and 4 px apart between rows. The legend starts 12 px under the plot (measured). The tile is 12 by 12 px (`spacing.legend-tile`) in the legend and in the tooltip (measured). The edges, the leader lines, the top line of a stacked area, the base line and the edge of a heat map cell are 1 px. A line, the top line of an area of one category and the comparison line are 2 px, and line 2 has dots of 3 px whose centers are 6 px apart. A reference line and the guide of an annotation are 1 px. The disc of an annotation is 16 px wide and stands 4 px under the base line. A chart with annotations leaves 40 px under the base line, for the discs and the names of the time axis (measured). A sparkline is 80 by 24 px (`w-20 h-6`) at every width (measured).

**States.** A legend item is on, with `[aria-pressed="true"]`, while its category shows, and off, with `[aria-pressed="false"]`, while its category is hidden. An off item keeps its label in `muted-foreground` and its tile at 30% (`[&_svg]:opacity-30`, measured). Focus-visible draws the `ring` edge and the 3 px halo on a legend item. The value above a stacked bar is the total of the categories that show: after a click on one legend item, each bar shows the value of that category (measured). The tooltip shows while the pointer rests on the plot, and the table button sets `aria-expanded`.

**Tokens.** `chart`, `chart-series-1`, `chart-series-2`, `chart-series-3`, `chart-series-4`, `chart-series-5`, `chart-series-6`, `chart-series-7`, `chart-series-8`, `chart-bar`, `chart-arc`, `chart-area-line`, `chart-gap`, `chart-leader`, `chart-label`, `chart-label-value`, `chart-total`, `chart-total-label`, `chart-base-line`, `chart-axis`, `chart-legend-item`, `chart-legend-item-off`, `chart-legend-tile`, `chart-tooltip`, `chart-tooltip-label`, `chart-line`, `chart-comparison`, `chart-reference`, `chart-annotation`, `chart-annotation-line`. A series key holds the color of its category: the marks of the color pattern style, the fill of the color style and a line in the two color styles. The key of a line holds chart-ink, its color in the pattern style. The keys of the comparison line and of a reference line hold muted-foreground in every style, and the key of an annotation holds its disc and its number. The gap key holds the edge of a bar, a slice and the top line of a stacked area in every style, which takes the ground under the chart. The edge keys of a bar, an arc and the line of a stacked area hold the same value as the gap key, so that a product that reads them gets the gap. The marks of a pattern are chart-ink, and the grid lines are border at 50%, so neither has a key. The value of a bar and the name on a donut are small at 500. The key of the tooltip holds its edge at full strength, and nova draws the edge at 50%. An off tile is at 30%. The steps of a heat map are chart-ink or chart-heat at an opacity, and a key holds no opacity, so they have no key.

**Accessibility.** Each chart has the accessibility layer of `## Charts`: the svg `title`, `aria-describedby` to the summary and the table, and a table button. When the table shows, it takes the look of `### table`. A legend item of a stacked chart is a `button`: Tab reaches it, Enter and Space toggle it, and `aria-pressed` tells whether its category shows (measured). The legend of a heat map is a list, and its items are not buttons. The patterns tell the categories apart without color. The name of each category is text on the chart or in the legend, and in the tooltip and the table. The labels on the chart are `aria-hidden`, because the table gives a screen reader the same values. In the color style the names in text carry the categories, because color alone fails WCAG 1.4.1. The data table of a line chart has a column for each line, for the comparison and for the annotations, and a second table lists the reference lines. The list of the annotations under the chart is text. A sparkline has no table, so its box has the `img` role and an accessible name that states the first and the last value (see `### Sparkline`). One gap is open: the tooltip layer of a bar chart adds a second `svg` with `role="figure"` and no name (measured). A screen reader can stop on it.

**Example.**

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import { BarChart } from 'layerchart/svg';
  const uid = $props.id();
  const prefix = `p${uid}`;
  const quarters = [{ key: 'q1', label: 'Q1' }, { key: 'q2', label: 'Q2' }];
  const data = [{ key: 'q1', value: 84 }, { key: 'q2', value: 112 }];
  const config = { q1: { label: 'Q1', color: 'var(--chart-1)' }, q2: { label: 'Q2', color: 'var(--chart-2)' } };
  const fills = data.map((d, i) => `url(#${prefix}-pattern-${i + 1})`);
  const ticks = [0, 60, 120];
  const edge = { stroke: 'var(--background)', strokeWidth: 1 };
  const svg = { title: 'Orders per quarter', 'aria-describedby': `${prefix}-summary ${prefix}-table` };
  const label = (key: string) => quarters.find((q) => q.key === key)?.label ?? key;
  let table = $state(false);
</script>

<figure class="flex min-w-0 flex-col gap-1">
  <figcaption class="text-sm font-medium">Orders per quarter</figcaption>
  <p id="{prefix}-summary" class="text-xs text-muted-foreground">Orders grow from 84 in Q1 to 112 in Q2.</p>
  <StackPatternDefs {prefix} />
  <Chart.Container {config} class="h-64 w-full">
    <BarChart {data} x="key" y="value" c="key" cRange={fills} yDomain={[0, 120]} yNice={false} rule={false}
      padding={{ top: 20, bottom: 20, left: 32, right: 4 }}
      props={{ svg, bars: edge, yAxis: { ticks }, grid: { yTicks: ticks }, xAxis: { format: label } }}>
      {#snippet belowMarks({ context })}
        <line x1={context.xRange[0]} x2={context.xRange[1]} y1={context.yScale(0)} y2={context.yScale(0)} class="stroke-border" />
      {/snippet}
      {#snippet aboveMarks({ context })}
        <g aria-hidden="true">
          {#each data as d (d.key)}
            <text x={context.xScale(d.key) + context.xScale.bandwidth() / 2} y={context.yScale(d.value) - 6} text-anchor="middle" class="fill-foreground text-xs font-medium tabular-nums">{d.value}</text>
          {/each}
        </g>
      {/snippet}
      {#snippet tooltip()}<Chart.Tooltip hideLabel formatter={tooltipRow} />{/snippet}
    </BarChart>
  </Chart.Container>
  <div><Button variant="outline" size="sm" class="print:hidden" aria-expanded={table} aria-controls="{prefix}-table" onclick={() => (table = !table)}>{table ? 'Hide' : 'Show'} the data as a table</Button></div>
  <div class={table ? 'mt-2' : 'sr-only'}>
    <table id="{prefix}-table"><caption>Orders per quarter</caption><!-- a row header for each quarter, and its value --></table>
  </div>
</figure>
```

**Phone.** Below 768 px the text of the axes, of the labels on the chart, of the legend and of the tooltip takes the 13 px `small` step (measured). A legend item is a target that sets no `data-slot`, so no phone key reaches it. Give it `class="max-md:min-h-11"`: it is then 44 px high below 768 px and 22 px from 768 px (measured). The legend wraps onto more rows and pushes the content under it down, and the plot keeps its height. A donut keeps 96 px at each side for its labels, so at 390 px in a card its outer radius is 67 px (measured). A tap on a bar or on a line chart shows no tooltip (measured at 390 px with touch input). So on a phone, the labels on the chart and the data table give the values. The labels at the right end of the lines take the 13 px `small` step, and the number in the disc of an annotation the 12 px `meta` step. The plot then leaves more room at its right, so at 390 px a line chart of 28 days names the first day and two or three Mondays (measured). A chart takes the width of its column, and its svg then holds that width. The svg can keep the chart wider than the screen: see `### Rendering` for `min-w-0` and `grid-cols-1`, and "Page width" in `### Width bands`.

## Do's and Don'ts

**Do.**

- Use only the tokens of this file: no hex value, no raw size, duration or shadow, and no arbitrary Tailwind value such as `p-[13px]`.
- Change the look of a product through a theme in the form of the shadcn Theme tab, and never through a change to a component.
- Show each state as a shape, an icon or a word as well as a color, so that a grayscale screenshot still shows every state.
- Give each status of a product one color and one icon, and show it with a word. A status color shows only on its icon, its badge, a meter fill and a timeline dot. The one exception is the text of a due date that comes close or passes (see below).
- Mark content that no person has confirmed yet, such as a proposal from the system or a draft from an agent, with a dashed edge in `input` (`border-dashed`) and a badge. Its text uses `muted-foreground` and passes 4.5:1. A dashed edge marks nothing else, except an empty drop zone, which takes a dashed `border` edge, and the frame of content that the product did not write, which keeps its dashed edge in `input` also after a person confirms it (see "Content that the product did not write" in `architecture/web-app.md`).
- Mark a stale proposal with a warning icon, disable its accept action with the reason as `### button` says, and offer a refresh. The action stays in the tab order with `aria-disabled="true"`, and its reason is a `tooltip` and its accessible description.
- Let a due date use `warning` or `destructive` as text when it comes close or passes, always with an icon on the same row. This is the one status color on text.
- Give every clickable element the hand pointer (`cursor-pointer`).

**Don't.**

- No gradient, glow, glass, emoji, illustration or decorative 3D icon.
- No dashboard of big rounded cards with shadows. A summary page uses lists and meters on the grid, in bands with dividers.
- No shadow on a static surface, and no radius above 8 px. The full radius serves only the shapes that are round by nature and the status badge.
- No centered page layout, also not for a sign-in page, and no centered empty state.
- No colored edge on a row or a card, also not for an overdue or a blocking item. The edge of an invalid input is the one colored edge.
- No color as the only carrier of a state, and no faint text that carries information.
- No `faint-foreground` on a ground other than `background`, such as `muted`, `selected`, a hover ground, `popover` or the sidebar. Use `muted-foreground` there.
- No spinner in a list or a pane, and no loop. A skeleton row stays still. The one spinner is the working state inside a button.
- No success toast for a trivial action.
- No dialog where a pane works. A dialog is for one task that ends in one step: a confirmation, one create form, or a list that the person checks before one action (see `### dialog`). All other work goes into a pane, a sheet or a page.
- No page hero and no big title inside a list.
- No tinted chip on a tinted ground.
- No `primary` and no feedback color as the status of a product item, and no status color on a large area. The one exception is the text of a due date that comes close or passes (see Do).

## Themes

A theme sets the value of each color role and of `--radius`, in light and in dark. It never adds a name and never removes one. Each theme is one file in `themes/`. At the first load, a page takes exactly one theme file: by the last import of the CSS (see Overview), or by the one `<link>` of a theme switch (see "A theme switch in a product" below).

| Theme | File | Look | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `default` | `themes/default.css` | Neutral grays with no hue, and a near-black primary | `npm run theme` writes it from the front matter. Never edit it by hand. |
| `graphite` | `themes/graphite.css` | A gray that is nearly neutral with a faint blue, an indigo primary, and a sidebar that is dimmer than the page | Written by hand, in the form of the Theme tab. |

**The form of a theme file.** A theme file has the form of the Theme tab of the create page at `ui.shadcn.com/create`: comments, a `:root` block with the light values, and a `.dark` block with the dark values. `--radius` is in `:root` only. Nothing else goes into the file: no `@theme`, no `@layer` and no other selector. Each block of a theme file in this repository holds the names of `themes/default.css`, and `npm run theme -- --check` refuses a theme file with a missing or an extra name. The commit hook runs that check.

**The theme generator.** The generator, `scripts/generate.mjs`, makes the theme of a product, and a block of the Theme tab is the second way. "Make a theme with the generator" in `README.md` gives the command of a product. "How a product takes a theme from the create page" below gives the steps of the Theme tab. The generator takes three inputs: a base color, an accent color and a contrast level from 0 to 100. It writes a theme file in the form above, with every color role of this file in light and in dark as an `oklch()` value, and `--radius`. The maintainer runs it in this repository:

```sh
npm ci
npm run generate -- --base "#f7f3ec" --accent "oklch(0.55 0.13 45)" --contrast 30 --out brand.css
```

**The inputs of the generator.** A color is `oklch(L C H)`, `#rgb` or `#rrggbb`. The base color is the page of one mode: of light mode when its lightness is 0.5 or more, and of dark mode below that. Its hue and its chroma tint every gray of the theme. The accent color is the primary fill of the same mode. The contrast level moves the text and the edges. At 30 the theme has the ratios of `graphite`, except one ground: `graphite` has `muted` at 1.04 in dark. At 0 the text and the edges are softer, down to the floors of 4.5:1 and 3:1, and at 100 the text is black on a light page. An input that the command lacks comes from `theme-inputs`. `--charts` takes the chart colors of a theme of `themes/` by its name, such as `--charts graphite`. Without `--charts`, or with `--charts default`, every generated theme takes the default set, the chart colors of the front matter. `--radius` sets another radius, such as `--radius 0.75rem`, and without it the radius is `rounded.lg`. `density` sets no color, so the generator does not read it.

**The scales of the generator.** The generator works in OKLCH on two scales of 10 steps, 100 to 1000, and each step has one role. Each step of the base scale has a contrast ratio against step 100, and it keeps the hue and the chroma of the base color:

| Step | Role in the base scale | Ratio to step 100 at contrast 30, light / dark |
|---|---|---|
| 100 | The page: `background` | 1 |
| 200 | The hover ground: `accent` and `border-faint`, and `secondary` in dark and on a light page lighter than L 0.990 | 1.09 / 1.08 |
| 300 | The active ground: `muted` and `sidebar-accent` | 1.14 |
| 400 | The border: `border` and `sidebar-border` | 1.27 / 1.37 |
| 500 | The edge of a control: `input` | 3.05 |
| 600 | The strong edge: `border-strong` | 3.9 |
| 700 | The faint text: `faint-foreground` | 4.6 |
| 800 | No role | 5.3 |
| 900 | The muted text: `muted-foreground` | 6.3 |
| 1000 | The text: `foreground`, and each `-foreground` of a ground | 12.6 / 14.6 |

From step 400 up, the contrast level k raises each ratio to the power 1 + (k − 30) / 280, and the floors of 3:1 for an edge and 4.5:1 for a text hold. `strong-foreground` and `chart-ink` take the ratio of step 1000 to the power 1.1. The accent scale sets only the two steps that have a role. Step 700 is `primary` and `ring`, and in the other mode it keeps the ratio of the accent color against its page. Step 300 is `selected`, with 11% of the accent chroma in light and 33% in dark. The page of the other mode keeps the softness of the base color: a light page at L 1 − d gives a dark page at L 0.145 + 1.7 × d. `sidebar`, `card` and `popover` take the lightness of the page with a fixed offset. In light, `secondary` takes the card ground, so that it is lighter than the page, as a card is. When the card ground is less than 0.01 lighter than the page, such as on a light page lighter than L 0.990, `secondary` takes step 200, which is darker than the page. `default`, on a white page, is such a theme. In dark, `secondary` takes step 200, which is lighter than the page. `scripts/lib/generate.mjs` gives the step of each role.

**The roles with a fixed hue.** `destructive`, `success`, `warning`, `info`, `link`, `chart-1` to `chart-8` and `chart-heat`, the tag roles and the diff roles keep the hue and the chroma of the front matter in each mode, and `overlay` keeps its value. So a label and a state keep their hues in every theme of the generator, and a chart keeps the hues of its chart set. Each of these roles starts at its value in the front matter, and the lift sets its lightness for the new grounds. With `--charts`, `chart-1` to `chart-8` and `chart-heat` start at the values of that theme file in place of the front matter. The text on a fill is white when the fill is darker than L 0.7, and else it is the page of dark mode. When no lightness passes with that text, the generator tries the other text once, in each mode.

**The lift.** The generator then checks every pair of `npm run contrast` in both modes. For each pair below its minimum, it moves the lightness of the text, the edge or the mark by 0.005 away from its ground, and it checks again. When the text of a pair is fixed, such as the white text on `primary`, it moves the fill away from the text. It lowers a chroma only to keep a moved color inside sRGB, and it never changes a hue, as "Tuning" below says. The command prints each value that it moved, with the pair that moved it. Then it runs `npm run contrast` on the file, and it fails when a pair is below its minimum. When no lightness passes, for example when a fill must be lighter for one pair and darker for another, the command stops and writes no file.

**The generator and the themes of this repository.** The inputs of `graphite` are `--base "oklch(0.982 0.001 282.734)" --accent "oklch(0.608 0.14 276.289)" --contrast 30 --charts graphite`, and the inputs of `default` are `theme-inputs`. The generator gives neither theme back exactly, so both files stay as they are: `graphite` is written by hand, and `npm run theme` writes `default` from the front matter. The preview page `/themes/compare` runs the same generator in the browser. It shows a theme of this repository beside the generated theme, and it lists the difference of each role in L, C, h and alpha. `npm run generate -- --self-test` writes seven themes, from the inputs of both themes and from five more input sets, and runs the contrast check on each. One of the sets needs the other text on `primary` in both modes. The self-test checks that the generated `graphite` has the chart colors of `graphite.css`, and a light `secondary` lighter than the page and within 0.007 in L of `graphite.css`. It checks that the generated `default`, which names no chart set, keeps the hues of the default set. Then the self-test checks that the generator refuses a mid-gray page, on which no dark text reaches 4.5:1. `npm run check` runs the self-test.

**How a product takes a theme from the create page.** A product takes its own theme from the create page. Copy the CSS block of the Theme tab into a theme file, and import that file in place of a theme of this repository. Never take a theme as a preset code. The same preset code gives a different theme on the create page of shadcn-svelte, and only the CSS block carries the same values to both sites. A block from the Theme tab sets the shadcn names only. `stack.css` gives each added role, such as `faint-foreground` or `success`, its value from `theme-fallbacks`, so that every class still works. Such a block needs the work of the next paragraph.

**The theme author sets the chart values, `chart-ink` and `chart-heat`.** A block from the Theme tab lacks `chart-6` to `chart-8`, `chart-ink` and `chart-heat`, and it keeps the same chart colors in light and in dark. A light chart color fails 3:1 on the light ground of light mode, and a dark one fails on the dark ground of dark mode. So the author sets `chart-1` to `chart-8`, `chart-ink` and `chart-heat` in both blocks. The author tunes the lightness of each chart color until it passes 3:1 on `background`, `card` and `popover` of its mode, and keeps its hue. The author gives `chart-heat` the hue of `chart-1`, with the chroma of `chart-1`, lowered only to stay inside sRGB. The author tunes its lightness until it passes 3:1 at 50% on the same grounds (see `### Heat map`). A block from the Theme tab also lacks the eight `tag-` roles, and `theme-fallbacks` gives each of them `muted-foreground`, so every label dot and every disc of a color picker is the same gray. So the author sets `tag-grey` to `tag-green` in both blocks too, each at 3:1 on `background`, `card`, `popover`, `muted`, `selected` and `sidebar`. A block from the Theme tab also lacks `diff-added` and `diff-removed`, and `theme-fallbacks` gives both `muted-foreground`. Then an added and a removed line take the same gray tint, and only the signs tell them apart. So the author sets both roles in both blocks, until `foreground` passes 4.5:1 on each tint (see `### diff`). `npm run contrast -- <file>` names each pair that fails, each chart name and each tag name that a block lacks, and each name that it resolved through `theme-fallbacks`.

**Tuning.** A theme passes every pair of `npm run contrast` in both modes before it is committed. Tune the lightness of a role. Lower the chroma only to keep a color inside sRGB, and never change a hue. In `graphite`, the light `primary` and `ring` are darker than the source indigo, because white text must pass 4.5:1 on the 80% hover of a primary button. Its `input`, `faint-foreground`, `destructive`, light `success`, `warning` and `info`, and its chart colors in both modes are tuned in the same way. Its dark `border` and `sidebar-border` are lighter than the source value, because that value is almost the dark `popover`, and a divider did not show on the pane over the list. At L 0.3, a divider is 0.117 above `background`, 0.084 above `card` and 0.046 above `popover` in OKLab L.

**A link in the text color.** A theme may set `link` to the same value as `foreground` in both blocks, for example a theme with no accent color. The underline then marks a link alone. That meets WCAG 2.2 success criterion 1.4.1 (Use of Color), because an underline is not color: failure F73 is a link in running text that only its color marks, and F73 also counts a mark that shows only on hover or on focus as missing. So a link in running text keeps its underline at rest in every theme, as `## Typography` says. The underline keeps the color of its text, which is the default of CSS, so a theme adds no underline color. The contrast check still reads `link` on `background` at 4.5:1 (see `## Colors`). With this value that pair is `foreground` on `background`, so it passes.

**The mode.** The `.dark` class on the `<html>` element selects dark mode, because `stack.css` defines the `dark` variant as `&:is(.dark *)`. The first mode follows the system setting (`prefers-color-scheme`). When a person picks a mode, the product keeps that choice, and each product decides where it stores the choice. Set `color-scheme: light` or `color-scheme: dark` on `<html>` together with the class, so that the scroll bars and the native controls of the browser follow the mode. Set both before the first paint, in a small script in `<head>`, so that the page never shows the wrong mode for a moment.

**The mode of mode-watcher.** The `sonner` component reads `mode.current` from mode-watcher, the package that it imports for the mode, and gives that mode to the `Toaster`. mode-watcher then writes `color-scheme` on `<html>` from its own stored choice, the key `mode-watcher-mode` in `localStorage`, which is `system` by default. It never reads `.dark`. So on a light system a page in dark mode gets `.dark` with `color-scheme: light` after each load, and its toasts draw light. On a dark system the reverse happens (measured in Chromium 148, mode-watcher 1.1.0). A product that uses the `sonner` component tells mode-watcher its mode with `setMode('dark')` or `setMode('light')` from `mode-watcher` whenever it sets `.dark`. It does this when the app starts, before the `Toaster` renders, and at each change of the mode. Do not mount `<ModeWatcher />`: it sets `.dark` from its own stored choice, and it writes `data-theme` from its key `mode-watcher-theme`, which is empty by default (read in the source of mode-watcher 1.1.0). Then `.dark`, `color-scheme` and `data-sonner-theme` of the `Toaster` agree after a reload and after a change (measured).

**The mode switch.** The switch between light and dark is an icon button. Its accessible name says the mode that it sets, such as "Switch to dark mode", and the name changes when the mode changes.

**The system mode.** A product that keeps a choice of mode also offers System, so that a person can go back to the setting of the device. A preferences page holds the choice as a segmented control of `### toggle-group` with System, Light and Dark, in this order, and the control always holds one of the three. A click on the item that is on clears the value of a Bits UI toggle group, so the control ignores an empty value in `onValueChange`. System removes the stored choice, and the page follows `prefers-color-scheme` again. It also follows a change of the device setting while the page is open: a `change` listener on `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` sets the class and `color-scheme` again. While a choice is stored, the user menu and the command palette also hold the action "Use the system mode". The icon button of the mode switch stays, for a quick change between light and dark.

**A theme switch in a product.** A product that lets the person switch the theme at run time imports no theme file. It serves the theme files of the pinned tag at one path, such as `/stack/vX.Y.Z/themes/`, and one `<link>` in `<head>` loads the file of the choice. The `<html>` element carries `data-theme="<name>"`, the file name of the theme without `.css`, such as `data-theme="graphite"`. The attribute is a part of this file, as a token name is, so every product uses this name and no other. No theme file and no rule of `stack.css` reads the attribute. The `<link>` can come before the CSS of the product, because no file of the imports sets a name that a theme file sets (checked for the 61 names of `themes/default.css`).

**The script of a theme switch.** The product keeps the choice, for example in `localStorage`. A small script in `<head>` reads the choice and checks it against the names of the theme files. Before the first paint, it loads the theme file and sets the attribute, so that the page never shows the wrong theme for a moment. The same script sets the mode (see "The mode" above). A `<link>` whose `href` a script changes does not hold the first paint: the page shows with no theme for 60 frames (measured in Chromium 148, with the file held back by 500 ms). A `<link>` that a script adds with `blocking="render"` holds the first paint (measured in Chromium 148). A `<link>` that `document.write` gives to the parser holds it too. The script uses `document.write` only where `blocking="render"` is not supported, because `document.write` fails under Trusted Types. "Pick a theme" in `README.md` gives the script.

Under a Content-Security-Policy, the script passes `script-src` by its SHA-256 hash, never by `'unsafe-inline'`. The script holds the list of themes and the path of the pinned tag, so the hash changes with each new tag. SvelteKit hashes only the scripts that it writes, so a SvelteKit product adds this hash to `kit.csp.directives['script-src']` itself. When the browser blocks the script, the page has no theme at all.

**A change of theme.** The switch keeps the choice, changes the `href` of the `<link>`, and sets the attribute in the `load` event of the `<link>`. The browser keeps the old file until the new file loads, so the values of the new file and the attribute change in the same frame (measured in Chromium 148). A switch that sets the attribute at once shows the attribute of the new theme with the values of the old theme for as long as the file loads.

**A value of the product per theme.** The product layer may key only its own `--product-` tokens on the attribute. It writes one block for each mode, after its `:root` and `.dark` blocks: `[data-theme="graphite"] { --product-paid: ... }` for light and `[data-theme="graphite"].dark { --product-paid: ... }` for dark. `[data-theme="graphite"]` alone has the specificity of `.dark` and comes after it, so its light value also holds in dark mode (measured). The product never redefines a shared token with the attribute.

## Charts

A chart draws in ink on paper. Each category takes its own pattern in `chart-ink`, on the ground under the chart. So a person tells the categories apart in print, in a grayscale screenshot and with no color vision. This pattern style is the default. The same patterns with their marks in `chart-1` to `chart-8` are the color pattern style, and solid fills of `chart-1` to `chart-8` are the color style. A product may take one of them as its default, and a person may pick one (see `### Styles`). The products draw charts with LayerChart on its SVG layer, in the chart parts of shadcn-svelte. `### chart` describes the component, and this section gives the rules that every chart follows.

### Categories

Category n of a chart takes the pattern `pattern-n` and the color `chart-n`, from 1 to 8, and the style of the chart decides which of the two show. The number comes from the fixed list of the categories of the chart, not from the order of the data. So a category keeps its pattern and its color when the data changes. A chart with more than eight categories shows the seven largest and puts the rest into an eighth category, Other.

### Styles

A chart style is two choices: the fill of each category, and the color of that fill. The fill of category n is its pattern, `pattern-n`, or solid. The color is `chart-ink` or `chart-n`, and it applies to every fill: to each pattern of `chart-patterns`, and to solid. So a chart has three styles:

| Style | Fill of category n | Color of the fill | Look |
|---|---|---|---|
| The pattern style, the default | `pattern-n` | `chart-ink` | Marks in ink, with the ground under the chart between them |
| The color pattern style | `pattern-n` | `chart-n` | Marks in `chart-n`, with the ground under the chart between them |
| The color style | Solid | `chart-n` | A solid fill of `chart-n`, with no pattern |

A solid fill in `chart-ink` is no style, because every category would look the same. The color pattern style and the color style are the two color styles. A heat map has no categories, so it has no patterns: its cells take the five steps of `### Heat map`, in `chart-ink` in the pattern style and in `chart-heat` in the two color styles. A line has no area to fill, so its stroke takes the place of a pattern. Line 1 is solid and line 2 is dotted in every style, in `chart-ink` in the pattern style and in `chart-n` in the two color styles (see `### Line chart`).

**Which style a chart takes.** Every chart takes the pattern style, unless its product takes another default or the person picks another style. A product takes one of the three styles as its default. It may take one of the two color styles when color helps a person to find one category across many charts, such as on an operations dashboard. A product may let each person pick a style in the preferences of the person. The pick applies to every chart that the person sees, so one person sees one style, and a category looks the same on every screen of that person. A printed chart, a PDF export and a chart in a document always take the pattern style, whatever the style on the screen, and they draw the marks of each pattern as shapes (see `## Print`).

**The pattern style.** The fill of a shape is the pattern of its category: marks in `chart-ink`, with the ground under the chart between them. In light mode the ink is dark on a light ground, and in dark mode the ink is light on a dark ground. A shape has no color fill and no ink edge, except the cell of a heat map. A 1 px edge in the ground keeps two shapes that meet apart (see `### Edges`).

**The color pattern style.** The fill of a shape is the pattern of its category, as in the pattern style, with its marks in `chart-n` in place of `chart-ink`. The pattern tells the categories apart, so the chart still reads in a grayscale screenshot and with no color vision. The color helps a person to find one category across many charts. Each mark is `chart-n` at full strength on the ground under the chart, so the pair of the color style covers it: `chart-n` on `background`, `card` and `popover` at 3:1 (see `### Chart colors`). The marks are the only outline of a shape, so WCAG 1.4.11 asks for 3:1 on each mark, and that pair has a small margin. A thin mark loses that margin. Chromium mixes the edge pixels of a mark with the ground. It also resamples the whole tile when the tile turns, when its side is not a whole number of device pixels, or when the chart starts inside a device pixel. So the color pattern style draws thicker marks than the pattern style (see `### Patterns`). Each line is 2 px wide with `shape-rendering="crispEdges"`, and each dot or square is at least 3 px wide. Measured in Chromium on 2026-10-24 at 1x, 1.25x, 1.5x and 2x, on `background`, `card` and `popover` in both modes of both themes. The chart started 0, 0.3, 0.5, 1, 2 and 3 CSS px from the origin. With the thin marks, some marks of patterns 1, 3, 5, 6 and 7 had no pixel at 3:1. The weakest was 1.70:1, a line of pattern 7 at 1.25x on a pair of 3.08:1. With the thicker marks, the strongest pixel of each mark is within 0.02 of its pair. The pattern style keeps the thin marks, because `chart-ink` passes 15:1 or more: its weakest mark is 3.25:1, a line of pattern 7 at 1.25x in graphite light. The labels on the chart, the edges, the legend and the tooltip are those of the pattern style. The tile of a legend item and of a tooltip row is the 12 px square with the pattern of its category, its marks in `chart-n`, and no edge.

**The color style.** Each category is a solid fill of `chart-n`, with no pattern.

**What the color style needs.** A chart that tells its categories apart by color alone fails WCAG 1.4.1 (Use of Color). So the color style keeps the name of each category in text: on the chart where the label fits, else in the legend, and always in the tooltip and in the data table. Each fill passes 3:1 on the ground under the chart (see `### Chart colors`), so that a person sees each shape. A 1 px edge in that ground keeps two fills that meet apart. The tile of a legend item and of a tooltip row is a 12 px square of `chart-n` with no edge.

### Patterns

`chart-patterns` holds one pattern for each category, with five fields: `type`, `angle`, `width`, `pitch` and `stagger`. The unit is the user unit of the SVG, which is 1 px at 1x.

- `type` is `lines`, `cross`, `dots` or `squares`. `cross` adds a second line at the angle plus 90 degrees.
- `pitch` is the distance from the center of one mark to the center of the next. The tile is a square with the side `pitch`.
- `width` is the thickness of a line, the diameter of a dot, or the side of a square.
- `angle` is in degrees. Angle 0 draws vertical lines. The angle turns the whole tiling clockwise through `patternTransform="rotate(<angle>)"`, so 45 leans like `/`, 90 draws horizontal lines and 135 leans like `\`. Dots and squares take angle 0.
- `stagger` makes the tile twice as high, for a second row that moves by half a pitch along x. The mark of the second row sits at both vertical edges of the tile, because a tile clips its content at its edges. A staggered pattern takes an even pitch. Half of an even pitch is a whole unit, so the marks of the second row fall on whole pixels at 1x, as the marks of the first row do.

Two patterns of dots, or two patterns of squares, differ in the width and the pitch of their marks, and not only in the stagger. Pattern 6 has smaller dots than pattern 2, further apart, and pattern 8 has larger squares than pattern 4, further apart. So a person tells them apart at 1x and in the 12 px tile. In the color pattern style the dots of pattern 6 are as wide as the dots of pattern 2. There the pitch and the stagger tell the two patterns apart.

**Marks in the color pattern style.** The color pattern style draws each line 2 px wide, with `shape-rendering="crispEdges"`, and each dot or square at least 3 px wide. The type, the angle, the pitch and the stagger stay. So patterns 1, 3, 5 and 7 have lines of 2 px, and pattern 6 has dots of 3 px. A mark of `chart-n` has a small margin over its pair of 3:1, and a thinner mark falls below it (see `### Styles`). The pattern style draws the widths of `chart-patterns`, with no `crispEdges`. `crispEdges` alone does not keep a mark at its value. It has no effect on a turned tile at 1x. It changes the 3 px dot of pattern 2 into the 3 px square of pattern 4. On a 1 px line at 1.25x it lowered the line to 1.69:1 (measured in Chromium on 2026-10-24).

**The hatch of a meter.** The pending segment of a meter (see `### progress`) takes pattern 1 in `muted-foreground`, with the marks of the color pattern style. These are lines at 45 degrees, 2 px wide, with `shape-rendering="crispEdges"`, at a pitch of 4 px. The tile has no ground, so the track shows between the marks. `muted-foreground` passes 5.41:1 or more on `background`, the ground of the track, in both modes of both themes. A 1 px line loses most of that ratio in the 4 px track: its weakest mark was 2.52:1, in default light at 1.25x. With the 2 px line, the strongest pixel of each mark is at the ratio of its pair at 1x, 1.25x, 1.5x and 2x, in both modes of both themes (measured in Chromium on 2026-10-25). The meter writes its own `<pattern>`, with an id from `$props.id()`, as a chart does.

**The hatch of a meter in print.** The listener of `### A screen page in print` draws the hatch as shapes, as it draws a chart pattern. The `<pattern>` of the meter names pattern 1 in `data-pattern`, `color-pattern` in `data-pattern-style` and `var(--muted-foreground)` in `data-pattern-ink`. So the listener draws the 2 px lines of the color pattern style in `muted-foreground`, as the screen shows them, and not the 1 px lines in `chart-ink` of a printed chart. Only the hatch of a meter takes the two attributes. Without them, the four meters with a pending segment in the preview printed four tiling patterns with images of 72 dpi. The strongest pixel of the hatch was gray 105 in one layout and gray 117, 4.61:1 on paper, in another, and it changed from meter to meter when the page moved by 1 px (measured). At most 23.1% of a segment was at gray 110 or darker. With them, the PDF held no pattern and no image. In each meter the strongest pixel was gray 106, the `muted-foreground` of default light, at 5.41:1. From 43.0% to 44.8% of the segment was at gray 110 or darker (measured at 300 dpi on 2026-10-25).

**The center of a mark.** The center of a line, a dot or a square is at `floor(pitch / 2) + (width mod 2) / 2` on each axis. A mark of odd width then sits on a half unit, and a mark of even width on a whole unit. So its edges fall on whole pixels at 1x. With pitch 4 and width 1 the center is 2.5, and the line covers one pixel. A line of width 1 on the whole unit 2 covers two pixels at half strength, and the pattern loses its contrast.

**Draw the tile by hand.** Each tile is an SVG `<pattern>` with `patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse"`. It holds the marks in `chart-ink`, or in `chart-n` in the color pattern style, and no ground of its own, so the ground under the chart shows between the marks. A line runs from edge to edge of the tile, so that it joins the line of the next tile. Never use the `lines` option of the LayerChart `Pattern`. Its angle 0 draws horizontal lines. It draws one fixed diagonal for each angle from 1 to 89 degrees, and the other diagonal from 91 to 179. Patterns 1, 4 and 6 of a chart with the prefix `p1`:

```svelte
<svg width="0" height="0" class="absolute" aria-hidden="true">
  <defs>
    <pattern id="p1-pattern-1" data-pattern="1" width="4" height="4" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" patternTransform="rotate(45)">
      <line x1="2.5" y1="0" x2="2.5" y2="4" stroke="var(--chart-ink, var(--foreground))" stroke-width="1" />
    </pattern>
    <pattern id="p1-pattern-4" data-pattern="4" width="5" height="5" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse">
      <rect x="1" y="1" width="3" height="3" fill="var(--chart-ink, var(--foreground))" />
    </pattern>
    <pattern id="p1-pattern-6" data-pattern="6" width="6" height="12" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse">
      <circle cx="3" cy="3" r="1" fill="var(--chart-ink, var(--foreground))" />
      <circle cx="6" cy="9" r="1" fill="var(--chart-ink, var(--foreground))" />
      <circle cx="0" cy="9" r="1" fill="var(--chart-ink, var(--foreground))" />
    </pattern>
  </defs>
</svg>
```

The same patterns 1 and 6 in the color pattern style, with a 2 px line and 3 px dots:

```svelte
<pattern id="p1-pattern-1" data-pattern="1" width="4" height="4" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" patternTransform="rotate(45)">
  <line x1="2" y1="0" x2="2" y2="4" stroke="var(--chart-1)" stroke-width="2" shape-rendering="crispEdges" />
</pattern>
<pattern id="p1-pattern-6" data-pattern="6" width="6" height="12" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse">
  <circle cx="3.5" cy="3.5" r="1.5" fill="var(--chart-6, var(--chart-1))" />
  <circle cx="6.5" cy="9.5" r="1.5" fill="var(--chart-6, var(--chart-1))" />
  <circle cx="0.5" cy="9.5" r="1.5" fill="var(--chart-6, var(--chart-1))" />
</pattern>
```

**The ids.** Each chart writes its own eight `<pattern>` elements, with the ids `<prefix>-pattern-1` to `<prefix>-pattern-8`. The prefix comes from `$props.id()` of the chart component, such as `p1`. So two charts on one page, or a chart in a dialog, never share an id, and a fill never points into another chart. The patterns sit in an `<svg>` of 0 by 0 px with `class="absolute"`, which takes no space. Never hide that svg with `display: none`, because a pattern in such an svg paints nothing. A chart in the color style writes no patterns. `@stack-kit/stack/parts/StackPatternDefs` writes the eight patterns of a chart for its prefix and its style, each with its number in `data-pattern`.

**In print.** Chromium prints a `<pattern>` fill as an image of 72 dpi, so a line of 1 px prints gray (see `### Charts in print`). So a page that prints draws the marks of each pattern as shapes, in a listener of `beforeprint` (see `### A screen page in print`). Each `<pattern>` of a chart names its number in `data-pattern`, as in the examples above. The listener draws the marks of that pattern in the pattern style, because a printed chart takes the pattern style (see **Which style a chart takes.**). The hatch of a meter keeps its own marks and its own ink in print (see **The hatch of a meter in print.**).

**The ready file.** Content with no CSS layer of STACK takes the patterns from the file `chart-patterns.svg`. Such content is a chart that a server draws as plain SVG for a screen, or a picture that holds a chart. A chart in a PDF draws its marks as shapes instead, because Chromium writes a pattern fill into a PDF as an image of 72 dpi (see `### Charts in print`). `npm run theme` writes the file from `chart-patterns`, `npm run theme -- --check` fails when the two differ, and the package ships it. The file holds the pattern style only: the widths of `chart-patterns`, with no `crispEdges`. A chart in the color pattern style writes its own patterns, as above.

- Put the content of the file once into the document that holds the chart, such as the first child of `<body>`. A picture that shows as an image is a document of its own, so copy the `<pattern>` elements into the `<defs>` of the picture. A fill finds a pattern by its id in its own document only.
- The ids are `stack-pattern-1` to `stack-pattern-8`, so a shape of category n takes `fill="url(#stack-pattern-n)"`. Every chart of the document shares the one set. The ids of a chart component start with the prefix of the chart, such as `p1-pattern-1`, so the two sets never meet.
- Each mark takes `currentColor`. The marks take the color of the svg of the file, which inherits the color of the element around it, and never the color of the shape that they fill (measured in Chromium). So the marks take the text color of the page, as `chart-ink` falls back to `foreground`, and the `ink` of a printed document. For another ink, set `color` on the svg of the file, such as the hex value of `chart-ink` of the theme.
- Never hide the svg of the file with `display: none`.

The patterns of the file and the patterns of the pattern style in the preview drew the same pixels in Chromium at 1x and 2x, in both modes (measured on 2026-10-25).

### Chart colors

`chart-ink` draws the marks and the lines of the pattern style, the leader lines of a donut in every style, and the steps and the edge of a heat map in the pattern style. `chart-1` to `chart-8` are the marks of the color pattern style, the fills of the color style, and the lines of the two color styles. The comparison line and a reference line take `muted-foreground`, and the disc of an annotation takes `foreground`, in every style, because none of them is a category. `chart-heat` is the hue of a heat map in the two color styles (see `### Heat map`). A block from the Theme tab lacks `chart-6` to `chart-8`, `chart-ink` and `chart-heat`, so each paint carries the fallback of `theme-fallbacks`, such as `var(--chart-ink, var(--foreground))`. The colors come from the theme in each mode, so a chart follows a change of `.dark` with no chart code.

`chart-ink` and each `chart-n` pass 3:1 on each ground that a chart sits on, `background`, `card` and `popover`, in both modes of every theme. `chart-ink` also passes 3:1 at 50%, the lightest step of a heat map. The text of a chart, in `foreground` and `muted-foreground`, passes 4.5:1 on the same grounds, so the same pairs cover the comparison line, a reference line and the disc of an annotation at 3:1. `npm run contrast` checks these pairs. A light fill fails 3:1 on a light ground, and a dark fill fails on a dark ground, so a chart color has a lightness near the middle (see `## Themes`). The measured ratios on the lowest of the three grounds, from `npm run contrast` on 2026-10-13:

| Color on `background`, `card` and `popover` | default light | default dark | graphite light | graphite dark |
|---|---|---|---|---|
